# From Σ to physical spacetime: the scientific contract (M0)

**Purpose.** The author's programme "From `Σ` to physical spacetime" (milestones M0–M9, gates A, B
and C; BACKLOG #349 onwards) asks for one ontic arena `Σ_*` — a measure, a symplectic form and a
Hamiltonian flow — in which the same dynamics writes records, gives outcome statistics, carries
clocks and signals, and supports an operational reconstruction of Lorentzian geometry with explicit
errors. This note is milestone M0. It fixes, before any further Lean is written, what the programme
**assumes**, what the corpus has **derived**, and what is **owed**, so that no later theorem can turn
a premise into a result by being stated carefully.

**Baseline.** Commit `d06f8bd7` (M1A landed: `RecordLayer/DiracPointerHamiltonian.lean`, BACKLOG
~~#349~~). Sources audited: [`POSITS.md`](POSITS.md), [`records-to-spacetime-status.md`](records-to-spacetime-status.md),
[`beyond-1p1-scoping.md`](beyond-1p1-scoping.md), [`residues.tsv`](residues.tsv). What the audit found
in them is §7.

**How to read the three registers.**

* **Register 1 (`UA-n`)** — assumed physical structure. An entry here is an input. Nothing in it is a
  result, however natural it looks, and each entry says what would discharge or constrain it.
* **Register 2 (`UD-n`)** — derived properties: Lean theorems, by name and module, each marked
  *exact* or *bound*, and marked with the **arena it is proved on**. A theorem proved on another
  arena is infrastructure for the programme; it is not a property of `Σ_*`.
* **Register 3 (`UO-n`)** — obligations whose truth the assumptions do not guarantee. Each carries a
  **repackaging test**: the condition under which a theorem with that title would only restate an
  entry of Register 1.

The identifiers `UA-`, `UD-`, `UO-` are local to this note. Posit numbers are those of
[`POSITS.md`](POSITS.md) and residue identifiers those of [`residues.tsv`](residues.tsv); neither is
renumbered or extended here.

---

## 1. The two distinctions the programme turns on

1. **Operational reconstruction is not derivation.** The carrier is the lattice Dirac chain, chosen
   because it has a relativistic continuum limit (UA-8). Recovering Lorentz coordinates from records
   of that carrier shows that observers inside the model reconstruct the geometry the model was
   chosen to have, with stated errors. It does not derive relativity from weaker principles. Which
   assumptions *force* the geometry is milestone M8 (gate C), and nothing before M8 may be worded as
   if it answered that.
2. **One arena, not a collection of witnesses.** The corpus proves records, single clicks, clocks,
   cone bounds and radar kinematics on several different arenas (§4.2). The programme's claims are
   about one shared arena. A result on a separate arena counts toward the programme only after a
   theorem identifies its dynamics with the flow of `Σ_*`, or re-proves it there.

A third rule follows from the corpus's own discipline and is applied throughout: **a property that
Lean proves about a definition is a property of the definition.** It becomes a property of the
dynamics only when the object it is about is produced by the flow.

---

## 2. The shared arena as it stands

`Σ_* = ℂℙ^{2M+1} × T²`, in Lean `KSigma (2 * M + 1 + 1)` with the form `arenaForm`
(`arenaForm_isSymplectic`, `LF4/ArenaSymplectic.lean`) and the measure `kMuL` (the Fubini–Study
measure on the base times Haar measure on the fibre, `LF4/KahlerInstance.lean`).

The joint energy is `diracPointerEnergy`, an instance of `coupledEnergy`
(`LF4/ArenaCoupledFlow.lean`):

  `𝓗(p, θ) = −2 ⟨H_D⟩_p − 2 g f(θ₂) ⟨P_j⟩_p`,

with `H_D = diracHam J μ` the one-particle Dirac ring of `M + 1` sites, `P_j = siteProj j` the
projector on site `j`, `g` a coupling constant and `f` a smooth periodic profile. Its Hamiltonian
flow `diracPointerFlow` is global and has a closed form (`diracPointerFlow_apply`): `θ₂` is constant,
the carrier is moved by the unitary `diracCarrierU` generated by `H_D + g f(θ₂) P_j`, and `θ₁` is
translated by `diracPointerShift`, whose rate is `−2 g f'(θ₂) ⟨P_j⟩`.

What this arena does **not** contain: a readout region, a ready state, a threshold, an
`ActualOccurrence`, a clock, a second observer, a second detector, an outcome probability, a causal
relation, a coordinate. The moving angle `θ₁` does not act back on the carrier: `𝓗` is independent of
`θ₁`, so the carrier sees the static potential `g f(θ₂) P_j`.

**The clock extension (BACKLOG ~~#357~~).** `ℂℙ^{4M+3} × T²`: the same kind of arena, form and
measure, with the carrier's index doubled by a two-level internal mass. The carrier is the device
`deviceHam J μ₀ μ₁` and the joint energy is `clockPointerEnergy`,

  `𝓗(p, θ) = −2 ⟨H_dev⟩_p − 2 g f(θ₂) ⟨X⟩_p`,

with `X = levelSwap` the exchange of the two levels at every site. Its Hamiltonian flow is
`clockPointerFlow` (`clockPointerFlow_apply`). This is not `Σ_*`. The pointer of `Σ_*` is coupled
to a site projector and this one to the level swap, and no arena yet carries both.
Since ~~#358~~ the clock extension has a readout (`tickReady`, `tickDisplay`, `tickShown`), and each
tick of its pointer is an occurrence of `clockPointerFlow` (`clockTickRecord`). It has no register
for the count of ticks.

**The two-pointer arenas (BACKLOG ~~#359~~).** `EchoSigma`: a projective base times two pointer
tori, with the product form `echoForm` (`echoForm_isSymplectic`) and the measure `echoMuL`, which is
`μ_FS` times Haar on each torus. The arena, the form and the closed-form flow of an energy with two
product terms are BACKLOG ~~#202~~'s (`hamiltonianFlow_twoCoupledEnergy`). This note adopts them
since ~~#359~~. Two joint energies are on it.

1. `diracTwoPointerEnergy` on `ℂℙ^{2M+1} × T² × T²`, two site detectors on the Dirac ring:

     `𝓗(p, θ, ψ) = −2 ⟨H_D⟩_p − 2 g₁ f(θ₂) ⟨P_j⟩_p − 2 g₂ h(ψ₂) ⟨P_k⟩_p`.

   Its flow is `diracTwoPointerFlow` (`diracTwoPointerFlow_apply`).
2. `clockSiteEnergy` on `ℂℙ^{4M+3} × T² × T²`, the device with the clock's pointer and a site
   detector:

     `𝓗(p, θ, ψ) = −2 ⟨H_dev⟩_p − 2 g₁ f(θ₂) ⟨X⟩_p − 2 g₂ h(ψ₂) ⟨Q_k⟩_p`,

   with `Q_k = deviceSiteProj k` the site projector on both levels. Its flow is `clockSiteFlow`
   (`clockSiteFlow_apply`).

Neither is `Σ_*`. On the fibres where one profile vanishes each restricts exactly to the one-pointer
flow of the other pointer (UD-70, UD-71). Since BACKLOG ~~#360~~ the second has a readout and a
ready arc on each torus (`echoTickShown`, `echoTickReady`, `echoSiteShown`, `echoSiteReady`; UA-4),
and a tick record and a position record are read off its flow in one run (UD-74 to UD-76). The first
had no readout, ready state or record until BACKLOG ~~#364~~, which puts a readout and a ready arc
on each of its tori (`echoFstShown`, `echoFstReady`, `echoSiteShown`, `echoSiteReady`; UA-4) and
reads one write in a run with two detectors (UD-86 to UD-88). The assumptions they add are UA-13.
Since BACKLOG ~~#361~~
the generators of both hop at most one site of the ring, as a property of the matrices (UD-77), and
the response of a site pointer's displacement to an intervention made elsewhere on the ring is
bounded above by a factorial tail in the distance (UD-79 to UD-81): for a kick and for the other
pointer's angle on the arena of two site pointers, for a kick only on the arena of the clock and a
site detector, and not for the clock's pointer. The interventions are those of UA-14.

---

## 3. Register 1 — assumed physical structure

Each entry: what is assumed, where it enters the Lean, its standing (posit, boundary, choice), and
what would discharge or constrain it.

### UA-1 — the arena

* **Assumed.** `Σ_*` is a projective base times a two-torus fibre with the product symplectic form.
  The fibre has one moving angle `θ₁` and its partner `θ₂`.
* **Where.** `KSigma`, `arenaForm`, `arenaForm_isSymplectic`.
* **Standing.** Choice. `Σ` is the floor of the account: it is not derived and deriving it is not an
  obligation of this programme. What the programme may do is *constrain* it — show which features of
  the arena the records, statistics and geometry require (M8).
* **Linked posit.** Posit 10 (which fibre coordinates carry record content). The author's decision is
  I3 (both); the corpus computes with I1 (`torusCell`, `globalBasin` read `θ₁` only); I3 has a rate
  field from the base since BACKLOG ~~#136~~ (`jointMomentContext`,
  `skewBasin_prob_jointMomentContext`), with its degeneracy map a posit. On `Σ_*` as built the flow
  moves `θ₁` and conserves `θ₂`, which sets the coupling: the only coordinate this dynamics writes
  is `θ₁`. An M2 or M3 statement must say which coordinates its regions read.
* **Discharge.** None sought for the arena. For the record dimension, Posit 10's line: derive from
  the interaction which coordinates are written.

### UA-2 — the measure

* **Assumed.** The typicality measure is `μ_FS` on the base (Posit 2, residue R-012) and Haar on the
  fibre, in product form (Posit 9): a preparation `ψ` is the measure `δ_[ψ] ⊗ Haar`
  (`epistemicMeasure`, `RecordLayer/GlobalBasin.lean`).
* **What backs it short of derivation.** `fsMeasure_unique`: `μ_FS` is the only `U(N)`-invariant
  probability measure on the base. `epistemicMeasure_eq_disintegration` and `csd_equivariance`: the
  product form is the disintegration of the ontic measure and is carried by the unitary flow.
* **What it is not.** It is not selected by the dynamics. `flow_admits_invariant_ne_fubiniStudy`: a
  projective unitary flow fixing two distinct rays has an invariant probability measure other than
  `μ_FS`. Invariance of `kMuL` under `diracPointerFlow` (UD-5) is necessary for the measure to be
  usable and says nothing about why it is this one. Expected, not checked: the carrier generator at
  fixed `θ₂` is Hermitian (`diracPointerH_isHermitian`), hence has two distinct eigenrays, so the
  hypothesis of that theorem holds on `Σ_*`.
* **Fibre law.** `θ₂` is conserved by the flow (`diracPointerFlow_snd_snd`). No law for `θ₂` is
  produced by this dynamics: whatever distribution a theorem gives `θ₂` is preparation input (UA-4).
  Relaxation of a fibre distribution is residue R-019. The corpus has it on a hyperbolic witness
  only (`relaxation_requires_hyperbolic_fibre`), and translation flows admit no decay envelope
  (`not_hasCorrelationDecay_kFlow`). The fibre action on `Σ_*` is a translation of `θ₁` at a rate
  depending on `θ₂` and the carrier, not a hyperbolic map, so those relaxation theorems do not
  apply to it. Whether that shear relaxes the `θ₁` marginal has not been examined.
  Since BACKLOG ~~#354~~ one fact is known: the uniform law of `θ₁` is stationary under the flow,
  for every law of `θ₂` and every carrier (UD-45, UD-46). Relaxation of a non-uniform law is still
  unexamined.
* **Discharge.** Posit 2's and Posit 9's lines in `POSITS.md`.

### UA-3 — the typicality interpretation

* **Assumed.** Probability *is* the volume ratio of the ontic measure (Posit 4). Every quantity the
  programme will call `P_{Σ_*}(i | ψ, M)` is the UA-2 measure of a region, read through this entry.
* **What backs it.** `born_frequency_convergence_N` is a coherence check (long-run frequencies under
  the product measure converge to the volume ratio). Nothing discharges the reading itself.
* **Discharge.** None known; `POSITS.md` Posit 4 records that.

### UA-4 — apparatus preparation

* **Assumed.**
  1. *The ready state.* The pointer starts at a ready value and is renewed for each measurement
     (Posit 5: `calibratedBank`, one measurement, one bank).
  2. *The apparatus parameters.* The detector's site `j`, the profile `f`, the coupling `g`, and (from
     M2) the readout region and threshold. They are the measurement context `M`. Since BACKLOG
     ~~#352~~ the regions are `diracReady` (pointer angle in `(0, 1/6]`) and `diracDisplayed`
     (pointer angle in `(1/3, 5/6]`), and the readout time `T` is a parameter of the same kind.
     Since BACKLOG ~~#358~~ the clock extension has the same two arcs (`tickReady`, `tickDisplay`).
     The pointer's start in the ready arc and the device's prepared phase, a quarter turn before the
     pointer's rest, are parameters of the same kind.
     Since BACKLOG ~~#360~~ the arena of the clock's pointer and a site detector (UA-13) has the
     same two arcs on each torus (`echoTickReady`, `echoTickDisplay`, `echoSiteReady`,
     `echoSiteDisplay`), placed by hand. The second profile `h`, the second coupling `g₂`, the
     detector's site `k` and the site pointer's start in its ready arc are parameters of the same
     kind.
     Since BACKLOG ~~#364~~ the arena of two site detectors has the same two arcs on each torus
     (`echoFstReady`, `echoFstDisplay`, `echoSiteReady`, `echoSiteDisplay`), placed by hand. Both
     pointers start in their ready arcs.
     Since BACKLOG ~~#369~~ the third torus of the arena of UA-15 has the same two arcs
     (`triThdReady`, `triThdDisplay`). Since BACKLOG ~~#381~~ the fourth torus of UA-17 (e) has
     a readout too (`quadFreeReady`, `quadFreeDisplay`), with the start `1/12` on the fibre
     `ξ₂ = 0` and the profile `trainF`, as apparatus input. In the variant UA-17 (f) (BACKLOG
     ~~#384~~) the third pointer starts anywhere in its ready arc on a fibre where its profile
     vanishes, and its rate `κ₃` and the flow time `T₁` are apparatus input. The third profile, the third strength and the second
     detector's site are parameters of the same kind.
     Since BACKLOG ~~#386~~ the first region pointer's ready arc `echoFstReady` is read at the
     flow time `roundBack`, after the start, and its first rate window is tightened to
     `κ₁ · roundUpper ≤ 1/6 − a_θ`; a readout and a window of the same kind.
  3. *The law of the conserved angle.* The distribution of `θ₂` over trials.
  4. *Measurement independence* (Posit 8): the choice of `M` is not correlated with the ontic point.
     Settings are not represented in `Σ`, and nothing backs this entry.
* **What constrains it.** `collapse_accuracy_bound`: approximate collapse is paid for in the measure
  of the ready region, and exact collapse forces a null ready set. `swap_not_blockLuders`: no fixed
  ray-level calibration serves degenerate measurements. `no_joint_hilbert_map`: no map on a joint
  Hilbert space reproduces a joint lift (the record-layer scope of Wigner–Araki–Yanase).
* **A tension to keep visible.** A ready pointer is a concentrated law for `θ₁`; Posit 9's fibre
  factor is uniform. Both are in the corpus, in different modules. A statement on `Σ_*` must say
  which fibre law it uses for `θ₁` and which for `θ₂`, and may not switch between a proof's steps.
  UD-45 to UD-48 use the uniform law for `θ₁` throughout. UD-49 conditions that same law on the
  ready region at time `0`, as an event; it does not replace the law.
  UD-50 to UD-54 do as UD-49 does.
  UD-55 to UD-57 use the uniform law for `θ₁` throughout, as UD-48 does.
  UD-58 to UD-60 use no fibre law. They hold at each point, and the measure enters only as
  invariance under the flow.
  UD-61 to UD-64 use no fibre law. UD-65 uses `kMuL` as a finite measure the flow preserves, and
  states no probability of a tick.
  UD-72 to UD-76 use no fibre law. They hold at each point. The instance of UD-76 is on the fibres
  where both profiles vanish, a null set of `echoMuL`, and it states no probability.
* **Discharge.** Posit 5's line (the same `H_int` family as Posit 1). For item 3, a dynamics that
  moves `θ₂`, which the `coupledEnergy` form excludes.

### UA-5 — the microscopic coupling

* **Assumed.** The interaction is the product term `−2 g f(θ₂) ⟨P_j⟩`: a base observable diagonal in
  the site basis times a function of the fibre. Its form, its locality (one site), its strength and
  its profile are chosen.
* **Standing.** Boundary R-015 (which `H_int` a given apparatus realises is a modelling input) for the
  choice. Residue R-016 concerns the arena-level generation of the shear and joint-lift propagators
  and not this one: `diracPointerFlow` is defined as the Hamiltonian flow of the energy, and
  `hamiltonianFlow_coupledEnergy` computes it.
* **What the coupling gives and does not give.** It gives a deterministic drift of the pointer
  proportional to the site occupancy (UD-4, UD-7). It gives no rate field, no outcome cell and no
  probability. The corpus's rate field is Posit 1 (UA-6).
  Since BACKLOG ~~#355~~ the last clause is sharper. With the readout of UA-4 the coupling does fix
  a probability, that of the click event (UD-50). It is not the expectation of an operator (UD-53),
  and it is still no rate field.
  Since BACKLOG ~~#365~~ one arena couples the pointer to the projector on a region of sites, half
  the ring, in place of one site: `−2 g f(θ₂) ⟨P_A⟩` (`regionProj`, `regionPointerFlow`). The form
  is the same and the locality is a region. That too is chosen.
* **Discharge.** None for the choice (a boundary). M8 compares alternatives.

### UA-6 — the cell law (Posit 1)

* **Assumed, wherever outcome cells sized by rates are used.** The rates a context assigns generate
  the phase rotations of its pointer torus: `IsTorusGenerated c`
  (`RecordLayer/CellLawForced.lean`). The corpus's field `momentContext` is a definition.
* **Standing.** Posit. §6 states exactly why it stays one.
* **Extended, since BACKLOG ~~#371~~.** A site's outcome cell is the union of the two basins of
  the (site, component) context (`siteCell`). The fine basis within a site and the order of the
  arcs are apparatus input.
* **Discharge.** Derive `IsTorusGenerated` from the dynamics that writes the record. Not done.
  Since BACKLOG ~~#355~~: the write of `Σ_*` does not supply it. The click probability that write
  produces is not the expectation of any operator (UD-53), so it is not the rate of the moment map.
  The discharge needs a pointer dynamics other than the one of UA-5.

### UA-7 — the external flow parameter

* **Assumed.** The parameter `t` of `diracPointerFlow` is the ontic time. It is not an observable of
  the model and no record reads it.
* **Where it already does work.** Every existing write window, retention window, clock reading and
  radar time in the corpus is stated in `t`. The author's decision on ~~#290~~ — the ontic time order
  decides which wing of an entangled pair is selected first — makes `t` physically significant for
  individual outcomes (`spacelike_boost_changes_outcomes`), never for statistics.
* **Discharge.** Not sought (M4 allows `t` to remain fundamental). What M4 owes is that *operational*
  time is a function of clock records only (UO-14).
* **Since BACKLOG ~~#357~~.** Every time in UD-58 to UD-60 is this parameter. The turning points
  and the period of the clock's pointer are flow times. No theorem recovers a time from records.
* **Since BACKLOG ~~#358~~.** `clockOpTime` is a function of the number of tick records and of the
  two rest energies, and the count is unchanged by an increasing relabelling of `t`
  (`isTickCount_comp_orderIso`). That is the sense in which UD-61 to UD-64 recover a time from
  records. The parameter still does three things there: it locates each tick record (start, write
  interval, window), it gives the horizon the count is taken at, and it is the quantity operational
  time is compared with.

### UA-8 — the microscopic propagation law

* **Assumed.** The carrier is one particle on a finite ring with the lattice Dirac Hamiltonian
  `diracHam J μ` (`CV/DiracClockChain.lean`): nearest-neighbour (`diracHam_apply_eq_zero`), Hermitian
  (`diracHam_isHermitian`), `ħ = 1`. Its evolution is unitary because the base energy is the
  expectation `−2 ⟨H⟩` — the unitary class, Posit 6 (`projectedFlow_eq_unitary_family` takes it as a
  hypothesis; `projectedFlow_unitary_of_flow_continuous` reduces it to continuity).
* **Relativity is input.** The Dirac chain is chosen with a relativistic continuum limit
  (`dirac_massless_limit`, `dirac_packet_limit`). Lorentz kinematics recovered from its records is
  therefore the first distinction of §1, not a derivation.
* **Discharge.** Posit 6's line for unitarity. For the choice of carrier, M8.

### UA-9 — composite structure and locality

* **Assumed.** Local tomography for composites (Posit 7, boundary R-017:
  `compositeAlgReconstruction`, `recordLocallyTomographic_iff_adjoin_eq_top`); locality of
  de-isolation is built in (`localDeisolation_factorises` holds by construction).
* **Where it will enter.** M3's no-signalling and sequential-update checks, and M5's interaction
  topology. `Σ_*` as built is a one-particle arena with one detector; no composite is on it.
  The two-pointer arenas of UA-13 have two detectors and still one particle. No composite carrier is
  on them. Since BACKLOG ~~#376~~ a composite carrier is on the arena of UA-17, taken as the
  tensor product by definition.

### UA-10 — stated detector laws and input weights on the existing arenas

* **Assumed in the results of §4.2.** The click law's rate `κ` and its arcs (~~#266~~), the detector
  gain (~~#252~~), and the use of the packet's site weights to size the arcs: "QM's weights are input,
  never constructed" (`records-to-spacetime-status.md`). Only ~~#251~~'s detectors are generated by a
  coupling.
* **Consequence.** `dirac_packet_one_click` is a theorem about a stated click law. Citing it on `Σ_*`
  without generating that law from `diracPointerFlow` would import the weights.

### UA-11 — kinematic idealisations in the radar results

* **Assumed in `radar_lorentz_exact`, `radar_lorentz_scaling` and the `d + 1` chart
  (`trilateration_chart_boostD`).** The messenger travels at exactly `1`; a moving clock reads
  exactly `√(1 − v²)` times rest time; the second observer's messenger is isotropic; clocks are
  calibrated and synchronised by hypothesis (`RecordLayer/RecordOccurrence.lean`). The chain's
  deviations enter as reading errors supplied to the statement, and the family along which every
  error shrinks is not instantiated on an arena.

### UA-12 — the clock's level and its coupling

* **Assumed.** The carrier of the clock extension (§2) is the device of `CV/DiracDevice.lean`: the
  ring of UA-8 with a two-level internal mass, `deviceHam J μ₀ μ₁`. The pointer is coupled to it by
  the product term `−2 g f(θ₂) ⟨X⟩`, with `X = levelSwap` the exchange of the two levels at every
  site. The observable, its support on every site, the strength and the profile are chosen.
* **Where.** `clockPointerEnergy`, `clockPointerEnergy_eq` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedDiracClock.lean`,
  BACKLOG ~~#357~~).
* **Standing.** Boundary R-015, as UA-5: which coupling an apparatus realises is a modelling input.
  The two levels and their splitting are the clock's definition, as in UD-14.
* **What the coupling gives and does not give.** It gives a drift of the pointer proportional to the
  real quadrature of the coherence between the levels (UD-58, UD-59). The quadrature is not
  rectified, so the pointer swings and returns. It gives no count, no threshold event and no record.
  A pointer coupled to the identity instead advances uniformly in `t` (`idxPointerShift_one`). That
  pointer reads UA-7 and no clock, and it is the case to exclude.
* **Since BACKLOG ~~#358~~.** Read through two arcs, the swing gives a threshold event and a record
  once a turn, with this coupling unchanged (UD-62, UD-64). The count is the number of those
  records. No degree of freedom holds it (UD-65).
* **Discharge.** None for the choice (a boundary). Owed under UO-14: a coupling whose record counts
  turns, and a readout at a site.
  Since ~~#358~~ the tick records of UD-64 count turns with the coupling as it is. What is owed is a
  register for the count, and a readout at a site.

### UA-13 — the two-pointer arena

* **Assumed.** (a) The arena: a projective base times two pointer tori with the product form. Each
  torus has one moving angle and its conserved partner. (b) The measure `echoMuL`: `μ_FS` times Haar
  on each torus. So at the start the fibre coordinates of the two pointers are uniform, independent
  of each other and independent of the base. (c) The joint energy is a sum: the carrier's energy and
  one product term for each pointer. There is no term between the pointers, and neither moving angle
  enters the energy. (d) The operator each pointer is coupled to: a site projector for each of two
  detectors (the choice of UA-5, made twice), or the level swap for the clock's pointer (the choice
  of UA-12) and the site projector on both levels for the detector beside it.
* **Where.** `EchoSigma`, `echoForm`, `echoForm_isSymplectic`, `echoMuL`, `twoCoupledEnergy`,
  `diracTwoPointerEnergy`, `clockSiteEnergy`, `deviceSiteProj` (`LF4/EchoCoupledFlow.lean`,
  `LF4/EchoVolume.lean`, `RecordLayer/UnifiedTwoPointer.lean`; BACKLOG ~~#202~~, ~~#359~~).
* **Standing.** Choice for the arena, as UA-1. Boundary R-015 for the couplings, as UA-5 and UA-12.
  For the measure, UA-2 and UA-3 again on the larger fibre: the flow preserves `echoMuL` (UD-67),
  and that is necessary for the measure to be usable and says nothing about why it is this one. No
  theorem on these arenas states a probability, so the independence in (b) is not yet used as a law.
* **What is derived from it, not assumed.** The pointers act on each other only through the carrier,
  as a static term in its generator (UD-68). The size of that action is bounded (UD-69). Each
  pointer alone is recovered exactly on the fibres where the other profile vanishes (UD-70, UD-71).
* **What it does not cover.** A third pointer. A coupling between the pointers. Since BACKLOG
  ~~#360~~ UA-4 is extended to the arena of the clock's pointer and a site detector: a readout
  region and a ready arc on each torus, as apparatus input. Since BACKLOG ~~#364~~ the same holds
  for the arena of two site detectors. Since BACKLOG ~~#367~~ a third instance of (d) is in use:
  two pointers coupled to the projectors on two regions of sites, the two halves of the ring
  (`regionTwoFlow`), with the same readouts and ready arcs. Since BACKLOG ~~#384~~ a fourth:
  two pointers coupled to the same region projector with different strengths, beside one on
  the other half (UA-17 (f)).
* **Discharge.** None for the choice. M8 compares alternatives. The interaction topology M5 must
  record (UO-15) is read off (c) and (d): the carrier's hopping and the two on-site operators.
  Since BACKLOG ~~#361~~ it is stated as a property of the coupled generators and proved for them
  (UD-77). That is the assumption written on the matrix. It is not derived from anything else.

### UA-14 — the interventions

* **Assumed.** What counts as a local intervention on a two-pointer arena. Two are admitted, and
  each is a change of the initial point of a run. (a) A kick: a unitary with the entries of the
  identity at every index over a site outside a set `Y` is applied to the carrier's initial ray.
  (b) A change of one pointer's conserved angle, with the carrier's ray, that pointer's moving
  angle and the other torus left as they were. No term of the joint energy performs either one,
  and no flow built on the arena carries one initial point to the other.
* **Where.** `IdOffOn`, `arenaKick`, and the pairs of initial points in
  `diracTwoPointerFlow_kick_snd`, `diracTwoPointerFlow_angle_snd` and `clockSiteFlow_kick_snd`
  (`RecordLayer/UnifiedCausalOrder.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#361~~).
* **Standing.** Choice. An influence defined as the response to an intervention is relative to the
  class of interventions admitted. A kick of a pointer's moving angle, a change of a strength or a
  profile, and an operation applied during the run are outside the class.
* **What is derived from it, not assumed.** An upper bound on the size of the response (UD-79 to
  UD-81). The size itself is not derived. The locality
  those bounds use is the generator's: the hopping of UA-8 and the on-site operators of UA-13 (d),
  as UD-77 states them.
* **Discharge.** None for the choice. An intervention made by a third apparatus inside the joint
  energy would replace it. That needs a larger arena.

### UA-15 — the three-pointer arena

* **Assumed.** As UA-13 with one more torus. (a) The arena: a projective base times three pointer
  tori with the product form, each torus with one moving angle and its conserved partner. (b) The
  measure `triMuL`: `μ_FS` times Haar on each torus, so at the start the fibre coordinates of the
  three pointers are uniform and independent of each other and of the base. (c) The joint energy
  is a sum: the carrier's energy and one product term for each pointer. There is no term between
  the pointers, and no moving angle enters the energy. (d) Which operator each pointer is coupled
  to is chosen where the arena is used. Since BACKLOG ~~#369~~ one choice is in use: the level
  swap for the clock's pointer (UA-12) and the site projector on both levels for each of two
  detectors (`clockTwoSiteFlow`). Since BACKLOG ~~#376~~ a second is in use, on a composite
  base: UA-17. Since BACKLOG ~~#381~~ a fourth torus is added by one more step (`QuadSigma`,
  `quadForm`, `fourCoupledEnergy`, `quadMuL` in `LF4/QuadCoupledFlow.lean`; UD-122), with the
  same form of energy and the same product measure, for UA-17 (e). Since BACKLOG ~~#384~~ the
  same four-torus arena is used with another choice of operators, UA-17 (f).
* **Where.** `TriSigma`, `triForm`, `triForm_isSymplectic`, `triMuL`, `threeCoupledEnergy`
  (`LF4/TriCoupledFlow.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#368~~). The construction that adds a torus to an arena is
  `stepForm`, `stepEnergy` (`LF4/PointerStep.lean`): the form of (c) is its hypothesis at each
  application.
* **Standing.** Choice for the arena, as UA-1 and UA-13. Boundary R-015 for the couplings. For the
  measure, UA-2 and UA-3 on the larger fibre: the flow preserves `triMuL` (UD-96), which is
  necessary for the measure to be usable and says nothing about why it is this one.
* **What is derived from it, not assumed.** That the flow is a skew product over the conserved
  angles, and the closed form (UD-95, UD-96). The pointers act on each other only through the
  carrier (read off `threeCoupledFlow_closed`; there is no bound on the size of that action for
  three pointers). No theorem on this arena states a probability, so the independence in (b) is
  not yet used as a law.
* **What it does not cover.** A fifth pointer. A
  coupling between pointers. A pointer whose moving angle enters the energy (UO-24).
* **Discharge.** None for the choice. M8 compares alternatives.

### UA-16 — a clock on its own carrier beside the messenger

* **Assumed.** The author decided (2026-11-11, under UO-16) that M6's clock is a second carrier
  and not a level of the messenger, and that it is the tick clock: the two-level device of UA-12
  with its tick pointer. The following realisation is this brick's choice and not part of that
  decision. (a) The arena is the product of the messenger's arena with its pointers and the
  clock's arena with its pointer, with the sum of the two forms. (b) The measure is the product
  of the two arena measures: at the start the clock is independent of the messenger and of its
  detectors. No theorem on this arena states a probability, and the run of UD-104 starts from
  one point of the clock's arena, so the independence in (b) is not used as a law. (c) Since
  BACKLOG ~~#372~~ the joint energy is the sum of the two energies with no term between the
  factors. A term that couples the clock to a detector is a later choice and will be recorded
  here. Since BACKLOG ~~#376~~ a pointer coupled to a product observable of the clock and the
  messenger is on another arena, that of UA-17, and not on this one. (d) The clock's ring and the messenger's ring are two rings. No distance between a site
  of one and a site of the other is defined, so "the clock is at the departure region" is not a
  statement of this arena.
* **Where.** `clockMessengerEnergy`, `clockMessengerFlow` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedClockMessenger.lean`)
  on `CrossSigma` with `crossForm` (`LF4/CrossCoupledFlow.lean`); the construction for any two
  compact symplectic arenas is `prodEnergy` (`LF4/ProductFlow.lean`).
* **Standing.** Choice for the arena, as UA-1, UA-13 and UA-15. For the measure, UA-2 and UA-3 on
  the product: the flow preserves it (UD-104), which is necessary for it to be usable and says
  nothing about why it is this one.
* **What is derived from it, not assumed.** That the flow is the pair of the two flows (UD-103,
  UD-104), so neither factor's history depends on the other's state.
* **What it does not cover.** A term between the factors. A distance between the rings. A clock
  localised at a region.
* **Discharge.** None for the choice.

### UA-17 — the composite carrier of the messenger and the clock

* **Assumed.** The author decided (2026-11-12, under UO-16) that the messenger and the clock are
  one composite carrier and that the clock's pointer is driven by a product observable. The
  following realisation is this brick's choice and not part of that decision. (a) The base is the
  projective space of the tensor product of the two carriers' state spaces, `ℙ(ℂ^ι ⊗ ℂ^κ)`, with
  the product of the two indices as its index. The carriers are distinguishable: there is no
  symmetrisation. The product rays are expected to be a proper subset of the base (not proved
  for `prodRay`; `bell_not_join` is the analogue for field sectors).
  (b) The arena is that base times three pointer tori with the product form, the arena of UA-15
  on a larger base, with its measure `triMuL`. Product rays are a null set of the base for
  `μ_FS` (a calculation, not in Lean), so a run that starts on a product ray starts on a null
  set. No theorem on this arena states a probability. (c) The carrier's own energy matrix is
  `A₀ ⊗ 1 + 1 ⊗ C₀`: no term between the messenger and the clock. In the instance,
  `H_D ⊗ 1 + 1 ⊗ H_dev` with the generators of UA-8 and UA-12; the clock's ring has its own
  size and hop. Two pointers are coupled to messenger observables `B₁ ⊗ 1`, `B₂ ⊗ 1` (the two
  regions of UA-13, third instance of (d)). The third pointer is coupled to the product
  `P ⊗ X`: `P` the messenger's projector on the departure region, the same region as the first
  pointer's, and `X` the clock's level swap times a strength `g_c` (the operator of UA-12). On
  the three-pointer arena the clock has no pointer of its own: the tick pointer of UA-12 and
  UA-16 is replaced by this one (see (e) for the four-pointer arena). No moving angle enters
  the energy. (d) The clock's ring is not placed. `X` acts at every site
  of the clock's ring, and the gate is a region of the messenger's ring. No distance between a
  site of one ring and a site of the other is defined. (e) Amendment, BACKLOG ~~#381~~: a second
  arena, the base of (a) times four pointer tori (`QuadSigma`, `quadForm`, measure `quadMuL`:
  Haar on the fourth torus, independent of the rest at the start). The fourth pointer is
  coupled to `1 ⊗ g₀ X` (`clockB Mc g₀`, at every site of the clock's ring, so (d) applies),
  through its own profile of its conserved angle; no moving angle enters the energy and there
  is no term between pointers. On this arena the clock has a pointer of its own. The gated
  pointer of (c) stays (not in the variant (f)). Its arcs and start are in UA-4. The route (a clock pointer that runs
  throughout) is on the author's instruction to continue (2026-11-13). The realisation above
  and the wording of this clause are this brick's choice. ⚠️ The author has not yet read this
  amendment. (f) Amendment, BACKLOG ~~#384~~: a variant of the arena of (e) in which the third
  pointer's clock factor is `g₃ · 1` (`clockOne Mc g₃`) and not the level swap. Its operator
  is then `P ⊗ g₃ 1`, a messenger observable: a second pointer on the near half (the region
  of the first pointer), with its own strength and profile (a fourth instance of UA-13 (d)).
  In this variant no term of the energy joins the two factors: the composite base is kept but
  neither half of the author's decision of 2026-11-12 is exercised. From a product ray it is
  the arena of UA-16 with one more region pointer (a calculation, not in Lean). The gated pointer of (c) is
  absent from this variant, so the author's product coupling is not exercised on it; the
  clock keeps the pointer of (e). Form, measure and the fourth pointer are those of (e). The
  variant is this brick's choice, made to add a record without a fifth torus. ⚠️ The author
  has not yet read this amendment.
* **Where.** `prodIdx`, `compositeH`, `gateFlow`, `gateClockFlow`
  (`RecordLayer/UnifiedCompositeCarrier.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#376~~), on `TriSigma` with `triForm`
  (`LF4/TriCoupledFlow.lean`); product rays are `prodRay` (`CV/ProductRay.lean`). For (e):
  `gateFreeFlow`, `gateFreeClockFlow` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedTwoCounts.lean`) on `QuadSigma`. For
  (f): `clockOne`, `threeRecordFlow` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedThreeRecords.lean`).
* **Standing.** Choice for the arena, as UA-1, UA-13, UA-15 and UA-16. Boundary R-015 for the
  couplings. That the composite of two carriers is the tensor product is UA-9's subject;
  `compositeAlgReconstruction` is not invoked here and the tensor product is taken as the
  definition. In (c) and (e) the third coupling is to a joint observable of the two carriers (in (f) it is a messenger observable), so UA-9's
  "locality of de-isolation is built in" is not claimed for it. For the measure, UA-2 and UA-3 on the larger base: the flow preserves `triMuL`
  (UD-111) and, on the arena of (e), `quadMuL` (UD-123), and for (f), `quadMuL` (UD-129), which is necessary for the measure to be usable and says nothing about why it is
  this one.
* **What is derived from it, not assumed.** On a fibre where the third pointer's profile
  vanishes, a product ray stays a product ray, the messenger with its two pointers moves as on
  its own arena, the clock moves freely, and the third pointer's rate is the product of the
  messenger's `⟨P⟩` and the clock's `⟨X⟩` (UD-111). The third pointer moves only while the
  messenger is in the region, within stated bounds (UD-112). On the arena of (e), where
  neither clock pointer acts back, the fourth pointer's displacement is that of the pointer of
  UD-58 on the clock's own arena (UD-123). In the variant (f) the third pointer's rate is
  `g₃` times the messenger's `⟨P⟩`, and from a product ray where the third and fourth profiles
  vanish all three region pointers move as on the messenger's own arena (UD-129).
* **What it does not cover.** The fibres where the third profile does not vanish: there the
  product structure is not shown to persist (that the product term entangles the messenger
  with the clock is expected, not in Lean; in (f) there is no product term and the opposite is
  expected, also not in Lean). An entangled start. A gate by a record: the third
  pointer responds to an occupancy, which is an expectation, and it is a cyclic coordinate by
  UD-106 (generic: it is the pointer the last step adds; not instantiated for `gateFlow`). A
  count held by a degree of freedom or read by a record (ticks and counts of readout histories
  are in UD-113 and UD-123). A clock localised at a region.
  Identical carriers. The interaction topology of UO-15 for this generator: the product term
  joins a region of the messenger's ring to every site of the clock's ring (not in (f)), and
  UD-77 is not proved for it.
* **Discharge.** None for the choice. M8 compares alternatives.

### UA-18 — the radar arena (frozen 2026-11-14; only the bare arena and the gate operators exist)

* **Assumed.** Step (3) of the plan under UO-16. This clause fixes the one arena on which
  steps (4) to (7) are to be carried out. It collects the author's decisions of 2026-11-14.
  In Lean only the bare five-pointer arena (~~#389~~) and the two-carrier gate operators
  (~~#390~~) exist; no carrier, wall, flow with a gate or reading. Every item below is a
  commitment about what will be defined, not a description of something defined.
  (a) *Three carriers on one chain with two walls.* A messenger and two clock carriers, one
  for each observer, each a two-component lattice Dirac field on the same chain of sites
  `0, …, K` with a hard wall at each end; the clocks carry the two-level structure of UA-12.
  The wall is a term of the energy: the chain's hop simply ends there. It is to be obtained as
  the mirror-odd sector of the ring of UA-8 (reflect the sites, change the sign of the lower
  component), which fixes the boundary condition: the upper component vanishes at each wall
  and the hop at the end sites is `√2` times the bulk hop. That boundary condition is this
  clause's choice among the self-adjoint ones. The composite of the three carriers is the
  tensor product, as in UA-17 (b).
  (b) *An observer is a clock packet.* Observer `A` is a packet of its clock carrier at rest
  beside the right wall. Observer `B` is a packet of its clock carrier moving toward `A`. The
  messenger starts with `B`. All three are prepared states (UA-4); they are not varied over.
  (c) *The couplings are coincidence projectors times a clock's level swap.* A coincidence
  projector is diagonal in the site basis of two carriers and is one exactly where their
  sites are within a fixed width of each other. Each pointer is coupled, through a profile on
  its own torus as in UA-13 (d), to `G · X_c`, with `G` a coincidence projector or its
  complement and `X_c` the level swap of one observer's clock. This extends the product
  coupling `P ⊗ X` of UA-17 (c), which the author admitted on 2026-11-12, from one region
  projector to sums of products of site projectors. No other kind of term is admitted on
  this arena. The author decided (2026-11-14): coincidence only. Order projectors (one
  carrier nearer a wall than another, at any distance), which the first draft of this clause
  used, are not local interactions and are not admitted.
  (d) *Five pointers, one for each reading.* For `B`: its ticks while the messenger is with
  it (the emission reading); its ticks while the messenger is not with it (added to the
  emission reading, the return reading); its ticks while `A` is not with it (the meeting
  reading). For `A`: its ticks while the messenger is not with it (the relay reading), and its
  ticks while `B` is not with it (the meeting reading). The arena is `ℂℙ^N × (T²)⁵` with the
  product form and the product measure, as UA-13 (a), (b) and UA-15.
  (e) *A reading is a number of tick records.* The author decided (2026-11-14): the reading
  of a clock at an event is the number of tick records that a gated pointer wrote while its
  gate was open. No coordinate stores the number. It is a fact about the run because the
  pointer stops ticking when the gate closes. With coincidence gates no gate stays closed: it
  reopens when the two carriers separate again, or meet again. So each reading is the number
  of tick records in the pointer's first open period, and it is fixed only from the event
  that closes the gate until the gate reopens. Each theorem is to state that window; ticks
  written after it belong to no reading.
  (f) *What may not be assumed on it.* By the test of UO-16: the messenger's speed and the
  moving clock's rate. The messenger's speed is to come from the band velocity of its packet
  and the clock's rate from UD-138.
  (g) *Measure.* The M6 statements must hold on an open set of the five pointers' conserved
  angles (the author's decision (5)), where the pointers act back on the carriers. Statements
  on the fibres where the profiles vanish are steps.
* **Where.** The bare five-pointer arena: `pentaForm`, `fiveCoupledFlow`, `pentaMuL`
  (`LF4/PentaCoupledFlow.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#389~~). The couplings of (c) as operators:
  `coincProj`, `gateOp` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedCoincidence.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#390~~). Two of them
  in one flow, for two carriers and from product rays only: `coincFlow`
  (`RecordLayer/UnifiedCoincidenceFlow.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#393~~). The reading of (e), under
  hypotheses on where the carriers are: `coincFlow_gated_reading`
  (`RecordLayer/UnifiedGatedReading.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#394~~). The relation "within a width" on a
  ring and the two weight hypotheses for a messenger packet and a clock packet: `ringNear`,
  `flat_gate_open`, `flat_gate_closed` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedGateRegions.lean`, BACKLOG
  ~~#395~~). The run with one gate and its count, under inequalities between the parameters:
  `ringGateRun`, `flat_clock_emission_reading` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedEmissionReading.lean`,
  BACKLOG ~~#396~~). Values meeting those inequalities, along a family:
  `emission_reading_family` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedEmissionValues.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#397~~).
  The run with both gates and the return reading, conditional on a band:
  `ringReturnRun`, `flat_clock_return_reading` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedReturnReading.lean`,
  BACKLOG ~~#399~~). The band replaced by a condition on the clock's phase, for a gate that
  closes quickly: `flat_clock_return_reading_of_phase` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedStopBand.lean`,
  BACKLOG ~~#400~~). Both pointers read in one run, one observer:
  `flat_clock_radar_reading` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedRadarReading.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#401~~).
  Values for it, a lap of the ring: `lap_radar_family`
  (`RecordLayer/UnifiedReturnValues.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#403~~). The five-pointer flow from
  a ray on the fibres where the profiles vanish, and M6 from the top for it:
  `pentaRun_eq`, `penta_radar_lorentz` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedPentaRun.lean`, BACKLOG
  ~~#404~~). Three carriers with the five gates, each pointer's displacement a
  two-carrier one: `triRun`, `tri_radar_lorentz` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedTriCarrier.lean`,
  BACKLOG ~~#405~~). A clock packet at rest on the ring and the rate inequality:
  `rest_clock_packet_ticks`, `abs_movingRate_sub_le` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedClockAtRest.lean`,
  BACKLOG ~~#406~~). The wall state of one Dirac carrier: `oddWall`
  (`RecordLayer/UnifiedOddWall.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#407~~). Where it is, and a clock at
  rest beside a wall: `regionWeight_oddWall_away_le`, `wall_clock_ticks`
  (`RecordLayer/UnifiedWallObservers.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#408~~). The same with numbers,
  and the moving clock's wall state ticking: `wall_clock_localised`,
  `wall_moving_clock_ticks` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedWallBudgets.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#409~~).
  The chain's coincidence relation and its gate bounds: `wallNear`, `wallGate_not_le`,
  `wallGate_le` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedWallGate.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#410~~). That gate for
  wall states, and the wall clocks' swing: `wall_gate_open`, `wall_gate_closed`,
  `wall_clock_swing_fine` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedWallOrbits.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#411~~). The
  four times: `radar_times_relay`, `radar_times_return`
  (`RecordLayer/UnifiedRadarTimes.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#412~~). A fast messenger:
  `light_wall_gate_open`, `light_wall_gate_closed`
  (`RecordLayer/UnifiedFastMessenger.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#413~~). The gates' flow times and
  the return: `gate_open_at_return`, `light_wall_gate_open_return`
  (`RecordLayer/UnifiedGateTimes.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#414~~). Values for the bounds of one
  gate, and the two clocks' gate: `wall_values`, `wallGate_clocks_not_le`
  (`RecordLayer/UnifiedWallValues.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#415~~). The chain with two walls as
  the mirror-odd sector of the ring, with its energy and evolution:
  `odd_sector_chain_flow_equiv` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedOddChain.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#416~~).
  The observer at the far wall: `far_wall_clock_swing_fine`, `far_wall_clock_outside_le`
  (`RecordLayer/UnifiedRingShift.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#417~~). The rate relation and the
  event times: `wall_observer_clock_rate_relation`, `wall_packet_return_time_bound`
  (`RecordLayer/UnifiedEventTimes.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#418~~). Histories from where the
  carriers are: `isGatedHistory_coincShift`, `isAwayHistory_coincShift`
  (`RecordLayer/UnifiedWallHistories.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#419~~). The moving observer's two
  histories for the wall states: `wall_observer_radar_histories`
  (`RecordLayer/UnifiedWallObserver.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#420~~). The relay and the two
  meeting histories: `wall_relay_history`, `wall_meeting_history_moving`,
  `wall_meeting_history_rest` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedWallRelay.lean`,
  `RecordLayer/UnifiedWallMeeting.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#421~~, ~~#422~~). One run with
  the five counts and the Lorentz statement, conditional on angle conditions and sizes:
  `tri_wall_radar_lorentz` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedWallRadar.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#423~~); the
  angle conditions from straight motion: `radar_gate_intervals`
  (`RecordLayer/UnifiedWallIntervals.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#424~~); the family's conditions:
  `radar_family_all_conditions` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedRadarFamily.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#425~~);
  the family's run with a vanishing error, on the fibres where the profiles vanish:
  `radar_family_lorentz` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedRadarRun.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#426~~).
  Nothing else yet. Existing pieces the rest is to be built from: the pointer step
  (`LF4/PointerStep.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#368~~) for the fifth torus; `prodIdx` and `gateFlow`
  (`RecordLayer/UnifiedCompositeCarrier.lean`, ~~#376~~) for the composite and the product
  coupling; `clock_packet_ticks` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedClockPacket.lean`, ~~#388~~) for an
  observer's clock; the packet bounds of ~~#375~~ and ~~#379~~ for the messenger.
* **Standing.** Choice for the arena, as UA-1, UA-13, UA-15, UA-16 and UA-17. Boundary R-015
  for the couplings. A coincidence projector joins two carriers and acts only where they are
  within the fixed width; its complement is `X_c` minus such a term and acts at every greater
  separation. That this meets UA-9's locality is not claimed here; UA-9 is about
  the tensor structure and is not invoked. ⚠️ Written from the author's five decisions of 2026-11-14 and the
  three answers of the same day (the moving observer; what a reading is; coincidence gates
  only). The choices this clause adds on its own are: the boundary condition at the walls;
  `A` beside the right wall; five pointers and no separate detector pointers. The author has
  not yet read the clause.
* **Known risks, stated so that a failure is recognised as one.** (i) A reading is fixed
  only during a window; if a window is shorter than one tick the reading is not determined.
  (ii) Three carriers and five back-reacting pointers
  make the bounds of step (7) long; nothing shows they close. (iii) A clock packet at rest
  beside a wall is not orthogonal to its mirror image, so the mirror route needs a separate
  normalisation for `A`. (iv) The horizon of UD-138 has not been shown long enough for the
  exchange.

### UA-19 — the transponder: a return caused at the event (decided 2026-11-18; nothing of it exists in Lean)

* **Assumed.** Obligation UO-26. The author decided (2026-11-18): the return from a point
  event is launched by a second carrier resting there, not read from a scattered part of
  the messenger. Every item below is a commitment about what will be defined.
  (a) *A transponder.* For each event that is to be radared, one more carrier, with two
  levels as in UA-12, prepared (UA-4) as a packet at rest at the event in its first level.
  The second level is the one that is to leave. The masses of the two levels and the window
  of momenta are not fixed by this clause.
  (b) *The contact term.* A term `g · G · X_T` of the carriers' energy: `G` the coincidence
  projector of the messenger and the transponder, `X_T` the transponder's level swap. This
  is the operator that UA-18 (c) admits. It is in the carriers' energy, with `g` the value
  of the profile at the conserved angle, on every fibre where the profile of a pointer
  coupled to `G · X_T` does not vanish (UD-198). So no term of a new kind is admitted. The
  mechanism is the action of a pointer on the carriers that UA-18 (g) names, on the fibres
  that the statements of M6 so far leave out.
  (c) *What is read.* The transponder's second level, by a coincidence gate with an
  observer's clock, as UA-18 (c) and (e).
  (d) *What may not be assumed.* By the test of UO-16: the time of the launch and the speed
  of the carrier that returns. The launch is to be shown to follow the meeting (UD-211,
  UD-212). The speed is to come from the band of the second level.
* **Where.** Nothing on the arena. For any Hermitian energy and any Hermitian term:
  `Matrix.norm_mul_exp_neg_I_add_mul_le` (`Mathlib/Analysis/Matrix/DuhamelState.lean`, UD-211)
  and `Matrix.le_norm_mul_exp_neg_I_add_mul`
  (`Mathlib/Analysis/Matrix/DuhamelFirstOrder.lean`, UD-212), BACKLOG #437.
* **Standing.** Choice of mechanism for UO-26. The author chose the transponder over the
  scattered part on 2026-11-18, on the assistant's recommendation. The choices this clause
  adds on its own are: two levels as UA-12; the contact term being exactly the operator of
  UA-18 (c); one transponder for each event. The author has not yet read the clause.
* **Known risks, stated so that a failure is recognised as one.** (i) The first-order term
  is proportional to `g` and the remainder of UD-212 to `g² t²`. Where that bound says
  anything the launched weight is small, so a reading needs a budget for a small weight or
  a treatment beyond first order. (ii) The contact term conserves the two carriers' total
  momentum and the level swap does not change the transponder's. Whether the second level
  leaves toward the observer is not known. (iii) On a fibre where a profile does not vanish
  the pointer also moves under the derivative of its profile; the two effects are present
  together. (iv) A packet at rest spreads, so the first level is at rest only for a time.

### UA-20 — a pointer that reads an earlier pointer (admitted 2026-11-19; nothing of it exists in Lean)

* **Assumed.** Obligations UO-24 and UO-25 (5). The author's word is "Continue all of them"
  (2026-11-19), given against a list in which this item was marked as needing the author's
  decision; it is taken here as admitting the term, and is to be struck if that was not
  meant. Every item below is a commitment about what will be defined.
  (a) *The term.* A step in the sense of UA-13 (d) whose observable is `b(θ₁) · K`: `b` a
  smooth profile of period one of an **earlier pointer's moving angle** `θ₁`, `K` a smooth
  function on the arena before that pointer, the whole multiplied by the new pointer's
  profile `g(ψ₂)` as in every step.
  (b) *What it changes.* UA-13 (c) says no moving angle enters the energy. With this term
  the earlier pointer's moving angle does. Its conserved angle `θ₂` is then conserved only
  on the fibres where `g(ψ₂) = 0`.
  (c) *What is read.* The new pointer's ticks. "The first record controls the second
  apparatus" is: the new pointer's displacement is the accumulated `b(θ₁) K` along the
  orbit, so it does not move while `b(θ₁) = 0`.
  (d) *What may not be assumed.* That the earlier pointer displays, or the order of the two
  records: both are to come from the flow.
* **Where.** Nothing. The engine exists: `hamiltonianFlow_stepEnergy` (`LF4/PointerStep.lean`,
  BACKLOG ~~#368~~) solves the flow of a step for any smooth energy and observable on any
  compact symplectic arena, and the arena with one pointer is one. Without the term there
  is no control: `fiveCoupledFlow_shiftPenta` (UD-227).
* **Standing.** Choice of a term of a new kind, recommended by the charter's reading that a
  record is the ontic selection in the fibre
  ([`unified-records-spacetime-task-plans.md`](unified-records-spacetime-task-plans.md) §3). It
  replaces UA-13 (c) for the arenas that use it and leaves every earlier arena as it is.
  The author has not yet read the clause.
* **Known risks, stated so that a failure is recognised as one.** (i) `b` has to be smooth,
  so "the first pointer displays" is a smooth window on its angle and not the indicator of
  the displaying arc; the two records' events are then not the same sets. (ii) Off the
  fibres where `g(ψ₂) = 0` the earlier pointer's conserved angle moves, and with it the
  value of its own profile: the earlier record's coupling drifts. (iii) The control is
  one-way on the fibres where `g(ψ₂) = 0`; whether that is enough for the plan's "the second
  interaction depends on the recorded outcome" is for the author.

## 4. Register 2 — derived properties


### 4.1 On the shared arena `Σ_*`

UD-1 to UD-7 are in `RecordLayer/DiracPointerHamiltonian.lean` (package `diracPointer_hamiltonian`)
and are all exact. UD-31 to UD-34 are in `RecordLayer/DiracPointerWrite.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#351~~, M2
part A), UD-35 to UD-39 in `RecordLayer/DiracPointerThreshold.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#352~~, M2
part B) and UD-40 to UD-44 in `RecordLayer/DiracPointerRecord.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#353~~, M2
part C); they are marked exact or bound. UD-45 to UD-49 are in
`RecordLayer/DiracPointerBorn.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#354~~, M3 part A).
UD-50 to UD-54 are in `RecordLayer/DiracPointerClick.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#355~~, M3 part B).
UD-55 to UD-57 are in `RecordLayer/DiracPointerBasin.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#356~~, M3 part C).

geometric statement.

Exact scope of UD-31 to UD-34. They are statements about a displacement. None is a record: no
readout region, ready state, threshold, outcome or retention interval appears, and no measure. The
comparison with the free chain is a theorem (UD-31) and not a premise: the free chain enters only
through a bound on its distance from the coupled carrier, and on the fibres with `f(θ₂) = 0` through
an equality of generators. The error term is the first-order Duhamel estimate. It is quadratic in
`t` where the signal is linear, so the lower bounds are informative only up to a time of order the
mean occupancy over `|g f(θ₂)|`. No lower bound is uniform in `θ₂`: at a zero of `f'` the pointer
does not move. The fibre on which a statement holds is preparation input (UA-4, item 3); no law for
`θ₂` is used. UD-33 identifies one lifted coordinate; the `ActualOccurrence` structures on the
detector arena (UD-12) are not transported. UD-34's carrier is a beating two-mode state with between
`20/1392` and `212/1392` of its weight on each site, not a carrier that arrives from elsewhere.

Exact scope of UD-35 to UD-39. They are statements about a threshold. None is a record: no
outcome is selected, no `ActualOccurrence` is built, and the signal statements hold at the one
time `T`, with no bound on how long the pointer stays in the displayed arc. The sizes come from
the first-order estimate `‖H‖ t²` on the coupled carrier, so `writeFibres` is a set on which the
write is proved; the set of fibres on which the carrier is displayed at `T` contains it and is
not determined. The signal carrier is prepared on the detector's site and the write is complete
in a time short on the scale `1/‖H‖`: nothing arrives. The control is bounded up to `T` only.
The fibres in neither set, and every fibre with `κ < 0`, carry no statement. Haar measure on the
`θ₂` circle is used to say that two sets are not null and for nothing else: no law for `θ₂` is
used (UA-4, item 3) and no probability of a write is stated.

Exact scope of UD-40 to UD-44. The record is a statement about the readout history of the
flow of UD-1, and the occurrence's evolution is that flow by definition: this is UO-4's test,
and it is a check on a definition, not a derivation. What is derived is the write. The window
is an interval with stated ends and is where a proof ends: for UD-42 nothing is proved after
`2/(3κ)`, in either direction, and for UD-44 the record is proved to have gone by `7/g`. The
times of UD-42 come from the first-order estimate of UD-36 and those of UD-44 from the closed
form of UD-34 with the sine bounded by one; none is sharp. "Selected" means fixed by the ontic
initial point of a deterministic flow: two carriers are compared, on the detector's site and
on another site, and a carrier with weight on both carries no statement. No probability is
stated and no Born weight appears; that is M3. The write is not universal (UD-43, UD-38), the
monotonicity of UD-40 is used with `κ ≥ 0` only, the carrier of UD-42 starts on the detector's
site and does not arrive, and UD-44 holds on one fibre, a null set of fibres.

Exact scope of UD-45 to UD-49. All are equalities of measures, except the lower bound of UD-49. The
preparation is the law of UA-2 with `θ₁` uniform; UD-45 to UD-47 also hold with any law for `θ₂`.
UD-45 to UD-47 are statements about one time, and they are an obstruction, not a derivation: they
say what such statistics cannot depend on. They use two features of the flow of UD-1, that `θ₂` is
conserved and that the carrier's evolution does not depend on `θ₁`, and they do not hold for every
measure-preserving flow. UD-48 is conditional on UA-6: its dependence on the carrier is the rate
field's own, and by §6 it does not pass a gate. UD-49 is the check that the restriction to one time
is needed. It bounds the two-time probability below for the carrier on the detector's site and says
nothing about a carrier with weight on both sites. No error bound of the form UO-8 asks for is
proved by UD-45 to UD-49; UD-57 is the conditional one.
The map `diracPointerHeld` is a comparison map and not a dynamics of `Σ_*`.

Exact scope of UD-50 to UD-54. UD-50 is an equality; the others are bounds. The probability is that
of the joint event. The probability conditional on a ready pointer is six times it, and since the
expectation is linear in the matrix the comparison of UD-53 is the same for both. `E` in UD-53 is
any matrix, so the comparison covers an effect, an effect times an efficiency, and an effect carried
back along a linear evolution of the carrier. UD-54 is one coupling and one readout time, with
`‖H_D‖ T ≤ 1/8`. For a long readout time the hypothesis of UD-51 can fail, and then only UD-50 and
UD-52 are proved. The fibre law is uniform. UD-50 to UD-54 use UA-1 to UA-5 and UA-8, and not UA-6.
They are about the coupling of UA-5, in which the pointer is driven by the expectation of `P_j`;
they do not say that no dynamics on `Σ` gives the quantum weights.

Exact scope of UD-55 to UD-57. The statements about the basin have `IsTorusGenerated` as a
hypothesis, and that hypothesis is UA-6: it puts the quantum weights into the rate field. So
these entries derive no weight, and gate A's answer stands. What they add is the size of the
detector's effect on a statistic that already has the quantum form. The basin event is a region
of the angle `θ₁` cut by the rate field at the carrier's ray at time `t`. The pointer's
displacement does not enter it, it is not the click event of UD-50, and no relation between the
two is proved. The terms of `ε_meas` present are the back-reaction on the carrier and, against
the prepared state, the finite time. A transition region and a retention time are not terms,
because the event is read at one time and is not a threshold on the pointer. The bounds are
upper bounds, not shown to be attained, and they say something only where the right-hand side is
below `1`. The outcome is a basis index (site and spin). The basin of a site is covered with
twice the error and not with the same error. The fibre law is uniform. UD-55 to UD-57 use UA-1
to UA-5 and UA-8, and UA-6 as a named hypothesis.

UD-58 to UD-60 are in `RecordLayer/UnifiedDiracClock.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#357~~, M4 part A). The arena
is `ℂℙ^{4M+3} × T²` of §2, with the coupling of UA-12. Every time in them is the flow parameter
(UA-7). UD-61 to UD-65 are in `RecordLayer/UnifiedDiracClockTicks.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#358~~, M4
part B), on the same arena with the same coupling.

Exact scope of UD-58 to UD-60. The pointer's motion is a component of the Hamiltonian flow of
one energy, and nothing else moves it. That is the part of M4's acceptance these entries meet.
They do not give a record. The pointer integrates the quadrature without rectifying it, so it
swings and returns, and its position at one time does not hold the number of turns. There is no
ready region, no threshold, no `ActualOccurrence` and no retention. They do not give operational
time: every time in them is `t`, and the turning points and the period are flow times that no
observer in the model reads. The law is exact for one mode on the fibres where `f(θ₂) = 0`, a null
set of fibres for a generic profile. Elsewhere it is an upper bound that grows as `t²` and is not
shown to be attained. The turning points and the period are proved on those fibres only. The
displacement's size depends on `θ₂` and on `g`, so the rate enters the pointer's motion as its
period and not as its size. The dilation clause is that of the uncoupled device (UD-14) and is
reused. The readout acts at every site and is not a detector at a place. The detector-resolution
error M4 names is not a term, because there is no readout of the pointer. UD-58 to UD-60 use
UA-1, UA-4, UA-7, UA-8 and UA-12; UA-2 enters only as invariance, and UA-6 not at all.

Exact scope of UD-61 to UD-65. They add no dynamics to UD-58: the pointer, the flow and the coupling
are those of part A, and the readout is two arcs of the pointer circle. What they give. Each tick is
a record of the joint flow, an `ActualOccurrence` in the sense of UD-41. The count of ticks is a
fact about the order of events in the readout history and uses no value of `t`. Operational time is
the count times the rest period `2π/(μ₁ − μ₀)`, one unit for each full turn. Against the dilated
flow time it is off by at most one unit, the detector's resolution, plus the finite-splitting term.
The localisation term M4 names enters the hypotheses under which the history is a swing, through
`clockSwingErr` and the phase of the prepared coherence, and not the final inequality. What they do
not give. The swing is proved outright only for one mode on the fibres with `f(θ₂) = 0`, a null set
for a generic profile. Elsewhere it is four inequalities whose error grows as `T²`, with no
instance. The horizon `T`, and each record's start, write interval and window, are flow times
(UA-7), and nothing in the model selects the event at which the count is taken. The count is not
held by a degree of freedom: each record is erased within its turn, and by UD-65 no real function of
the state gains one a step. UD-65 does not exclude a register that holds the count modulo a fixed
number, and none is built. The readout acts at every site. No arena carries this pointer and the
site pointer of `Σ_*`, so no theorem has a tick record and a position record in one run. (Since
BACKLOG ~~#360~~ one arena carries this pointer and a site pointer on the device, and UD-74 to
UD-76 have both records in one run there, with conditions.) UD-61 to
UD-64 use UA-1, UA-4, UA-7, UA-8 and UA-12. UD-65 uses UA-2 and the flow's invariance (UD-58). UA-6
is not used.

### 4.1c On the two-pointer arenas

UD-66 and UD-67 are in `LF4/EchoVolume.lean` and UD-68 to UD-71 in
`RecordLayer/UnifiedTwoPointer.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#359~~). UD-72 to UD-76 are in
`RecordLayer/UnifiedClockSiteRecords.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#360~~). The arenas are those of §2 under
UA-13. Every time in them is the flow parameter (UA-7). UD-66 to UD-71 meet no obligation of §5.
UD-72 to UD-76 are what condition (d) of UO-14 is annotated with. UD-77 to UD-81 are in
`RecordLayer/UnifiedCausalOrder.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#361~~) and are what UO-15 is annotated with.
UD-82 and UD-83 are in `RecordLayer/UnifiedCausalArrival.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#362~~) and bear on
condition (e2) of UO-15. UD-84 and UD-85 are in `RecordLayer/UnifiedSiteRecord.lean` (BACKLOG
~~#363~~) and bear on conditions (d1) and (d2) of UO-14. UD-86 to UD-88 are in
`RecordLayer/UnifiedTwoSiteRecords.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#364~~) and bear on UO-10. UD-92 is in
`RecordLayer/UnifiedCountAtWrite.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#366~~) and bears on condition (c) of UO-14.
UD-99 and UD-100 are in `RecordLayer/UnifiedSplitCarrier.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#370~~) and answer
UO-10 for a split carrier, as an obstruction. UD-101 and UD-102 are in
`RecordLayer/UnifiedCellSelection.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#371~~), on `Σ_*` with one torus and listed here
because they bear on UO-10: the selection by site cells, and a no-go for readouts local to their
sites.
