| UD-92 | **The clock's count at a position write.** For any swing at the rate `R` and any time `T ≥ 0` there is a count `N`, with `\|2πN − R T\| ≤ 2π`; so `\|N · 2π/R − T\| ≤ 2π/R`, and for two times `\|(N₁ − N₀) · 2π/R − (T₁ − T₀)\| ≤ 4π/R`. In the instance of UD-76, along the one orbit: the site readout's history is a write begun at `τ₀ = (M+1)/(12 g₂)` and complete at `τ₁ = (M+1)/(6 g₂)`; the tick history has counts `N₀` at `τ₀` and `N₁` at `τ₁`, with `N₁ · 2π/R` within one tick period of `τ₁` and `(N₁ − N₀) · 2π/R` within two of `τ₁ − τ₀`. The site readout is `true` exactly for `(M+1)/(8 g₂) < t`, on `0 ≤ t ≤ 3(M+1)/(8 g₂)`, and the tick history has a count `N` at `t* = (M+1)/(8 g₂)` with `N · 2π/R` within one period of `t*` | `IsSwing.exists_tickCount_abs_sub_le`, `IsSwing.abs_tickTime_sub_le`, `IsSwing.abs_tickTime_sub_sub_le`, `clockSite_count_at_write_train`, `echoSiteShown_clockSiteFlow_train_iff`, `clockSite_count_at_display_train` | the count bounds are upper bounds of one or two periods; the switching instant exact; on a null set of fibres, one mode; the counts are of a history up to a flow time, held by no degree of freedom |
| UD-99 | **A pointer driven by a half-occupied site, and the split fibres.** In the setting of UD-86: with `κ > 0`, `96 N ≤ κ` and a starting expectation in `[3/8, 5/8]`, the readout of a ready pointer at `pshift (κ ∫₀ᵗ⟨D⟩)` is `true` at every time in `[23/(25κ), 26/(25κ)]`. For an observable with expectation in `[0, 1]` and `κ > 0` the readout is not `true` at any time in `[0, 1/(6κ)]`. The split fibres `splitFibres` are the pairs `(θ₂, ψ₂)` with `96 B < κ₁`, `96 B < κ₂`, `23 κ₁ < 25 κ₂` and `25 κ₂ < 26 κ₁`, `B = ‖H_D‖ + \|g₁ f(θ₂)\| + \|g₂ h(ψ₂)\|`; on them both rates are positive and `96 ‖H‖ ≤ κ` for each. The set is open for `C¹` profiles. For both profiles `trainF` and equal strengths `g` with `96 ‖H_D‖ < 2 g` it contains `(0, 0)` and has positive measure | `shown_of_half_occupancy`, `not_shown_of_le_sixth`, `splitFibres`, `isOpen_splitFibres`, `splitFibres_bounds`, `zero_mem_splitFibres_trainF`, `volume_splitFibres_trainF_pos` | conditional statements with sufficient constants, not sharp; the fibre set is a definition; the measure is Haar on the pair of conserved angles, for one pair of profiles and equal strengths |
| UD-100 | **A split carrier makes both detectors display.** On the arena of UD-70, detectors at two different sites `j ≠ k`, both pointers in their ready arcs, a split fibre, smooth profiles. For a carrier with at least `3/8` of its occupancy on each of `j` and `k`: at the flow time `1/κ₁` the first readout and the second readout are both `true`, along the one orbit of `diracTwoPointerFlow`. For every carrier the first readout is not `true` up to `1/(6κ₁)` and the second not up to `1/(6κ₂)`. The ray of `e_(k,0) + e_(j,0)` has occupancy `1/2` on each of the two sites, so it is such a carrier | `diracTwoPointerFlow_double_display`, `diracTwoPointerFlow_split_not_shown_early`, `split_ne`, `splitRay`, `arenaObs_siteProj_splitRay_fst`, `arenaObs_siteProj_splitRay_snd`, `diracTwoPointerFlow_splitRay_double_display` | exact given the fibre and the occupancies; one flow time, with no window; about the coupling of UA-5 — an obstruction, not a statement about the weights of quantum mechanics |
| UD-101 | **Site cells.** `siteCell c j` is the region of `Σ_*` where the fibre angle `θ₁` lies in the arc that the rate field `c` gives, at the point's own base point, to one of the two basis states of the site `j`: a union of two basins of UD-21. The cells of two different sites are disjoint. Every basin lies in a site cell, and under the preparation law at any base point the union of the site cells has probability `1`. For a torus-generated rate field the probability of the cell of `j` under the preparation law at the carrier `p` is the occupancy of `j`. For the split carrier of UD-100 each of its two sites has probability `1/2` and the two cells share no arena point | `siteCell`, `measurableSet_siteCell`, `siteCell_disjoint`, `globalBasin_subset_iUnion_siteCell`, `epistemicMeasure_iUnion_siteCell`, `epistemicMeasure_siteCell`, `epistemicMeasure_siteCell_splitRay` | the disjointness and the total are exact and use no hypothesis on `c`; the weights are **conditional on UA-6**; kinematic, as UD-21 is: no flow writes a cell; does not pass a gate (§6) |
| UD-102 | **No readout local to its site has one outcome with the weights.** Three pairwise almost disjoint events have total probability at most `1`. On a chain with three different sites, for any probability law `μ` on a fibre and null-measurable readout regions `S j p` with `μ (S j p ∩ S k p) = 0` for `j ≠ k` and `μ (S j p)` the occupancy of `j`: there is no `r` with `S j p = r j (occupancy of j at p)` for all `j`, `p`. So for the site cells of a torus-generated field there is no such `r`: for at least one site the fibre region is not a function of that site's own occupancy | `measure_add_add_le_one`, `not_siteLocal_of_one_outcome`, `siteCell_not_siteLocal` | exact; a no-go for a stated sense of "local" (dependence on the carrier through the own occupancy only, a shared fibre point with a law independent of the carrier, equality of regions); three sites; one carrier, no settings |

Exact scope of UD-66 to UD-71. What they give. One joint energy, hence one flow, carries a clock
pointer and a detector pointer, or two detector pointers. The flow is solved, and the generator the
carrier obeys is written down with both couplings in it. The earlier one-pointer flows are its
restrictions to the fibres where the other profile vanishes, and away from those fibres the
departure is bounded. What they do not give. UD-66 to UD-71 put no readout, ready state, threshold
or `ActualOccurrence` on these arenas and read no record. UD-72 to UD-76 do that for the clock and
the site detector. The entries of §4.1 and §4.1b are not proved again here: they transfer
verbatim on a null set of fibres and elsewhere only within the bounds of UD-69, which grow as `t²`.
The bounds of UD-69 do not see the distance between the sites and are no propagation bound. (The
bounds that do are UD-78 to UD-81.) There
are two tori and not a family. On a fibre where a pointer's profile vanishes and its derivative does
not, that pointer still moves while the carrier's generator has no term from it. UD-66 to UD-71 use
UA-13, UA-5, UA-8 and UA-12, and the profiles and strengths of UA-4. UA-2 enters only as invariance,
and UA-6 not at all.

Exact scope of UD-72 to UD-76. What they give. On the arena of the clock's pointer and a site
detector, a tick record and a position record are two occurrences with one propagator, read along
one orbit from one start state. With the detector coupled the clock's swing costs one more error
term, the cross-talk, and the site pointer's monotonicity costs none. One instance has both records
with no hypothesis left but the parameters. What they do not give. That instance is on the fibres
where both profiles vanish, a null set of `echoMuL`, and for one momentum mode. On those fibres
neither pointer acts on the device: it moves freely and drives both pointers, the cross-talk is
zero, and an action of one apparatus on the other record is not exhibited. Off those fibres the four
inequalities of the swing and the three displacement values of the write are hypotheses, the swing's
error grows as `T²`, and no instance on a set of fibres of positive measure is given. In the
instance the occupancy of every site is `1/(M+1)`, so the write times are the same for every `k` and
the position record does not single out its site. The record is a definite fact of the one
trajectory: the accumulated occupancy of site `k` crossed the threshold. It is not a statistical
statement, and no weight of quantum mechanics is derived from it. Tick records are held for less
than a turn and the position record for a stated window. Every start, write interval and window is a
flow time (UA-7), and the count of ticks at the position write is not taken. The readouts and ready
sets are definitions. By the test of §6: no hypothesis of UD-76 is a Register 1 entry other than the
parameters of UA-4 and UA-12; no definition contains the write; the object is the flow of the joint
energy; and the statements fail for a flow that does not move the pointers. UD-72 to UD-76 use
UA-13, UA-12, UA-7, UA-8 and the arcs, profiles and strengths of UA-4. UA-2 and UA-6 are not used.

Exact scope of UD-77 to UD-81. What they give. The cone bound of UD-15 was for the free chain. It
now holds for the generators the two-pointer arenas have, with both couplings in them, because the
proof uses only that the generator hops at most one site. A site pointer's displacement responds to a
kick of the carrier far away, or to the other site pointer's conserved angle, by at most a factorial
tail in the distance. The cross-talk bound of UD-69 between two site pointers is thereby joined by
one that falls with the distance between their sites. One statement is about a record: the position
write of UD-74 is still written after a remote kick, within margins. No instance in which those
margins hold is exhibited, and `d` is at most about half the ring. What they do not give. Every
bound at `t > 0` is a tail that is small when `2‖H‖t` is small against `d`, and it is not zero
unless a prefactor vanishes. Nothing says a remote intervention has no effect at a positive time.
This argument gives no exact cone for a lattice with a continuous flow parameter; that none exists
is not proved here. The one exact statement is at time `0`. Every bound is
an upper bound. No response is shown to be attained and none is bounded below, so no influence is
shown to arrive. The speed in the tail is `2‖H‖`, the norm of the whole generator. It is not
optimised and is not the chain's group velocity. No relation between record events is defined. A
relation defined by a threshold on a bound would not be an order without further argument, and
`CausalPrecedes` of UD-16 stays induced by an assumed graph. On the arena of two site pointers no
record is read, so the statements there are about displacements. The clock's pointer is coupled to
the level swap, which is supported on no proper set of sites, so the argument does not apply to it.
Its response to a kick anywhere, and the response of
either pointer of that arena to the other's angle, are not bounded by distance here, and UD-69's
bounds stay the only ones for them. The interventions are changes of the initial point and are no
part of the dynamics (UA-14). By the test of §6: the hypothesis that a generator hops at most one
site is UA-8 and UA-13 (d) restated, and it is proved for the two generators and not assumed of
them; the bounds are not that hypothesis restated, since they carry the norm, the time and the
factorial; the statements do not apply to a generator with a term joining distant sites, which
does not satisfy `HopsOne`. UD-77 to UD-81 use UA-14, UA-13, UA-12, UA-8, UA-7, UA-5 and the arcs,
profiles and strengths of UA-4. UA-2 and UA-6 are not used.

Exact scope of UD-82 and UD-83. What they give. The bounds of UD-78 to UD-81 are from above, and
none of them shows a response that is not zero. UD-82 gives the response its leading term: the
first Taylor term the kick does not commute with, with the next tail as the error. UD-83 evaluates
it once. For one carrier, a kick at the site next to the second detector changes the current into
the detector's site, and the second pointer's displacement then differs between the kicked and
the unkicked run by `2 g₂ h'(ψ₂) J t²` to leading order. Its angles on the circle differ when the
response is also less than a turn. That holds at every pair of pointer
angles, because the couplings are diagonal and drop out of the commutator with `P_k`. What they
do not give. The instance is at distance one. There the cone bound of UD-78 holds with `d = 0`
only, and the response found is of order `t²`, below that bound. For a kick `d` sites away UD-82
gives the order `t^(d+1)` with the coefficient `Δ_d`, and `Δ_d` is not computed for `d ≥ 2`: no
response is shown to arrive from a farther site. The carrier is one stated superposition over two
sites and the kick one stated sign. The carrier has half its weight on the detector's own site,
and the kick changes the relative sign of its two amplitudes; a carrier off the detector's site
is not treated. For a carrier on one site the kick is a phase of the whole ray
and changes nothing. The lower bound is positive for `t < |J|/(4‖H‖²)` and says nothing later. The
error constant is not sharp. The statement is about a displacement and not a record. The kick is
an intervention of UA-14. No relation between record events is defined. By the test of §6: no
hypothesis of UD-83 is a Register 1 entry other than the parameters; the response is computed from
the flow of the joint energy; and its leading term is zero for `J = 0`, a carrier that does not hop. UD-82 and
UD-83 use UA-14, UA-13, UA-8, UA-7, UA-5 and the profiles and strengths of UA-4. UA-2 and UA-6 are
not used.

Exact scope of UD-84 and UD-85. What they give. In the instance of UD-76 the carrier is one
momentum mode and the position record is written at the same times wherever the detector is.
Here the record depends on where the carrier is. A carrier mostly on a site `x` writes when the
detector is at `x` and is not displayed through the same window when the detector is elsewhere.
That holds for every moving angle of the clock's pointer and on a set of pairs `(θ₂, ψ₂)` of
positive Haar measure, for the detector profile `trainF` and a strong enough detector. So for
those carriers the position write no longer needs a null set of fibres. No `echoMuL` measure of
the set of start points is stated. What they do not give. The
arena has one site detector. A detector at another site is another arena with the site projector
moved, so UD-85 compares two flows. One trial in which one of two detectors writes and the other
does not is not shown. That needs the arena of UD-70 with a readout on each torus (UO-10). The
detector is fast: `16‖H‖ < 3κ` puts the write at `1/(2κ)`, a time short against `1/‖H‖`, by which
the occupancy of the detector's site has changed by at most `3/16`. Nothing is said after
`2/(3κ)`, for either placement of the detector. No moving packet is followed, and nothing is
said for a detector slower than the carrier. The carrier is a class of rays and not an evolved
packet. Carriers with occupancy between `1/8` and `7/8` are not covered. The clock's swing is not
treated: on the record fibres its four inequalities of UD-62 stay hypotheses, and the tick record
with them, so the two records together are still outright only in UD-76. The record is a definite
fact of the one trajectory and not a statistic, and no weight of quantum mechanics is derived:
the thresholds are on an occupancy, and what they decide is whether the accumulated occupancy
crossed an arc. The constants are sufficient and not sharp. Every time is a flow time (UA-7). By
the test of §6: no hypothesis of UD-85 is a Register 1 entry other than the parameters and the
ready arc of UA-4; no definition contains the write; the object is the flow of the joint energy;
and the statement fails for a pointer that does not move. UD-84 and UD-85 use UA-13, UA-12, UA-8,
UA-7, UA-5 and the arcs, profiles and strengths of UA-4. UA-2 enters only through the measure on
the pair of conserved angles. UA-6 is not used.

Exact scope of UD-86 to UD-88. What they give. UD-85 compared two arenas. Here one flow carries
two site detectors with a readout each, and for a carrier that sits mostly on one detector's site
that detector writes while the other is not displayed, along the one orbit. The cross-talk between
the detectors is inside the statement, through `‖H‖`. It holds on a set of pairs `(θ₂, ψ₂)` of
positive Haar measure for two `trainF` detectors of equal strength. What they do not give. The
other detector is covered up to `1/(2κ)` of the writing one, the completion of the write. Nothing
is said about it afterwards: the carrier moves on the scale `1/‖H‖`, and a later write by the
other detector is not excluded. A carrier split between the two sites is not covered, and for it
exactly one outcome is not shown. With half the occupancy on each site both displacements are
within the bounds of `κ t / 2`, so this argument does not prevent both detectors, or neither,
from displaying. So one trial, one outcome is shown only where the outcome is already fixed by
where the carrier is. The detectors are fast, `16‖H‖ < 3κ` for both, and no moving packet is
followed. The two rates are within a factor `4/3` of each other. The only carrier of the class
exhibited is the one on the site. The record is a definite fact of the one trajectory: no
probability is stated and no weight of quantum mechanics is derived. There is no clock on this
arena and no statement about signalling. No `echoMuL` measure of the set of start points is
stated. Every time is a flow time (UA-7). By the test of §6: the hypotheses of UD-88 that are
Register 1 entries are the ready arcs and the parameters of UA-4 and UA-5; the carrier's
occupancy and the fibre membership are conditions on the start point; no definition contains the
write; the object is the flow of the joint energy; and the statement fails for pointers that do
not move. UD-86 to UD-88 use UA-13, UA-8, UA-7, UA-5 and the arcs, profiles and strengths of
UA-4. UA-2 enters only through the measure on the pair of conserved angles. UA-6 is not used.

Exact scope of UD-92. What it gives. A radar coordinate is the reading of a clock at an emission or
a reception. UD-92 is not one: no signal is emitted or received. It is the first reading of the
clock of UD-58 at an event that a record supplies:
the instant the position readout switches, which the record's own history fixes. The count there,
times the tick period, is that instant up to one period, and an interval between two such
instants up to two. What it does not give. The count at the event is the count of the tick
history up to the flow time of the event. The two histories are compared at equal values of the
flow parameter (UA-7), and nothing in the arena makes the comparison: no register for the count
is built on this arena, and no register is stamped by the write. (On the one-pointer clock arena
no real function of the state holds the count, UD-65; that statement is not restated for
`clockSiteFlow`.) The instance is UD-76's: one momentum
mode on a null set of fibres, where the device moves freely and the position record does not
single out its site. Tick records are erased within their turn, so the count is a property of the
history and not of the state at the event. The resolution is one tick and is not improved. `R` is
the level splitting of the mode; the dilation clause of UD-59 is not used. By the test under
UO-14: the instant is not a readout scheduled at a flow time, since it is where the site
readout's history turns; but reading the tick history "up to" it uses the flow parameter to
align the two histories, and that use is access to UA-7. UD-92 uses UA-13, UA-12, UA-7 and the
arcs, profiles and strengths of UA-4. UA-2 and UA-6 are not used.

Exact scope of UD-99 and UD-100. What they give. UD-88 left open the carrier that is split between
the two detectors' sites, which is the case "one trial, one outcome" is about. UD-100 settles it
for this arena: both detectors display. Each pointer is driven by the occupancy of its site, so a
carrier with half its occupancy on each site turns both pointers at half rate, and both cross the
threshold at about `1/κ`. In this regime the flow does not select one site. So with the coupling of
UA-5 and these readouts, on these fibres and for these carriers, one run does not have one
outcome. That is an obstruction, of the same kind as
gate A's (UD-50 to UD-53): the pointer reads an occupancy and not an outcome. It holds on a set of
pairs `(θ₂, ψ₂)` of positive Haar measure. What they do not give. Nothing here is a prediction
about detectors. The weights of quantum mechanics for one click or the other are not derived and
are not contradicted: in this contract they are conditional on the cell law (UA-6, Posit 1), and
this arena does not use it. What would select one outcome is not in this joint energy. The
detectors are fast, `96‖H‖ < κ` for both, and `κ₂` is between `0.92 κ₁` and `1.04 κ₁`, so that one flow time serves both. The carriers are a class, at least `3/8` of the
occupancy on each of the two sites, with the equal superposition exhibited. Between that class
and the class of UD-88 nothing is proved. Both readouts are shown `true` at one flow time; no
`IsRecordWrite` with a window is built. No `echoMuL` measure of the set of start points is
stated. By the test of §6: the hypotheses of UD-100 that are Register 1 entries are the ready
arcs, the parameters and the coupling; the occupancies and the fibre membership are conditions on
the start point; and the statement is about the flow of the joint energy, which fails to give one
outcome. UD-99 and UD-100 use UA-13, UA-8, UA-7, UA-5 and the arcs, profiles and strengths of
UA-4. UA-2 enters only through the measure on the pair of conserved angles. UA-6 is not used, and
that is the point.

Exact scope of UD-101 and UD-102. What they give. UD-100 is on the two-pointer arena: pointers
coupled through their own site projectors both display, for the carriers and fibres stated there.
UD-101 and UD-102 are on `Σ_*`, with one fibre angle and no detector's pointer; they are listed
here because they bear on UO-10. If the outcome is read from the cell of the arena point, then at
most one site is
selected, for every arena point and every carrier, with no hypothesis on the rate field. With the
weights assumed (UA-6), the split carrier selects each of its two sites with probability `1/2`
and never both. And UD-102 says this cannot be had locally: on three sites, a family of readout
regions that depends on the carrier only through the occupancy of its own site cannot have one
outcome almost surely together with the weights. The proof uses the three carriers split over the
three pairs of sites. So the site cells are not all local to their sites: for at least one
site the fibre region of its cell is not a function of that site's occupancy alone. For the cells
as ordered here the regions of the first and the last site are such functions, and a site in
between has its arc placed by the occupancies before it in the index order (a calculation, not in
Lean). Which cell holds the arena point is a fact of the ray and the fibre angle. What they do not give.
The statements are kinematic. A site cell is a region. No flow of this corpus writes it, no
pointer displays it, and no `IsRecordWrite` is built; UD-50 to UD-53 show that the coupling of
UA-5 does not supply it. The weights are assumed and not derived. The sense of "local" is the
stated one: the fibre point is shared by all sites and its law does not depend on the carrier
(UA-2). A model whose hidden variable is a position, with the carrier's weights as its law, is
outside the hypothesis; it is local and gives one outcome. So UD-102 is a statement about `Σ_*`,
where the ontic state is the ray and a fibre point. It asks equality of regions, not equality up
to null sets. It needs three sites and says nothing for two. It has one carrier and no settings,
so it is not Bell's theorem. The order of the arcs in a cell is the order of the index, which is
a convention; the theorem says that some dependence on the carrier beyond the site's own occupancy is
unavoidable, and does not say which; a dependence on other data of the same site is not excluded
by the statement. The exclusion and the weights are assumed for all carriers, and the regions are
null-measurable. The hypothesis is met by `momentContext` on any chain with three sites. By the test of §6: UD-101's weights are the rate field's, and
its disjointness is a property of the definition (arcs at running sums), so it passes no gate; UD-102 has no Register 1 entry among its hypotheses except the law of the
fibre being independent of the carrier, and it is a no-go. UD-101 uses UA-1, UA-2, UA-3 (every probability here is the
typicality reading of the preparation law), the site index of UA-8 (`diracHam` is not used) and
UA-6 as a named hypothesis, extended as stated under UA-6. UD-102 uses the site index of UA-8
and, in its application to the cells, UA-2, UA-3 and UA-6.

### 4.1d On the region-detector arena

UD-89 to UD-91 are in `RecordLayer/UnifiedArrivalRecord.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#365~~). The arena is
`ℂℙ^{2M+1} × T²` with the joint energy `−2⟨H_D⟩ − 2 g f(θ₂)⟨P_A⟩`: `Σ_*` of §2 with the site
projector replaced by the projector on a region `A` of sites (UA-5, extended). Its flow is
`regionPointerFlow`. It is not `Σ_*`. Every time is the flow parameter (UA-7). UD-89 to UD-91 are
what UO-16 is annotated with; they do not meet it. UD-93 and UD-94 are in
`RecordLayer/UnifiedDepartureArrival.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#367~~), on the two-pointer arena of UA-13
with two region projectors, `ℂℙ^{2M+1} × T² × T²` with the joint energy
`−2⟨H_D⟩ − 2 g₁ f(θ₂)⟨P_A⟩ − 2 g₂ h(ψ₂)⟨P_B⟩` and the flow `regionTwoFlow`. UD-105 is in
`RecordLayer/UnifiedTransitCertificate.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#373~~), on the same arena and start as
UD-94.
UD-108 and UD-109 are in `RecordLayer/UnifiedFineTransit.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#375~~). UD-110 is
in `CV/ProductRay.lean` and has no arena. UD-111 to UD-114 are not on this arena: they are on
the composite carrier of UA-17 (`RecordLayer/UnifiedCompositeCarrier.lean`,
`RecordLayer/UnifiedGateTicks.lean`; BACKLOG ~~#376~~, ~~#377~~) and are filed here because they
use the region projectors and the packets of this section. UD-115, UD-116 and UD-119 are on
the same composite carrier (`RecordLayer/UnifiedGateCount.lean`, `RecordLayer/UnifiedFineReturn.lean`;
BACKLOG ~~#378~~, ~~#379~~); UD-117 is for the free packet and UD-118 for the arena of UD-93.
UD-120 and UD-121 are on the composite carrier (`RecordLayer/UnifiedGateReturn.lean`, BACKLOG
~~#380~~). UD-122 is the four-pointer arena (`LF4/QuadCoupledFlow.lean`); UD-123 and UD-124 are
on the composite carrier with its fourth pointer (`RecordLayer/UnifiedTwoCounts.lean`, BACKLOG
~~#381~~). UD-125 is on the arena of UD-93 and UD-126 on the composite carrier with its fourth
pointer (`RecordLayer/UnifiedRoundTrip.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#382~~). UD-127 is on the arena of UD-93
and UD-128 on the composite carrier with its fourth pointer
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedArrivalReturn.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#383~~). UD-129 and UD-130 are on the
variant of UA-17 (f), with a second near-half pointer in place of the gated pointer
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedThreeRecords.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#384~~). UD-131 is a statement about two
readout histories and the function that drives them, and UD-132 is its instance in the run
of UD-130 (`RecordLayer/UnifiedReadoutCertificate.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#385~~). UD-133 is the
companion statement for a rise, from one pointer read on two arcs, and UD-134 its instance
in the same run, with the dip (`RecordLayer/UnifiedReadoutRise.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#386~~).
UD-135 and UD-136 exhibit one start that meets the hypotheses of UD-130, UD-132 and UD-134
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedDipInstance.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#387~~). UD-137 and UD-138 are on the
clock arena of UA-12, for a packet of the clock carrier
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedClockPacket.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#388~~). UD-139 is the bare five-pointer
arena of UA-18 (`LF4/PentaCoupledFlow.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#389~~), and UD-140 its couplings
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedCoincidence.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#390~~). UD-141 and UD-142 close UD-138's
conditions for narrow packets (`RecordLayer/UnifiedClockPacketInstance.lean`, BACKLOG
~~#391~~); UD-143 and UD-144 bound where such a packet is
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedClockPacketLocalised.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#392~~). UD-145 and UD-146 are the
flow with two gated pointers from a product ray (`RecordLayer/UnifiedCoincidenceFlow.lean`,
BACKLOG ~~#393~~). UD-147 and UD-148 are the reading of a gated pointer under hypotheses on
where the carriers are (`RecordLayer/UnifiedGatedReading.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#394~~). UD-149 and
UD-150 are the two weight hypotheses for a messenger packet and a clock packet on one ring
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedGateRegions.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#395~~). UD-151 and UD-152 are the clock's swing with a
margin and the gated pointer's count for a messenger packet and the flat clock packet
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedEmissionReading.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#396~~). UD-153 and UD-154 are values for it: four
scalar conditions and a family with a relative error at most `15/k`
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedEmissionValues.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#397~~). UD-155 and UD-156 are the count of a swing
over a window and of a pointer whose swing is offset, the pointer side of the return reading
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedWindowSwing.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#398~~). UD-157 and UD-158 are the return
reading: the count of the complementary gate, conditional on a band
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedReturnReading.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#399~~). UD-159 and UD-160 bound where the
emission pointer stops and replace the band by a condition on the clock's phase, for a gate
that closes quickly (`RecordLayer/UnifiedStopBand.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#400~~). UD-161 and UD-162 read both
pointers of one run and form the radar time and distance in ticks
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedRadarReading.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#401~~). UD-163 and UD-164 state M6 from
the top as one conditional theorem and fix the list of what remains
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedRadarSkeleton.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#402~~). UD-165 and UD-166 exhibit
values for a run with a return and a quickly closing gate, on the ring
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedReturnValues.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#403~~). UD-167 and UD-168 solve the
five-pointer flow on the fibres where the profiles vanish and state M6 from the top for it
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedPentaRun.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#404~~). UD-169 and UD-170 take the carrier to
be a messenger and two clocks and reduce each pointer's history to a two-carrier one
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedTriCarrier.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#405~~). UD-171 and UD-172 give the rate
inequality and a flat clock packet at rest (`RecordLayer/UnifiedClockAtRest.lean`, BACKLOG
~~#406~~); UD-173 the mirror-odd state of a packet, the wall of UA-18 (a)
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedOddWall.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#407~~). UD-174 and UD-175 place the messenger's
wall state through the reflection and give a clock at rest beside a wall that ticks
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedWallObservers.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#408~~). UD-176 and UD-177 evaluate the
budgets and make the moving clock's wall state tick (`RecordLayer/UnifiedWallBudgets.lean`,
BACKLOG ~~#409~~). UD-178 gives the coincidence relation of the walled chain and its gate
bounds from arcs (`RecordLayer/UnifiedWallGate.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#410~~). UD-179 and UD-180 show that gate open and closed for wall
states and give the wall clocks' swing (`RecordLayer/UnifiedWallOrbits.lean`, BACKLOG
~~#411~~); UD-181 defines the four times of the run and bounds what a slower messenger
costs (`RecordLayer/UnifiedRadarTimes.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#412~~). UD-182 gives a fast messenger its budgets and
wall gate (`RecordLayer/UnifiedFastMessenger.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#413~~); UD-183 the flow
times at which the wall gate is open and closed (`RecordLayer/UnifiedGateTimes.lean`,
BACKLOG ~~#414~~). UD-184 passes the tolerance test for the bounds of one gate, and UD-185
gives the two clocks' gate (`RecordLayer/UnifiedWallValues.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#415~~). UD-186 shows
that the mirror-odd sector is a chain with hard ends (`RecordLayer/UnifiedOddChain.lean`,
BACKLOG ~~#416~~). UD-187 translates carriers round the ring, which puts the observer at
rest at the far wall (`RecordLayer/UnifiedRingShift.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#417~~); UD-188 gives
the event times from the gates and the rate relation with the packets' velocity
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedEventTimes.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#418~~). UD-189 derives the gated and the away
history of a gate displacement from where the two carriers are
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedWallHistories.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#419~~). UD-190 proves the moving
observer's emission and return histories on the walled chain
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedWallObserver.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#420~~). UD-191 and UD-192 prove the relay
and the two meeting histories (`RecordLayer/UnifiedWallRelay.lean`,
`RecordLayer/UnifiedWallMeeting.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#421~~, ~~#422~~). UD-193 joins the five for one run
and applies UD-170; UD-194 gives its seven angle conditions from straight motion
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedWallRadar.lean`, `RecordLayer/UnifiedWallIntervals.lean`, BACKLOG
~~#423~~, ~~#424~~). UD-195 proves the six conditions and the sizes for the family
(`RecordLayer/UnifiedRadarFamily.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#425~~). UD-196 joins UD-193 to UD-195: the
family's run satisfies the Lorentz statement with an error of order `1/k` of its length, on the
fibres where the profiles vanish (`RecordLayer/UnifiedRadarRun.lean`, BACKLOG ~~#426~~).

Exact scope of UD-89 to UD-91. What they give. The records of UD-85 and UD-88 are written by fast
detectors on a carrier that is at the detector from the start. Here the carrier starts away from
the detector. The packet travels, and the far half's pointer writes at some flow time in
`[625π/3, 4375π/3]`, an interval that contains the predicted arrival `625π`: the readout is not
`true` before `625π/3`, while along the uncoupled chain the far half holds at most a quarter of
the weight, and the write is complete by `4375π/3`. That the write is complete after the arrival
is not proved. The
pointer is a pointer of the joint Hamiltonian flow, with its back-reaction on the carrier inside
the statement. The conserved angles on which it holds have positive Haar measure, for `trainF`
at one strength; the carrier is one ray, so no measure of the set of start points is stated. The
weight bounds are UD-12's module's, for the free chain; what is new is that the detector is
generated. What they do
not give. The detector is on half the ring: the record says the accumulated weight on that half
has crossed the threshold, and it locates nothing within the half. The write is complete at some `τ` in `[625π/3, 4375π/3]`. That interval
contains the predicted arrival `625π` and is seven times longer at its end than at its start, and
`τ` is not computed, so no speed is read off the record. Before `625π/3` the readout is silent
because the accumulated weight is small. The far half is not empty then: it holds the packet's
tail. There is one packet and one ring, and no family along which the interval shrinks. The
detector is weak, of strength about `1/(1250π)`, and the arrival fibres are where
`|g f(θ₂)| t²` is small over the transit: a neighbourhood of `θ₂ = 0` whose size is not computed.
There is one pointer. The arena has no emission record, no second detector and no clock, so no
radar coordinate can be formed on it. The record is a definite fact of the one trajectory, held
for `1/(3κ)`, and no weight of quantum mechanics is derived. By the test of §6: the hypotheses of
UD-91 that are Register 1 entries are the ready arc, the profile and the strength of UA-4 and the
coupling of UA-5; the fibre membership is a condition on the start point; no definition contains
the write; the proof uses the carrier's motion (UA-8) through the weight bounds of UD-12's
module. The conclusion is not shown to fail for a carrier that does not move, and it would not
fail for one holding a constant weight between `1/7` and `1/2` on the far half at `θ₂ = 0` (a
calculation, not in Lean). The threshold reads accumulated weight. It does not by itself certify
a crossing. UD-89 to
UD-91 use UA-8, UA-7, UA-5 and the arc, profile and strength of UA-4. UA-2 enters only through
the measure on `θ₂`. UA-6 is not used.

Exact scope of UD-93 and UD-94. What they give. UD-91 has the arrival record alone. Here the same
run has a second pointer, on the half of the ring the packet starts in, and both records are read
along one orbit of one joint flow: the near pointer writes by `625π/3`, while along the uncoupled
chain the packet is still mostly on its half, and the far pointer does not show before `625π/3`
and writes by `4375π/3`. The back-reaction of both detectors on the carrier is inside the
statement. The pairs of conserved angles on which it holds have positive Haar measure, for
`trainF` at two stated strengths; the carrier is one ray, so no measure of the set of start points
is stated. What they do not give. The first record is a departure and not an
emission. The packet is prepared on the near half and the pointer records that accumulated weight
on that half crossed the threshold; nothing emits it and no instant of emission is singled out.
With write interval `[0, τ_d]` the not-displayed-before clause is empty: the statement is that the
near readout is `true` through its window for some `τ_d ≤ 625π/3`. The order of the two records is
through the one time `625π/3`: the first write is complete by it and the second readout does not
show before it, and the two could meet there. `τ_d` and `τ_a` come from the intermediate value
theorem and are not computed, so no transit time and no speed are read off the pair. Each
detector is on half the ring. There is one packet and one ring, and the detectors are weak. There
is no clock on the arena: no time is read by a clock at either record, so no radar coordinate is
formed. A clock in the same run is a third pointer. (The arena for it is UD-96.) The
records are definite facts of the one trajectory, each held for its window, and no weight of
quantum mechanics is derived. By the test of §6: as for UD-91, with two pointers. The statement
about the order fails for a carrier whose far-half weight stays zero. With a constant far-half
weight between `1/7` and `1/4` both conclusions would still hold (a calculation, not in Lean), so
the order through `625π/3` does not certify a transit.
UD-93 and UD-94 use UA-13, UA-8, UA-7, UA-5 and the arcs, profiles and strengths of UA-4. UA-2
enters only through the measure on the pair of conserved angles. UA-6 is not used.

Exact scope of UD-105 (BACKLOG ~~#373~~). What it gives. The records of UD-91 and UD-94 are threshold
crossings of accumulated weight, and a carrier whose far-half weight is a constant between `1/7`
and `1/4` would satisfy their conclusions (a calculation, not in Lean). UD-105 is a statement on
the same flow and start that no carrier with a constant far-half occupancy satisfies: the mean
occupancy of the far half is at most `1/3` over `[0, T₁]` and at least `2/3` over `[T₂, T₃]`, and
a constant `w` would have to be at most `1/3` and at least `2/3` (`integral_arenaObs_of_const`
with `not_transit_of_linear`). The estimates are those of UD-94 and `dirac_arrival`, integrated
over a second window; what is new is the form of the statement, an upper bound early and a
lower bound on a late increment for the same half, and a fibre condition with no relation
between a detector's rate and the transit time. It holds on a set of positive Haar measure for
both profiles `trainF` and any positive strengths, a neighbourhood of `(0, 0)` whose size is not
computed. What it does not give. The certificate is four inequalities on the carrier's
accumulated occupancy. No pointer, rate or angle is in it; the pointers enter through an identity
of angles (`regionTwoFlow_cross_pointers`) and carry the accumulated occupancies only modulo one
turn. For the strengths of UD-94 the proved bounds allow the far pointer up to `7/3` turns by
`T₃`, and at least `1/2`. There is no readout region, no threshold and no `IsRecordWrite`, so
this is not a record and it certifies nothing to an observer who has only the records of UD-94.
The near half's two inequalities restate the far half's, since the two occupancies add to one.
The two rate conditions of `transitFibres` are used by no theorem; the certificate needs only
the back-reaction condition. The four times are values of the flow parameter (UA-7). What is
excluded is a carrier whose occupancy of the far half is constant in time; a carrier that moves
in some other way and gives the same numbers is not excluded, and "transit" has no direction,
path or speed in it. The change is located only within `[0, T₃]`: the four numbers give a time
in `[0, T₁]` with far occupancy at most `1/3` and a time in `[T₂, T₃]` with at least `2/3`. The
location to `[T₁, T₂]`, a factor of five, belongs to the free packet's pointwise bounds and is
not proved for the coupled carrier. The write times of UD-91 and UD-94 are not located. The
fibre condition is not that of UD-94, and no theorem states the records and the certificate on
a common fibre. No distance, no speed and no clock appear, and nothing here is evidence about
Lorentz kinematics. By the test of §6: the hypotheses that are Register 1 entries are the
couplings and the parameters; no definition contains the conclusion; the object is the flow of
the joint energy; and the statement fails for a carrier with a constant occupancy, which is what
was asked. It passes no gate: it has no record in it. UD-105 uses UA-13, UA-8, UA-7, UA-5 and the
profiles and strengths of UA-4. UA-2 enters only through the measure on the pair of conserved
angles. UA-6 is not used.

### 4.1e The pointer step and the three-pointer arena

UD-95 is in `LF4/PointerStep.lean` and UD-96 in `LF4/TriCoupledFlow.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#368~~). UD-95
is about any compact symplectic arena and has no carrier in it. UD-96 is the arena of UA-15. No
record is read in either, and they meet no obligation of §5. UD-97 and UD-98 are in
`RecordLayer/UnifiedClockTwoSites.lean` (BACKLOG ~~#369~~): the device, the clock's pointer and
two site detectors on that arena, `ℂℙ^{4M+3} × T² × T² × T²`, with the flow `clockTwoSiteFlow`.
They are what UO-16 is annotated with; they do not meet it.

| ID | Property | Lean | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|
| UD-95 | **Adding a pointer torus to a symplectic arena.** For a `C^∞` manifold `S` modelled on a finite-dimensional space, with a symplectic form `β`: `stepForm β = β ⊕ dψ₁ ∧ dψ₂` on `S × T²` is symplectic. For smooth `H`, `G` on `S` and a smooth periodic `g`, the energy `stepEnergy H G = H(s) + G(s) g(ψ₂)` is smooth, and if `X_H`, `X_G` are Hamiltonian vector fields of `H`, `G` for `β` then `(X_H + g(ψ₂) X_G, (G g'(ψ₂), 0))` is one of the step energy for `stepForm β`. For compact `S` the Hamiltonian flow of the step energy is `(s, ψ) ↦ (φ_t(s), (ψ₁ + g'(ψ₂) ∫₀ᵗ G(φ_u(s)) du, ψ₂))`, with `φ` the Hamiltonian flow on `S` of `H + g(ψ₂) G`; in particular its first component is that base flow. If for every `c` the base flow of `H + c G` at time `t` preserves a measure `μ`, the flow at time `t` preserves `μ ⊗ vol_{T²}`. The form `echoForm` is `stepForm arenaForm` and `twoCoupledEnergy` is a `stepEnergy` | `stepForm`, `stepForm_isSymplectic`, `stepEnergy`, `stepEnergy_eq`, `contMDiff_stepTerm`, `contMDiff_stepEnergy`, `contMDiff_add_const_smul`, `isHamiltonianVectorField_stepLift`, `isHamiltonianVectorField_stepTerm`, `isHamiltonianVectorField_step`, `hamiltonianFlow_stepEnergy`, `hamiltonianFlow_congr`, `hamiltonianFlow_stepEnergy_fst`, `map_hamiltonianFlow_stepEnergy`, `echoForm_eq_stepForm`, `twoCoupledEnergy_eq_stepEnergy` | exact; for any compact symplectic arena, with no carrier, detector or record in it; the measure statement is conditional on the base flows |
| UD-96 | **The flow of a ray coupled to three tori.** `triForm = stepForm echoForm` on `ℂℙᴺ × T² × T² × T²` is symplectic. `threeCoupledEnergy` is the step over `twoCoupledEnergy` with the ray observable `−2⟨B_C⟩`: `−2⟨A⟩ − 2⟨B_A⟩ f(θ₂) − 2⟨B_B⟩ g(ψ₂) − 2⟨B_C⟩ k(χ₂)`, smooth. Adding `c` times `−2⟨B_C⟩` to the two-torus energy is replacing `A` by `A + c B_C`. `threeCoupledFlow` is its Hamiltonian flow: continuous and measurable at each time, a one-parameter group. With `χ₂` held, the ray and the first two tori move by `twoCoupledFlow` of UD-66 with uncoupled matrix `A + k(χ₂) B_C`, `χ₁` advances by `k'(χ₂)` times the accumulated `−2⟨B_C⟩` along that orbit, and `χ₂` is fixed. In closed form, for Hermitian matrices: the ray moves by the Schrödinger unitary of `A + f(θ₂) B_A + g(ψ₂) B_B + k(χ₂) B_C`, and `θ₁`, `ψ₁`, `χ₁` advance by `f'(θ₂)`, `g'(ψ₂)`, `k'(χ₂)` times the accumulated `−2⟨B_A⟩`, `−2⟨B_B⟩`, `−2⟨B_C⟩` along that one orbit. The flow preserves `triMuL = μ_FS ⊗ vol_{T²} ⊗ vol_{T²} ⊗ vol_{T²}` | `TriModel`, `TriSigma`, `triForm`, `triForm_isSymplectic`, `echoRayEnergy`, `contMDiff_echoRayEnergy`, `threeCoupledEnergy`, `threeCoupledEnergy_apply`, `contMDiff_threeCoupledEnergy`, `twoCoupledEnergy_add_smul_echoRayEnergy`, `threeCoupledFlow`, `continuous_threeCoupledFlow`, `measurable_threeCoupledFlow`, `threeCoupledFlow_zero`, `threeCoupledFlow_add`, `threeCoupledFlow_apply`, `threeCoupledFlow_closed`, `triMuL`, `triMuL_map_threeCoupledFlow` | exact; for any Hermitian quadruple, not about detectors; the measure read off the closed form — the top-form measure of `triForm` is not identified with `triMuL` |
| UD-97 | **The device, the clock's pointer and two site detectors.** On the third torus the ready set `triThdReady`, the displayed set `triThdDisplay` and the readout `triThdShown` are the arcs `readyArc` and `displayArc` on the third pointer; all are measurable. On a fibre where the third profile vanishes, the first component of `threeCoupledFlow` is `twoCoupledFlow` and the third pointer is turned by `k'(χ₂)` times the accumulated `−2⟨B_C⟩` along that orbit. `clockTwoSiteFlow` is `threeCoupledFlow` for the device's Hamiltonian, the level swap and two site projectors: a one-parameter group that preserves `triMuL`. On a fibre with `k(χ₂) = 0` its first component is `clockSiteFlow` of UD-71, and its third pointer is its start translated by the rate `−2 g₃ k'(χ₂)` times the accumulated occupancy of site `k₃` along the orbit of `clockSiteFlow` | `triThdReady`, `measurableSet_triThdReady`, `triThdDisplay`, `measurableSet_triThdDisplay`, `triThdShown`, `measurable_triThdShown`, `triThdShown_eq_true_iff`, `threeCoupledFlow_fst_of_lift_eq_zero`, `threeCoupledFlow_snd_of_lift_eq_zero`, `clockTwoSiteFlow`, `clockTwoSiteFlow_zero`, `clockTwoSiteFlow_add`, `triMuL_map_clockTwoSiteFlow`, `clockTwoSiteFlow_fst_of_lift_eq_zero`, `clockTwoSiteFlow_thd_pointer_of_lift_eq_zero` | the readout and the ready set are definitions (apparatus input, UA-4); the rest exact; the reductions hold on the fibres where the third profile vanishes, a null set for a generic profile |
| UD-98 | **Three records in one run, and the clock's count at each detector's record.** A pointer at `1/12` turned by `t c`, `c > 0`, is in the displayed arc exactly for `1/(4c) < t`, on `0 ≤ t ≤ 3/(4c)`. The start `clockTwoSiteTrainStart` is that of UD-76 with a third pointer at `1/12` and `χ₂ = 0`; all three profiles are `trainF`. Along the orbit of `clockTwoSiteFlow` from it the first component is the orbit of UD-76, so the clock's pointer is a swing at the level splitting `R` on every horizon, for all strengths of the detectors. With `g₂, g₃ > 0`, `μ₀ < μ₁ < 2μ₀`, `R` the mode's level splitting and the clock strength `g₁` fixed as in UD-76: the first detector's readout is `true` exactly for `(M+1)/(8 g₂) < t` on `0 ≤ t ≤ 3(M+1)/(8 g₂)`, and the second detector's exactly for `(M+1)/(8 g₃) < t` on `0 ≤ t ≤ 3(M+1)/(8 g₃)`. The tick history has counts `N₂` at `t₂ = (M+1)/(8 g₂)` and `N₃` at `t₃ = (M+1)/(8 g₃)`, with `\|N_i · 2π/R − t_i\| ≤ 2π/R` and `\|(N₃ − N₂) · 2π/R − (t₃ − t₂)\| ≤ 4π/R` | `pshift_twelfth_mem_displayArc_iff`, `clockTwoSiteTrainStart`, `clockTwoSiteFlow_train_fst`, `isSwing_clockTwoSiteFlow_train`, `echoSiteShown_clockTwoSiteFlow_train_iff`, `triThdShown_clockTwoSiteFlow_train_iff`, `clockTwoSite_records_train` | exact switching instants; count bounds of one and two periods; on a null set of fibres; one mode — the same occupancy at every site, so no signal travels |
| UD-103 | **Two symplectic arenas side by side.** For compact symplectic arenas `S`, `S'` and smooth `H`, `K`: the energy `H(a) + K(b)` on `S × S'` is smooth, its Hamiltonian vector field for `π₁^* α + π₂^* β` is the pair of the two fields, and its Hamiltonian flow is `(Φ_H^t a, Φ_K^t b)`. If each flow is measure preserving for `μ` and `ν`, the joint flow is for `μ ⊗ ν` | `prodEnergy`, `prodEnergy_eq`, `contMDiff_prodEnergy`, `isHamiltonianVectorField_prodEnergy`, `hamiltonianFlow_prodEnergy`, `measurePreserving_hamiltonianFlow_prodEnergy` (`LF4/ProductFlow.lean`) | exact; generic, no carrier and no record; the measure statement is conditional on each factor's |

From the messenger's start of UD-94 and the clock's train start of UD-62: there are flow times `τ_d ≤ 625π/3 ≤ τ_a ≤ 4375π/3` with the departure readout and the arrival readout, read on the messenger's factor along the joint orbit, each an `IsRecordWrite`, tick counts `N_d` at `τ_d` and `N_a` at `τ_a` of the clock's history read on the joint orbit, and `\|(N_a − N_d)(2π/R) − (τ_a − τ_d)\| ≤ 4π/R` | `IsSwing.prodMk_snd`, `clockMessengerEnergy`, `contMDiff_clockMessengerEnergy`, `clockMessengerFlow`, `clockMessengerFlow_apply`, `clockMessengerFlow_fst`, `clockMessengerFlow_snd`, `map_clockMessengerFlow`, `clockMessenger_departure_arrival_counts` (`RecordLayer/UnifiedClockMessenger.lean`) | the flow and the measure are exact; the run is an existence statement with the bounds of UD-94 and a two-tick bound; no coupling between clock and messenger; counts aligned by the flow parameter |

Exact scope of UD-95 and UD-96. What they give. The flows of §2 for one and two pointer tori were
each proved for their own arena. UD-95 is the construction they share, for any compact symplectic
arena: add a torus, couple it to a function of the base through its conserved angle, and the flow
is a skew product over the base's flows. UD-96 is that theorem applied to the two-torus arena.
So an arena exists on which a clock's pointer and two detectors' pointers can be under one
Hamiltonian flow, with the flow in closed form and its measure preserved, and a fourth pointer
would be one more application. What they do not give. The step adds one torus at a time. No arena
with a variable number of tori is defined, and each level has its own statement. The one-torus
flow of UD-1 is still its own proof and not an instance of UD-95. The coupling's form is UA-15's:
it is the hypothesis of the step, not a consequence. The measure statement at each level is read
off the closed form. There is no carrier, no coupled operator, no readout and no record in these
two modules, and no statement about a clock or a detector. By the test of §6: nothing here is a
statement about records, so the test does not apply; the entries are the dynamics that later
records must come from. UD-95 and UD-96 use UA-15, and through the two-torus arena UA-13. UA-2
enters only as invariance. UA-6 is not used.

Exact scope of UD-97 and UD-98. What they give. One flow now carries a clock's pointer and two
detectors' pointers, and one run has a readout history on each. "Record" in UD-98 means a
readout's switching instant: `false` on `[0, t_i]` and `true` on `(t_i, 3 t_i]`. No
`IsRecordWrite` or `ActualOccurrence` with propagator `clockTwoSiteFlow` is constructed, and no
lemma places the start in `triThdReady`. The clock's ticks are records by `IsSwing.isRecordWrite`
of UD-63 applied to this swing, which is not instantiated here. The clock ticks, each detector's readout
switches at an instant its own history fixes, and the clock's count is taken at both instants. The
interval between the two detector records, read in ticks, is the flow interval within two tick
periods. Those are two of the things a radar reading uses, a record's instant and a clock's count
at it, from one start state under one dynamics; the rest is listed below. What they do not give. No signal travels. The carrier is
one momentum mode, with the same occupancy at every site at all times. Each detector's pointer
turns at its own rate, and the two records are apart in time because the strengths `g₂` and `g₃`
differ. The interval is `(M+1)(1/g₃ − 1/g₂)/8`. It is not a transit time, it does not depend on
the two sites, and no distance or speed is read off it. So this is not a radar exchange, and by
the test of UO-16 nothing here is evidence about Lorentz kinematics. The instance is on the
fibres where all three profiles vanish, a null set, where the device moves freely and drives the
three pointers. Off those fibres only the reduction on the zeros of the third profile is proved,
and there is no cross-talk bound for three pointers. The counts are of the tick history up to
flow times (UA-7), held by no degree of freedom. The records are definite facts of the one
trajectory, and no weight of quantum mechanics is derived. What separates this from a one-way
signal is named: a travelling carrier in this arena needs the crossing bounds of UD-12's module
for the two-level device, whose levels have different rest energies and speeds, and the clock's
swing for a packet over the transit, which is condition (a) of UO-14. By the test of §6: no
hypothesis of UD-98 is a Register 1 entry other than the parameters, the arcs and the couplings;
no definition contains a write; the object is the flow of the joint energy; and the statements
fail for pointers that do not move. UD-97 and UD-98 use UA-15, UA-12, UA-8, UA-7 and the arcs,
profiles and strengths of UA-4. UA-2 enters only as invariance. UA-6 is not used.

Exact scope of UD-103 and UD-104 (BACKLOG ~~#372~~, the first brick of M6). What they give. The
clock is now a device that is not the messenger. One Hamiltonian flow, on the product arena of
UA-16, carries the messenger with its departure and arrival pointers and the clock with its tick
pointer. One orbit has the two record writes of UD-94 and the clock's swing, and the clock's
count is taken at a flow time by which each write is complete. So the clock's counts at those
two flow times differ, in tick periods, from the difference of the times by at most two periods.
What is new is UD-103: that the pair of the two flows is the Hamiltonian flow of one energy on
one arena is a theorem, by uniqueness of integral curves, and not a juxtaposition. What they do
not give. The run is a conjunction on a product: no conjunct ties the clock to the messenger.
The two-period bound holds for any two non-negative flow times of the clock's swing
(`IsSwing.abs_tickTime_sub_sub_le`); the messenger's dynamics enters only by naming the two
times. Those times are existential witnesses and are not located: `0 ≤ τ_d ≤ 625π/3 ≤ τ_a` is
all that is stated, so `τ_a − τ_d ≥ 0` and no more. No hypothesis relates the tick period
`2π/R` to that interval, so both counts may be zero. The ticks are counted (`IsTickCount`); they
are records by `IsSwing.isRecordWrite` of UD-61, which is not instantiated on this arena. On
arenas with stated detector laws, BACKLOG ~~#271~~ (`dirac_timed_signal`) already has a departure,
an arrival and the located ticks of a separate device in one evolution. There is no coupling: the clock's history does not depend on the messenger's state, and
the reverse (`clockMessengerFlow_snd`, `clockMessengerFlow_fst`). Nothing places the clock at the
departure region; by UA-16 (d) that is not a statement of this arena. The counts are aligned with
the writes by the flow parameter (UA-7): no degree of freedom holds a record together with a
count, and no record reads the count. The two writes do not certify a transit; the scope of
UD-93 and UD-94 stands, with the empty "before" clause of the departure. The clock is the exact
instance of UD-64: one momentum mode, `trainF`, the fibre `θ₂ = 0`, one start point, so no
measure of clock starts is stated. The bound is two ticks and compares a count with a difference
of flow times. No distance, no speed and no second observer appear, and nothing here is evidence
about Lorentz kinematics. By the test of §6: the hypotheses of UD-104 that are Register 1
entries are the arena and its additive energy (UA-16), the parameters, the arcs and the
couplings; no definition contains a write; the object is the flow of the joint energy; but the
conclusion about the interval is a comparison with the flow parameter that does not use the
messenger's dynamics at all, so it passes no gate. UD-103 uses no Register 1 entry. UD-104 uses
UA-16, UA-13, UA-12, UA-8, UA-7, UA-5 and the arcs, profiles and strengths of UA-4. UA-2 enters
as invariance only (`map_clockMessengerFlow`); the positive measure of the messenger's fibres is
UD-94's, and the joint start set of the run is null for `echoMuL ⊗ kMuL`, since the clock starts
at one point. UA-6 is not used.

Exact scope of UD-106 and UD-107 (BACKLOG ~~#374~~). What they give. The next brick of M6 was to be
a coupling of the clock to the departure detector, so that a count belongs to a record otherwise
than through the flow parameter. That coupling is not built. These two entries give what is
proved in its place. A readout is a region of a pointer's moving angle (UA-4), and the joint
energy of the arena of UA-16 contains no moving angle. So its flow commutes with turning the
three pointers (UD-107). With the carriers and the conserved angles held fixed, two starts that
differ only in where the departure pointer begins have the same clock history, the same
carriers and the same arrival pointer; nothing but a pointer's own angle depends on where that
pointer started. So no other degree of freedom tells two such runs apart, whether or not the
readout comes to be displayed in one and not in the other. Such a pair is not exhibited in
Lean. UD-106 is the same statement for one step of UD-95 and for the pointer that the step
adds. The statements are mathematically small: a moving angle is a cyclic coordinate, and each
is read off a closed form already in the corpus (UD-95, UD-104).
This bears on condition (b) of UO-14 and on "a count that belongs to a record" in UO-16; it
does not close either. What they do not give. (i) It is an obstruction for a form of the energy
and not for `Σ`. (ii) UD-107 is proved for `clockMessengerFlow` only. The same commutation is
expected on the other arenas of UA-13 and on the arena of UA-15, where no moving angle enters
the energy either; it is not stated in Lean for them. (iii) UD-106 covers only the pointer that
the step adds. A step whose `G` reads the moving angle of an earlier pointer is of the step form
and is not covered; that is UO-24. For an earlier pointer of a tower the statement needs every
later `G` to be independent of that pointer's moving angle (UA-13 (c), UA-15 (c)), and that
induction is not in Lean. (iv) A dependence through the occupancy that drives a pointer is not
excluded. A second degree of freedom driven by the same accumulated occupancy varies with the
readout through that common cause; what is excluded is a dependence on the pointer's starting
angle alone. So "no register can hold the count at a record" is not a consequence of these
entries. UD-65 excludes a measurable real function of the clock arena's state that holds the
count; an angle-valued register is excluded by neither. (v) A pointer whose moving angle acts
back (UO-24) is not ruled out; the corpus does not have one. (vi) A pointer whose rate depends
on the messenger and the clock together, such as the expectation of a product observable on a
composite carrier, is not ruled out. Its displacement is a joint functional of both histories.
It does not remove the obstruction: its pointer is again a cyclic coordinate, and it responds
to the carriers and not to a record. (vii) A term of the kind of BACKLOG ~~#212~~ between the
clock's carrier and a detector's conserved angle leaves every moving angle out of the energy,
so the same commutation is expected for it. That is a calculation and not in Lean: the energy
of ~~#212~~ has two terms in one conserved angle and is not a `stepEnergy`, so UD-106 does not
apply to it as stated. It is not built. (viii) UD-107 is stated for the uncoupled energy of
UD-104, where the clock's independence of the messenger is already UD-104's. By the test of
§6: the entries restate UA-13 (c), UA-15 (c) and UA-16 (c) ("no moving angle enters the
energy") as a symmetry of the flow, and say so; they are an obstruction and pass no gate.
UD-106 uses no assumption beyond its hypotheses; where it is applied, the form is UA-13 (c) or
UA-15 (c). UD-107 uses UA-16, UA-13, UA-12 and UA-5 (UA-8 through the carriers' generators).
Reading UD-107 as a statement about readouts uses UA-4. No measure enters. UA-6 is not used.

Exact scope of UD-108 and UD-109 (BACKLOG ~~#375~~). What they give. UD-105 bounded only
accumulated occupancies, from a packet whose pointwise bounds are `1/4` and `3/4`. UD-108 replaces
the packet by a family indexed by `n`, with the bound of BACKLOG ~~#265~~ (the translation phase
locates the weight) made as small as wanted. UD-109 states the result at each flow time for the
carrier coupled to the two region pointers of UA-13: at most `1/(2n)` on `[0, (625π/3) n²]`,
at least `1 − 1/(2n)` on `[(3125π/3) n², (4375π/3) n²]`, and nothing between the two intervals
or after the second (the packet goes on round the ring). For `n ≤ 2` these are no sharper than
the `1/4` and `3/4` of BACKLOG ~~#265~~. What they do not give. (i) It is not the first sharpened packet: BACKLOG ~~#268~~ has one
instance, for the free packet and a test detector. (ii) The bound is bought with a larger
system: the ring's length grows as `n²`, the number of sites as `n³`, the number of modes as
`n`, the flow times as `n²`; the hop changes with `n` as well, so it is a family of generators.
(iii) The allowed back-reaction shrinks as `n⁻³`; nothing is
proved off the fine fibres. (iv) For the coupled carrier the window between "before" and
"after" is the old one scaled by `n²`; the margin `δ` is narrowed for the free packet only,
and in angle only: the unlocated window in flow time is `2500 n² δ`, which grows as `δ → 0`
with the `n` the proof supplies. No window in flow time is shown to shrink.
(v) They are statements about the carrier's occupancy. No pointer inequality and no
`IsRecordWrite` is stated, and the write times of UD-94 are not relocated. (vi) Every time is
a flow time (UA-7). (vii) Little weight, not none: no bound vanishes outside a cone. By the
test of §6: the hypotheses that carry the conclusion are the generator (UA-8), the drift
coupling (UA-5) and the two-pointer energy (UA-13); the entries are estimates for that
dynamics and pass no gate. The back-reaction estimate is that of BACKLOG ~~#357~~ and
~~#367~~, composed. UD-108 uses UA-8 and UA-7. UD-109 uses UA-5, UA-8, UA-13, UA-7
and the profiles and strengths of UA-4; UA-2 enters only through the measure on the pair of
conserved angles (UA-13 (b)). UA-6 is not used.

Exact scope of UD-110 to UD-112 (BACKLOG ~~#376~~). What they give. The arena of UA-17 and its
flow in closed form where that is exact, and the first pointer on the unified arenas whose rate
depends on the messenger and the clock together: it is the product of the messenger's occupancy
of a region and the clock's reading. So, within the bounds of UD-112, the pointer's
displacement is close to that of the clock's pointer of UD-58 on its own arena while the
messenger is in the region, and on the later interval it moves at a rate at most
`|k'||g_c|/n`.
What they do not give. (i) Only the fibres where the gated pointer's profile vanishes, a null
set for `trainF`, and only product starts, a null set of the base (two calculations, not in
Lean). Off them the product structure is not shown to persist; that the product term
entangles the messenger with the clock is expected and not in Lean; nothing is proved there.
(ii) The gate is the messenger's occupancy, an expectation. No readout of the departure
pointer enters. The gated pointer is a cyclic coordinate by UD-106, which is generic and
covers the pointer that the last step adds; that is not instantiated for `gateFlow`, and
UD-107 is for the arena of UA-16 and is not restated here. The dependence on the messenger is
shown along one run; no theorem compares two messenger starts. The comparison with the
clock's pointer is across arenas: the clock has no pointer of its own on this one. (iii) No ready arc, threshold, `IsSwing`, `IsTickCount` or
`IsRecordWrite` is stated on this arena. That the gated pointer's swings are tick records
whose count stops at departure is not proved. (iv) The bounds on the family of UD-108 grow
with the system: `|k'||g_c| t/n` with `t` up to `(625π/3) n²`. Against the swing of the free
pointer, of order `|k'||g_c|/R` for a clock of rate `R`, that is small only when `n R` is
small; many swings before departure with a small error needs `1/n² ≪ R ≪ 1/n`. That is a
calculation and not in Lean. (v) Nothing is stated between the two intervals or after the
second. (vi) The clock is not placed: UA-17 (d). (vii) Every time is a flow time (UA-7). By
the test of §6: UD-110 is matrix algebra with no assumption in it. For UD-111 and UD-112 the
hypotheses that carry the conclusion are the product term of UA-17 (c) and the vanishing of
the third profile; the form of the pointer's rate is read off the energy, and what is derived
is that the product structure persists on those fibres and the size of the two errors. They
pass no gate: there is no record in them. UD-111 and UD-112 use UA-17, UA-15, UA-13, UA-12,
UA-8, UA-7 and UA-5, with the profiles and strengths of UA-4; UA-2 enters through `triMuL`.
UA-6 is not used. Earlier gates: BACKLOG ~~#274~~ has a gate by a register under a stated
hybrid law; here the gate is a term of one joint energy.

Exact scope of UD-113 and UD-114 (BACKLOG ~~#377~~). What they give. The first tick records and
the first tick count on the arena of UA-17, from a pointer whose swing depends on where the
messenger is: while the messenger's occupancy of the near half is within `1/(2n)` of `1`, the
gated pointer's displacement is within `|g_c| T/n` of the swing of UD-62, and the swing
criterion of UD-62, the count and the record write of UD-61 and the count-time bound of UD-92
apply to it. The ticks are `IsRecordWrite`s of the third pointer's readout history; they are
not packaged as `ActualOccurrence`s of `gateClockFlow` (UD-64's form is not instantiated on
this arena). After the transit, on the stated
interval, no passage from ready to displayed lies inside a sub-interval `[a, b]` with
`|k'||g_c| (b − a)/n ≤ 1/6`; for the whole interval that needs `|k'||g_c| n ≤ 1/(2500π)`,
which no theorem assumes. What they do not give. (i) That the count stops at departure. Nothing is stated
between `(625π/3) n²` and `(3125π/3) n²`, where the pointer may tick, or after
`(4375π/3) n²`; a tick begun before `(3125π/3) n²` may complete inside the interval; and no
theorem gives the count at a time after departure. (ii) A register: no degree of freedom
holds the count (UO-14 (b)). (iii) Many ticks. The hypothesis `|g_c| T/n < 1/12` bounds the
rate from above, and the count is within one of `R T/2π`. That both hold with any number of
ticks, for large `n`, is UD-116 and not these entries; for small `T` the statements hold
with count zero. (iv) A gate by a record: item (ii) of the scope of UD-110 to UD-112 stands. (v) A positive measure of fibres or
starts: UD-111. (vi) The clock's own pointer on this arena: that it goes on ticking is UD-62
on the clock's arena. (vii) Every time is a flow time (UA-7). By the test of §6: the swing is
UD-62's with the error of UD-112, the arcs are apparatus input (UA-4) and the amplitude `1/4`
is tuned by the strength; what is derived is
that the error stays inside the arcs' margins up to `T`. They pass no gate: the count is not
tied to a departure record. UD-113 and UD-114 use the assumptions of UD-111 and UD-112, and
UA-4 for the arcs. UA-6 is not used.

Exact scope of UD-115 to UD-119 (BACKLOG ~~#378~~, ~~#379~~). What they give. UD-116 removes the
vacuity left in UD-113: for a given clock and a given `K` its two hypotheses are lower bounds
on `n`, so the gated pointer has `K` ticks before the messenger leaves for every large `n`.
UD-115 bounds the count at any later time from above. UD-117 to UD-119 follow the messenger
once round its ring at any margin `δ`: out of the far half, inside it, and out of it again.
With `E = 2|k'||g_c| fineEta n δ`: on the first interval the gated pointer's displacement is
within `E t` of that of the pointer of UD-58 on the clock's own arena; on the second it
changes at a rate at most `E`; on the third its advance is within `E (t₂ − t₁)` of that
pointer's advance. That is a return of the messenger to the gate of UA-17. Earlier returns:
BACKLOG ~~#200~~ and ~~#202~~ (the echo arena, with records and a radar reading), ~~#270~~ (a
reflection on the Dirac chain, under the stated detector law of UA-10), ~~#274~~ and ~~#278~~
(a relay under a stated hybrid law); none is on a joint flow with the clock, and this one has
no record. What they do
not give. (i) A count at arrival or at return. The bound of UD-115 is that of a pointer moving
at its largest rate, about `3R(T − s)/(4κ)` ticks where the clock makes about `R(T − s)/(2π)`;
it does not fix the count through a gap. (ii) Ticks on the return. The swing of UD-113 is read
from the flow time `0`; after the return the gated pointer starts from an angle that no
theorem of these entries locates, and no swing, tick, count or record is stated there. So no radar time is formed from counts.
(iii) A count that stops, or a count at departure: `HasTicks` in UD-116 is a lower bound, at
a flow time `2πK/R` that is a vanishing fraction of `[0, (625π/3) n²]` for a given clock as `n`
grows; the count at `(625π/3) n²` is given by no theorem unless `|g_c| n < 1/(2500π)`.
(iv) Small errors: `fineEta n δ` is of order `1/(n δ²)`, but each bound is `E` times the
length of its interval, and the intervals have length of order `n²`. Over a whole interval
the bound is of order `|g_c| n/δ²`: as in item (iv) of the scope of UD-110 to UD-112, small
against the swing only when `|g_c| n/δ²` is small, that is for a clock whose rate falls with
`n`. A calculation, not in Lean. The gaps have length `2500 n² δ` in flow time. (v) A second
observer, a distance or a speed. One messenger, one clock, one round of one ring; the angle
`t/(1250 n²)` is the predicted one of UD-108. (vi) The limits of UD-111: a null set of fibres
for the gated pointer and product starts (calculations, not in Lean); the comparison with the
pointer of UD-58 is across arenas. (vii) Every time is a flow time (UA-7). By the test of §6:
the hypotheses that carry the conclusions are those of UD-111 to UD-113 (the product term of
UA-17 (c), the vanishing third profile, the strength tuned by `hg`, the arcs of UA-4). What
is derived is that the two lower bounds on `n` suffice, the travel bound, and the sizes of the
errors over the longer horizon. They pass no gate. UD-117 uses UA-8 and
UA-7. UD-118 uses in addition UA-5, UA-13 and UA-4, and UA-2 through the measure on the pair
of conserved angles. UD-115, UD-116 and UD-119 use in addition UA-17, UA-15 and UA-12. UA-6
is not used.

Exact scope of UD-120 and UD-121 (BACKLOG ~~#380~~). What they give. The three bounds of UD-119
joined across the two gaps, for the clock of UD-113: on the return leg the gated pointer's
displacement is the swing of UD-113 plus an offset, a quarter of the difference of the cosines
of the clock's phase at the two flow times `T_b` and `T_a` (predicted angles; no record
locates them). The offset is there because the
gated pointer's rate is the product of the messenger's occupancy and the clock's reading:
while the messenger is away the pointer's rate is at most `2|g_c| fineEta n δ` (little over
the interval only when `|g_c| n/δ²` is small) and the clock's phase moves on. Read through the
fixed arcs and the start of UD-113, whether the gated pointer ticks on the return leg depends
on the offset: in one stated range (not shown non-empty in Lean) it is never ready there
(`gateClock_return_not_ready`); when the offset is small it is a swing again
(`isSwing_gateClock_return`). No theorem gives a count across the absence. What they do not
give. (i) A radar time: no theorem here gives a count through the absence or through the
gaps, and the return count is read from a chosen flow time. (ii) Satisfiability of either
case. The error is of order `|g_c| (n/δ² + n² δ)`; that it is below `1/12` with an offset in
the stated range, for some clock, index and margin, is a calculation and not in Lean. It
needs a clock whose rate is of order `1/n²`: large enough for a whole turn to lie on the
return leg or for the offset to reach `1/12`, and small against `δ²/n` and `1/(n² δ)`. Nor is
a whole number `j` of turns with `2πj/R` on the return leg shown to exist. (iii) The other
values of the offset. (iv) A statement about `Σ`, or about the coupling alone. The
obstruction is for the product coupling of UA-17 (c) with the drift law of UA-5 read through
the fixed ready and display arcs and the start `1/12` (UA-4). In the never-ready case the
pointer still swings, and arcs placed by the offset would read ticks. Another coupling, or
such arcs, may behave otherwise; no theorem here shows that no fixed arcs work for every
absence. (v) A record that marks the start of the return swing: the
flow time `2πj/R` is chosen, not given by a record. (vi) The limits of UD-111, UD-113 and
UD-118 stand: a fibre where the gated pointer's profile vanishes and a product start (null
sets by calculations not in Lean), the round fibres, one clock start; the gate is an
occupancy; the ticks are `IsSwing` and `IsTickCount` statements about a readout history, not
`ActualOccurrence`s. (vii) Every time is a flow time (UA-7). By the test of §6: the
hypotheses that carry the conclusions are those of UD-111 to UD-113, the round fibres of
UD-118, the arcs and start of UA-4, and the two conditions on the offset; what is derived is the offset's form and the two consequences. They pass no gate.
UD-120 and UD-121 use the assumptions of UD-113 and UD-119. UA-6 is not used.

Exact scope of UD-122 to UD-124 (BACKLOG ~~#381~~). What they give. A clock pointer that runs
throughout, on the same arena and in the same run as the gated pointer and the two region
pointers: by the amendment (e) of UA-17 the clock has a pointer of its own, and where neither
clock pointer acts back its displacement is exactly that of UD-58's pointer (an equality of
real displacement functions across arenas), so the swing, the ticks and the count of UD-61 and
UD-62 hold for it at every horizon. So one history has two counts. On a stretch `[a, b]` of
the interval of UD-109 on which the far half's occupancy is at least `1 − 1/(2n)`, the
clock's count advances with the flow time within two periods (UD-92, which holds for any two
non-negative flow times) and the gated count by 0 or 1, so the difference of the two advances
is within three periods of `b − a`. The time content is the clock's alone; no hypothesis
relates `2π/R` to `b − a`, and all four advances may be zero. Not new on other arenas: a
clock pointer that swings at every horizon beside other pointers in one run is UD-98 (BACKLOG
~~#369~~, the same clock carrier) and UD-104 (~~#372~~, the uncoupled product arena); what is
new here is that the clock is a tensor factor of the composite carrier, in the run that also
carries the pointer coupled to `P ⊗ X`. What they
do not give. (i) A radar time. `a` and `b` are flow times inside the absence, not a departure
and a return, and no record marks them. Over the two transit windows the gated count is
bounded only by UD-115, too weakly, and on the return leg it depends on the offset of UD-120.
(ii) A ticking gated pointer. `g_c` is a free real in these entries; only `g₀` is tuned. The
gated pointer is the swing of UD-113 only when `g_c` also has the tuned value, and that swing
is not restated on this arena; for `g_c` far below it the gated pointer is not shown to tick
at all and the gated count may be zero throughout. With `g_c` tuned, the hypothesis
`|g_c| (b − a)/n ≤ 1/6` allows a number of periods of order `n`. A calculation, not in Lean. (iii) A count held by a degree of freedom, or read by a
record: UO-14 (b). Both counts are read at flow times. (iv) Fibres or starts of positive
measure: both clock pointers' profiles must vanish on the fibre, and the start is a product
ray (null sets by calculations not in Lean). (v) A gate by a record. (vi) Every time is a
flow time (UA-7). By the test of §6: the fourth pointer is added by assumption (UA-17 (e)),
and that it is a swing is UD-62 carried to this arena by the product structure of UD-111;
the gated bound is UD-114. What is derived is that the two hold in one run and what their
difference reads. They pass no gate: neither count is tied to a record. UD-122 uses UA-15
and, through the lower arenas, UA-13; UA-2 enters only as invariance.
UD-123 and UD-124 use UA-17, UA-15, UA-13, UA-12, UA-8, UA-7, UA-5 and UA-4; UA-2 enters
through `quadMuL` and, for the fine fibres, through the measure on the pair of conserved
angles. UA-6 is not used.

Exact scope of UD-125 and UD-126 (BACKLOG ~~#382~~). What they give. A record written on the
return leg of the round trip of UD-118, in the run that carries the clock's own pointer. The
first pointer is coupled to the near half; its strength is chosen so that everything
accumulated before the return leg begins does not carry it into its displayed arc, and the
first presence and the return together do. Its readout does not display before the return leg
begins and displays through the window `1/(3κ)` after a flow time `τ` on the return leg, the
end of the stated write interval. The clock's own pointer has a count `N` at `τ`, with
`N · 2π/R` within one period of `τ`; `N` may be zero, and no hypothesis relates the period to
the length of the return leg. What they do not give. (i) A radar time from two records. The start
is a prepared one; nothing records an emission, and this pointer is not the departure record
of UD-94. (ii) A certified return. The write is a threshold of accumulated occupancy; a
carrier with a suitable constant near-half occupancy would give a readout with the same two
clauses (a calculation, not in Lean; such a carrier is not exhibited on this arena). What
shows the round trip is UD-118, which is not a record. (iii) A sharp time. `τ` is located only within the return leg, and it is not the
instant the readout turns. The `τ` the crossing lemma gives is where the displacement reaches
`1/3`; under the rate hypotheses that is more than half the return leg after its start (a
calculation, not in Lean; the theorem states only that `τ` is on the return leg), and the
readout itself may turn earlier. The time content of the count is the clock's alone
(UD-92): the bound holds at every non-negative flow time. (iv) A count read by the record, or
held by a degree of freedom: UO-14 (b). (v) A reading of a distance. The messenger comes back
because the ring closes; the flow time of the return is fixed by the ring's length and the
packet's velocity (UA-8). (vi) The limits of UD-123: both clock pointers' profiles vanish on
the fibre, the start is a product ray (null sets by calculations not in Lean), one clock
start; the gated pointer is present with a free strength, is not shown to tick and plays no
part; the write and the count are `IsRecordWrite` and `IsTickCount` statements about readout
histories, not `ActualOccurrence`s. (vii) That the
two rate hypotheses hold for some `n` in Lean: they are compatible when `δ ≤ π/48` and
`fineEta n δ ≤ 1/48` (`two_roundUpper_le_roundLower`), and that some `n` gives the latter is
not stated. Earlier readings: BACKLOG ~~#360~~ and ~~#366~~ have a clock's count at a
position write, with the flow time shown to be the switching instant, and ~~#369~~ counts at two
