Exact scope of UD-210 (BACKLOG #296, second part; milestone M7, obligation UO-17; Task 8 of
the plan of 2026-11-18). What it gives. The quantity a coincidence gate reads, the weight in
a region of sites, bounded by the `d` translation phases of UD-209: for any state, and for
the packet in a box that follows the group velocity, with the time entering only through
`|t|` times each axis's drift error. What it does not give. Any number. The starting term
`⟨ψ₀, ψ₀⟩ − Re(ū_i T_i(0))` and the drift error are sums over the window that are not
evaluated, so no window is shown to meet a gate's budget; that is the limit in physical
units, which stays open. The sides of the box are assumed to keep outside positions at
`Re ≤ γ_i` and are not built. The bound is a sum over the axes. Nothing for `μ = 0`. No new
assumption.

Exact scope of UD-211 (BACKLOG #437, first brick; obligation UO-26). What it gives. The half
of a caused return that holds for every mechanism: if the return is launched by a term `V`
of the carriers' energy, then up to the time `t` the part of the state outside the free
orbit is at most the time integral of `‖V e^{−isH₀} X‖`, so nothing is launched before the
free orbit has weight where `V` acts. What it does not give. That a return is launched, or
how much: the first-order term with a second-order remainder is the next theorem. A choice
of `V`. Any statement on the arena: off the fibres where the profiles vanish the pointers
act back on the carriers, and nothing here treats that. No new assumption.

Exact scope of UD-212 (BACKLOG #437, second brick; obligation UO-26, assumption UA-19).
What it gives. The form of what a term `V` of the energy launches: at each time `s` it acts
on the free orbit, and the result is carried on freely; the perturbed orbit is the free one
plus that, within `‖V‖² ‖X‖ t² / 2`. With UD-211 this is items (1) and (2) of UO-26 as
statements about any `V`. What it does not give. Any launch. The integral is not evaluated
for the contact term of UA-19 (b), so its weight, and where and when it is launched, are
not known. A remainder that is small because the free orbit avoids `V`. Anything beyond
first order. Anything on the arena. No new assumption beyond UA-19, which this row does
not use.

Exact scope of UD-213 (BACKLOG #438, first brick; obligation UO-25 (2), Task 2 of the plan of
2026-11-18). What it gives. The step that was missing between "the events contain the start
for one ray" and "for a set of rays": the histories that the counts are read from are stable
when the ray is turned by a unitary near the identity, with an explicit rate. What it does
not give. Positive measure of anything. The family's five histories at the turned rays
(their slack has to be read from the family's proof), the counts at each of them, the
Fubini–Study measure of the turned rays, and the `HistoryPreparation` are owed. A turned
ray is not a product of the three carriers' rays, and the product rays are themselves a
null set, so the counts have to come from the general-ray theorem UD-199
(`penta_radar_lorentz_fibre`) and not from the three-carrier open theorem of UD-205.
Positive measure in the pointer fibres is not positive measure in the arena, and nothing
here changes that. No new assumption.

Exact scope of UD-214 (BACKLOG #438, second brick; obligation UO-25 (2)). What it gives. The
counts and the Lorentz statement on a set of starts that is a product: rays turned by
unitaries within `r_W` of the identity, times a box of pointer angles, for any carrier whose
five displacements have the histories at the unturned ray. The turned rays are not required
to be products of carriers' rays. What it does not give. The family's instance: the five
histories of UD-196 with a named slack have to be stated as a theorem first. The measure of
the set of turned rays. The `HistoryPreparation`. Positive measure in the arena is still
not shown. No new assumption.

Exact scope of UD-215 (BACKLOG #438, third brick; obligation UO-25 (2)). What it gives. The
set of rays on which UD-214 gives the counts is not a null set: positive Fubini–Study
measure for the turned rays of any radius. With UD-214 this is the two halves of "positive
measure in the arena" for any carrier whose histories are given. What it does not give. The
family's instance, which still needs its five histories as a statement. The product with
the box of angles under `μ_FS ⊗ vol⁵` and the transport by the labelling of the basis. The
`HistoryPreparation`. A value for the measure. No new assumption.

Exact scope of UD-216 (BACKLOG #437, third brick; obligation UO-26, assumption UA-19).
What it gives. The remainder of UD-212 in a form that is small before the meeting: it is at
most `‖V‖ t` times the accumulated action of `V` on the free orbit, which is the quantity
UD-211 bounds the whole deviation by. So up to the meeting nothing is launched and the
first-order description is exact to that order, for any `V`. What it does not give. The
first-order term for the contact term of UA-19 (b): its weight, and where and when it is
launched. Anything for `‖V‖ t ≥ 1`. Anything on the arena. No new assumption.

Exact scope of UD-217 (BACKLOG #296, third part; milestone M7, obligation UO-17). What it
gives. The region a coincidence gate on the torus would use, as a named set of sites: a box
of arcs, one for each axis, about the packet's directions, moving at the group velocity;
and UD-210's bounds for it without a hypothesis on the sides. What it does not give. Any
number: the starting term and the drift error are not evaluated. That an arc is a run of
consecutive sites, or its length. A gate. No new assumption.

Exact scope of UD-218 (BACKLOG #438, fourth brick; obligation UO-25 (2)). What it gives. The
plan's M6 statement for the family on a product set of starts that is not null on the ray
side: every ray within `1/(12000 k²)` of the family's (in the sense of UD-215, which has
positive Fubini–Study measure) times a box of pointer angles. No hypothesis on histories.
What it does not give. Positive `μ_FS ⊗ vol⁵` measure of a set in the arena: the transport
by the labelling of the basis and the product with the box are owed. The five record
events of UD-208 containing that set, which needs the strengths of this theorem and of
UD-208 to be the same or UD-208 restated for these. The `HistoryPreparation`. No new
assumption.

Exact scope of UD-219 (BACKLOG ~~#438~~; obligation UO-25 (2), Task 2 of the plan of
2026-11-18). What it gives. A record history of the unified arena, in the SigmaLayer's
semantics, whose compatible region has non-zero measure for the arena's own measure
`μ_FS ⊗ vol⁵`, with the carrier's ray varied over a neighbourhood: so the existing
`HistoryPreparation` and its conditional measure apply to the radar's records without a new
definition. The counts of that history satisfy the Lorentz statement. What it does not
give. The counts by name, or a value for the measure. A fibre measure: none is used, and
positive measure in a pointer fibre was not taken for positive measure in the arena. A
register, or a second apparatus that reads the record (UO-25 (5)). Any statement that
conditioning on the history is an interaction. The Born weights for these events (UO-25
(6)). No new assumption.

Exact scope of UD-220 (BACKLOG #296, fourth part; milestone M7, obligation UO-17). What it
gives. The quantities that the bounds of UD-210 and UD-217 are sums of, for any Clifford
family and with no choice of matrices: the norm of each mode's spinor and the real part of
the overlap of two, with an alignment bound of second order in the momentum step. What it
does not give. A number for a packet: the modulus of the overlap, the normalised amplitudes
and the sums over a window are owed, so the bound of UD-217 is still not evaluated. The
imaginary part of the overlap, which for two or more axes displaces the packet's phase at
first order; the bounds built on the real part are valid without it and not sharp. No new
assumption.

Exact scope of UD-221 (BACKLOG #296, fifth part; milestone M7, obligation UO-17; Task 8 of
the plan of 2026-11-18). What it gives. The first statement in `d` dimensions in which
something is shown small: a flat packet on a box of momenta has all but
`Σ_i (1/N_i + η + |t| (2π/L) Sm_i) / (1 − γ_i)` of its weight in a named box of sites that
moves at the group velocity, for any Clifford family. This is the localisation a coincidence
gate on the torus needs as an input. What it does not give. The energy-step errors `Sm_i`
from the window's width, and with them the drift term in physical units with errors free of
`L` (the alignment defect is shown free of `L`; the drift term is not). A sharp bound. A
massless carrier. The two-level clock on a packet. A gate, a record, or anything on the
arena. No new assumption.

Exact scope of UD-222 (BACKLOG #296, sixth part; milestone M7, obligation UO-17). What it
gives. With UD-221: a flat packet on a box of momenta has all but
`Σ_i (1/N_i + η + |t| r_i) / (1 − γ_i)` of its weight in its moving box, with `η` and the
rate `r_i` written in physical units and shown to have no `L` in them beyond a term of order
`1/L`. This is the `d`-dimensional counterpart of the ring's packet limit, for any Clifford
family. What it does not give. A family of lattices with a limit theorem (`Tendsto`). An
optimised window. A sharp bound, or the displacement of the box by the imaginary part of the
overlap. A massless carrier. The two-level clock on a packet. No new assumption.

Exact scope of UD-223 (BACKLOG #439, first brick; obligation UO-25 (3), Task 3 of the plan of
2026-11-18). What it gives. The existing frame of ~~#99~~ applied to tick records with no
new definition: a macroscopic coordinate whose cells are the record histories, so that
"compatible with this history" and "in this macrostate" are the same set. What it does not
give. Stability: the cells are fixed by the flow up to the reading times and nothing shows
they persist or are large. The radar's instance and the weight of its cell. The Born
weights of these events, which is UO-25 (6) and is not the weight of a `globalBasin`. No new
assumption.

Exact scope of UD-224 (BACKLOG #437, fourth brick; obligation UO-26 (1), assumption UA-19).
What it gives. The first of UO-26's items for the structure UA-19 names: a carrier with two
levels whose free Hamiltonian does not mix them, prepared in the first, is found in the
second only as far as the coupling has acted on the free orbit. The departure is therefore
bounded by the flow and not placed by the preparation. What it does not give. Item (2): a
launch, its weight, its place and its time. The contact term itself in place of `G`, and
the integrand as the free orbit's weight where the two carriers coincide. Anything on the
arena. UA-19 is used only for its structure (a), (b); no further assumption is added.

Exact scope of UD-225 (BACKLOG #439, second brick; obligation UO-25 (3)). What it gives. The
radar's records in the frame of ~~#99~~ with content: one cell of the macroscopic projection
of the five readings is the compatible region of the radar's history, it is not a null set
of the arena, and its counts obey the Lorentz comparisons. What it does not give. Which
cell. Stability of the cell: nothing says a later reading of the same pointers returns the
same string, and that is the part of Task 3 that is open. A law for the pushed-forward
measure. No new assumption.

Exact scope of UD-226 (BACKLOG #437, fifth brick; obligation UO-26 (1), assumption UA-19).
What it gives. Item (1) of UO-26 for the contact term that UA-19 (b) names, not for a
generic coupling: the transponder's second level is populated only as far as the free orbit
has amplitude where the two carriers are within the gate's relation. What it does not give.
That amplitude as the coincidence weight of two packets, or a bound for it from the
packets' boxes (UD-221, UD-222 give the boxes). Any launch. Anything on the arena. No
assumption beyond UA-19.

Exact scope of UD-227 (BACKLOG #440, first brick; obligation UO-25 (5), Task 5 of the plan of
2026-11-18; UO-24). What it gives. The answer, for the arena as frozen, to "can a record
control a second apparatus": no. A record there is a pointer's moving angle, and nothing
else depends on it. What it does not give. A record that does control an apparatus: that
needs a pointer whose moving angle is in the energy (UO-24), which is a term of a new kind.
A carrier-mediated succession, in which a second pointer reads a carrier that the first
interaction changed, is a different statement and is planned separately; it is not record
control. No new assumption.

Exact scope of UD-228 (BACKLOG #439, third brick; obligation UO-25 (3)). What it gives. The
reason a finite cap exists for the macroscopic projection of UD-223: a count is bounded by
the duration of the run times the pointer's speed over the gap between its arcs. What it
does not give. The cap for the radar: the pointer's speed bound (`|z| ‖G‖`, from the flow's
formula) and the arcs' gap in the circle's metric are owed. A lower bound for the count.
No new assumption.

Exact scope of UD-229 (BACKLOG #441, first brick; obligation UO-25 (6), Task 6 of the plan of
2026-11-18). What it gives. For tick records the statistic that is linear in the pointer's
displacement: the mean count over the starting angle is the displacement less one gap, with
no saturation. This is a different statement from the click law of UD-50, which saturates,
and it is the first candidate for a Born bridge that is not a cell law. What it does not
give. A bridge: the displacement has to be identified with the accumulated expectation of
a positive observable along the orbit, and that with a weight, and neither is done. The
distribution of the count. The average against `μ_FS ⊗ vol⁵`. Anything for a pointer that
swings. Whether the mean count's linearity is what Posit 1 asks for is not claimed; the
cell law's obligation UO-20 is unchanged. No new assumption.

Exact scope of UD-230 (BACKLOG #297, first brick; Task 9 of the plan of 2026-11-18, items 1
and 2 of the task plan's §7). What it gives. The support-growth argument of
`RecordLayer/ChainCone.lean` and `RecordLayer/UnifiedCausalOrder.lean` with the ring removed:
it needs a site map and an adjacency relation, and nothing else. The torus of any dimension
is an instance, with the lattice Dirac Hamiltonian of `CV/DiracLatticeD.lean` as the
generator. What it does not give. A detector or a record on the torus (items 3 and 4 of §7).
A lower bound: nothing says an influence arrives. A cone speed: the rate is `2 ‖H‖` and is
not computed. An order of events or a metric: the graph is the generator's own interaction
topology restated, as UO-15's test says. The ring's theorems keep their own proofs;
`HopsOne.hopsG` and `coe_growIter` show they are the ring's case. No new assumption.

Exact scope of UD-231 (BACKLOG #297, second brick; Task 9 of the plan of 2026-11-18, items 3
and 4 of the task plan's §7, each in part). What it gives. For a detector placed on any set
of sites of any graph, a bound on its pointer before the growth of its sites reaches where
the carrier started, on every fibre and with the detector's own coupling in the generator;
and the condition under which it displays. The torus of any dimension with the lattice Dirac
carrier is an instance of the first. What it does not give. A display on the torus: the
occupancy of a box of sites by a packet (`CV/DiracPacketWindowD.lean`) is a statement about a
vector, and it is not joined here to the arena's observable along the orbit. A tick record:
the readout is read at one time and no `TickReadout` is built, so the detector is not in a
record history and Task 9's acceptance (a record that is a `RecordedFact`) is not met. A time
of arrival: the bound is a factorial tail at the rate `2 ‖H‖`. No new assumption: the
coupling is UA-5's form on another carrier.

## 5. Register 3 — open obligations

Each entry: the obligation, why the assumptions do not guarantee it, and the repackaging test.
Proposed module names are the programme's. `DiracPointerWrite.lean` exists since BACKLOG
~~#351~~ (M2 part A); M2 part B is in `DiracPointerThreshold.lean` (~~#352~~) and M2 part C in
`DiracPointerRecord.lean` (~~#353~~), two modules the programme did not name.
`DiracPointerBorn.lean` exists since ~~#354~~ (M3 part A). M3 part B is in
`DiracPointerClick.lean` (~~#355~~), a module the programme did not name, and M3 part C in
`DiracPointerBasin.lean` (~~#356~~), another. `UnifiedDiracClock.lean` exists since ~~#357~~
(M4 part A). M4 part B is in `UnifiedDiracClockTicks.lean` (~~#358~~), a module the programme did
not name. `UnifiedTwoPointer.lean` (~~#359~~) is another: it meets no obligation below and builds the
arena that UO-10, UO-13, UO-14 (d) and UO-15 are about. `UnifiedClockSiteRecords.lean` (~~#360~~)
is another: it reads the two records of UO-14 (d) off that arena. `UnifiedCausalOrder.lean` exists
since ~~#361~~ (M5's bound; UO-15 stays, with conditions). `UnifiedCausalArrival.lean`
(~~#362~~) is a module the programme did not name: a response that is not zero, at distance one.
`UnifiedSiteRecord.lean` (~~#363~~) is another: the position record of UO-14 (d) made to depend
on the carrier's site. `UnifiedTwoSiteRecords.lean` (~~#364~~) is another: readouts on the arena
of two site detectors, and UO-10 with conditions for a carrier on one detector's site.
`UnifiedArrivalRecord.lean` (~~#365~~) is another: a reception record, one ingredient of UO-16.
`UnifiedCountAtWrite.lean` (~~#366~~) is another: the clock's count at a position write.
`UnifiedDepartureArrival.lean` (~~#367~~) is another: a departure and an arrival record in one run.
`LF4/PointerStep.lean` and `LF4/TriCoupledFlow.lean` (~~#368~~) are two more: the arena with three
pointer tori. They meet no obligation. `UnifiedClockTwoSites.lean` (~~#369~~) is another: a clock
and two site detectors on that arena, with a swing and two readout switching instants in one
run. `UnifiedSplitCarrier.lean` (~~#370~~) is another: UO-10 for a split carrier, answered as an
obstruction. `UnifiedCellSelection.lean` (~~#371~~) is the selection by site cells and the no-go
for a readout local to its site.
The others do not exist.

### M2 — a record generated by the flow (`DiracPointerWrite.lean`)

The programme separates four statements. They stay separate here.

* **UO-1 — readout region and ready state (definitions).** A region of the pointer circle and a ready
  value of `θ₁` for the pointer of `Σ_*`. *Not a result:* these are apparatus parameters (UA-4).
  *Test:* a region whose definition mentions the carrier's prepared state, or its weights, is not
  an apparatus parameter.
  ▸ **Met 2026-11-09 (BACKLOG ~~#352~~) → UA-4 item 2 and UD-35.** `diracReady` and
  `diracDisplayed` are conditions on the pointer angle `θ₁` and on nothing else, which is what
  `mem_diracReady_congr` and `diracDisplayed_congr` check. They remain chosen: the arcs `(0, 1/6]`
  and `(1/3, 5/6]` are not derived from anything.
* **UO-2 — displacement created.** A lower bound on `|diracPointerShift|` over a stated interval for
  an incident packet. *Not guaranteed:* the shift is `−2 g f'(θ₂)` times the accumulated occupancy
  `∫ ⟨P_j⟩` along the **coupled** carrier, generated by `H_D + g f(θ₂) P_j`, not along the free one.
  The static potential can reflect the packet, and there is no closed form on the ring (UD-8 had one
  only because the two-site exchange is solvable). *Test:* a bound that assumes the occupancy
  integral, or assumes the free packet's trajectory, has not used the flow.
  ▸ **Met 2026-11-09 (BACKLOG ~~#351~~) → UD-31, UD-32, UD-34, with two conditions that stay
  here.** How the test is passed: the bound is on the coupled carrier; the free chain enters as a
  comparison whose error is a theorem (UD-31), and on the fibres with `f(θ₂) = 0` the two carriers
  have the same generator. *Still owed under this identifier:* (a) a carrier with no initial weight
  at the detector that then arrives — the instance of UD-34 has weight on every site at every time;
  (b) an error that does not grow as `t²`, without which there is no lower bound past a time of
  order the mean occupancy over `|g f(θ₂)|` off the exact fibres.
* **UO-3 — threshold crossed, and the two cases distinguished.** The displaced pointer lies in the
  readout region and the pointer of a run with no packet at the detector does not. The control is
  itself owed: `hasDerivAt_diracPointerShift_siteRay_of_ne` gives only an initial rate of `0` for a
  carrier that starts elsewhere, and the chain then carries it to site `j`; a bound on the
  occupancy over the write interval is needed for the control as for the signal. *Not guaranteed,
  and known to fail on a
  set of positive measure:* every periodic `C¹` profile has a zero of `f'` (`exists_rateAt_eq_zero`),
  near which the pointer does not move. On the two-site arena that set is `transition_region`. The
  statement on `Σ_*` must name its exceptional set and bound its measure, or prove the write only
  for the stated `θ₂`. *Test:* a universal exact write is false for this model.
  ▸ **Met 2026-11-09 (BACKLOG ~~#352~~) → UD-36 to UD-39, by the second alternative and with
  three conditions that stay here.** The write is proved for the stated fibres, `writeFibres`, and
  the fibres on which it is proved to fail by `T`, `slowFibres`, are named and shown to have positive
  measure for every `C¹` profile. The control is a carrier prepared on another site, bounded by the
  same occupancy estimate as the signal. *Still owed under this identifier:* (a) the measures of the
  two sets, which are shown positive and not computed, and any statement on the fibres in neither;
  (b) the control past `T`, when the chain has carried it to the detector's site; (c) a signal that
  arrives — the carrier of UD-37 starts on the detector's site, which is UO-2's condition (a) again.
* **UO-4 — a definite outcome selected.** The write as an `ActualOccurrence` whose `evolve` field is
  `diracPointerFlow`. *Test:* `ActualOccurrence` carries its evolution as a field; instantiating it
  with any map other than the M1 flow adds a record process to the dynamics, which the milestone's
  acceptance forbids.
  ▸ **Met 2026-11-10 (BACKLOG ~~#353~~) → UD-40 to UD-43, with three conditions that stay
  here.** `diracPointerRecord` is an `ActualOccurrence` on `Σ_*` whose `evolve` field is
  `diracPointerEvolve`, the flow `diracPointerFlow` over the elapsed time, by definition
  (`diracPointerRecord_evolve`). Its write field is `isRecordWrite_diracPointerFlow`, a theorem
  about the readout history of that flow. "Selected" has the one meaning a deterministic flow
  allows: the history is fixed by the ontic initial point, and on a record fibre the carrier on
  the detector's site reads `true` through the window while the carrier on another site reads
  `false` (`diracPointer_record`). *Still owed under this identifier:* (a) a carrier with weight on
  both sites: its click probability is M3's (gate A; UD-50 to UD-54 since BACKLOG ~~#355~~),
  and no statement is made about its history on one fibre; (b) a carrier that
  arrives, which is UO-2's condition (a); (c) the fibres with a negative rate and the fibres in
  none of the named sets. UD-12's occurrences are not transported: this is a new occurrence, on
  `Σ_*`.
* **UO-5 — persistence and readability.** A nonzero retention interval. *Not guaranteed, and
  expected to be finite:* on a finite ring the carrier returns (`message_one_returns` is the
  two-site case). The claim is an interval with stated ends, never permanence.
  ▸ **Met 2026-11-10 (BACKLOG ~~#353~~) → UD-41, UD-42, UD-44, with two conditions that stay
  here.** The window is `τ₂ − τ₁` for any three times at which the displacement is at most `1/6`,
  at least `1/3` and at most `2/3`. On a record fibre it is `1/(6κ)` (`siteRayRecord_window`); for
  the two-mode packet on `θ₂ = 0` it is `(58 − 24g)/(29g)` (`twoModeRecord_window`). Readability
  is the measurable readout of UD-35, a function of `θ₁` alone, constant through the window.
  Finiteness is proved in one case, and not by the route expected above: the two-mode record has
  gone by `7/g` because the pointer has turned once round (`twoMode_record_erased`), not because
  the carrier returned. *Still owed under this identifier:* (a) the end of retention for the
  record of UD-42, where nothing is proved after `2/(3κ)`; (b) sharp ends: each window is where
  a proof ends, and none is shown to be the longest.
* **UO-6 — the existing detector registers.** Whether the registers of
  `RecordLayer/DiracDetector.lean` (`detFlow`) are this flow's pointer, or a statement of how they
  differ. Until then UD-12 is a result on a separate arena.
  ▸ **Met 2026-11-09 (BACKLOG ~~#351~~) → UD-33.** They are this flow's pointer on the fibres with
  `f(θ₂) = 0`, coordinate by coordinate and up to the constant `−2 f'(θ₂)/⟨ψ, ψ⟩`; on the other
  fibres they differ by at most `2 |g| |f'(θ₂)| · |g f(θ₂)| t²`, because the register integrates
  the free packet and the pointer the carrier that feels the site potential. What is identified is
  a displacement. UD-12's `ActualOccurrence` remains a result on the detector arena until UO-4.

### M3 — outcome statistics, or the obstruction (`DiracPointerBorn.lean`, `DiracPointerClick.lean`; gate A)

The question is the programme's: can the Hamiltonian and the apparatus determine the measures of the
outcome regions without stipulating the moment-map rate field or importing the Born weights as the
detector law?

* **UO-7 — outcome regions fixed by the context.** Regions of `Σ_*` defined from the apparatus
  parameters (UA-4) and the flow alone. *Test:* see §6 — a region built from `momentContext`,
  `circleCell` sized by `momentMap`, or arcs sized by site weights has the conclusion in its
  definition.
  ▸ **Applied 2026-11-10 (BACKLOG ~~#354~~).** UD-47 quantifies over every measurable region of the
  fibre: those are the regions fixed by the apparatus alone. UD-48's region is the basin of a rate
  field, which depends on the base point, and it is recorded as conditional on UA-6. The two-time
  event of UD-49 is built from `diracReady`, `diracDisplayed`, the readout time and the flow, and
  passes this test. *Still owed under this identifier:* an outcome statement for that event.
  ▸ **Met 2026-11-10 (BACKLOG ~~#355~~) → UD-50.** The click event `diracClick` is that event, and
  its probability is the `θ₂`-average of a fixed window of the displacement
  (`diracClickProb_eq_lintegral`). The region is fixed by the arcs, the readout time and the flow.
  Nothing in it is sized by a weight.
* **UO-8 — the error bound.** `|P_{Σ_*}(i | ψ, M) − ⟨ψ, P_i ψ⟩| ≤ ε_meas`, with every contribution to
  `ε_meas` an explicit term: transition region (UO-3), finite coupling time, back-reaction on the
  carrier, finite retention. *Not guaranteed.* See the two calculations below.
  ▸ **A lower bound on `ε_meas`, 2026-11-10 (BACKLOG ~~#354~~) → UD-47.** For a region of the fibre
  read at one time under the law of UA-2, the bound holds for all carriers only with `ε_meas ≥ 1/2`
  (`exists_half_le_abs_fibreRegion_sub_siteWeight`). No upper bound is proved for any region that
  passes UO-7's test.
  ▸ **A lower bound for the click event, 2026-11-10 (BACKLOG ~~#355~~) → UD-53, UD-54.** At the
  coupling and the time of UD-54, for every matrix `E` some carrier has a click probability
  differing from the expectation of `E` by at least `P(siteRay j) / 20` (`diracPointer_clickLaw`).
  So for this event the bound holds against no operator with a smaller `ε_meas`, and there is no
  upper bound to prove.
  ▸ **The conditional bound, met 2026-11-10 (BACKLOG ~~#356~~) → UD-55 to UD-57.** For a
  torus-generated rate field (UA-6, a named hypothesis) and a profile with `|f| ≤ F`, the
  probability of the basin of a basis state at time `t` along the coupled flow is within
  `2 |t| |g| F` of the weight of that state at the free carrier
  (`abs_diracBasinProb_sub_zero_coupling_le`), and within `2 |t| (‖H_D‖ + |g| F)` of its weight in
  the carrier as prepared (`abs_diracBasinProb_sub_init_le`). The terms of `ε_meas` are the
  back-reaction on the carrier and, in the second form, the finite time. A transition region and
  a retention time are not terms: the basin event is read at one time and is not a threshold on
  the pointer. With the coupling off the error is `0` (`diracBasinProb_zero_coupling`). By §6 this
  does not pass a gate: the weights are in the rate field. *Not proved:* that either bound is
  attained; a bound for a site with the same error; an upper bound for a region that passes
  UO-7's test (for the click event there is none to prove).
* **UO-9 — or the obstruction.** If the rate field cannot be derived: the precise missing condition,
  or a no-go theorem. Not a new hypothesis inside a definition.
  ▸ **Part met 2026-11-10 (BACKLOG ~~#354~~) → UD-45 to UD-47: a no-go theorem for statistics read
  at one time.** Under `δ_{x₀} ⊗ (Haar ⊗ ν)`, with any law `ν` of `θ₂`, the law of the arena point
  at time `t` equals its law with the pointer held (`map_diracPointerFlow_dirac_prod`). So the
  probability of an apparatus-fixed region of the fibre is its measure, for every carrier. The
  missing condition, stated: the region must depend on the base point (UA-6), or the event must
  refer to the pointer at two times. *Still owed under this identifier:* the event *ready at `0` and
  displayed at `T`*, which is calculation 2 below. UD-49 shows the no-go does not hold for it, and
  nothing else about it is proved.
  ▸ **Met 2026-11-10 (BACKLOG ~~#355~~) → UD-50 to UD-54: the obstruction for the event about two
  times.** The click probability is determined by the flow (UD-50). It vanishes below a cut-off in
  the occupancy (UD-51), it stays below the probability of a ready pointer (UD-52), and it is at a
  stated distance from the expectation of every matrix (UD-53, with the instance UD-54). The missing
  condition, stated: a base-dependent cell law (UA-6), or a pointer dynamics in which the record is
  not a threshold on a drift driven by the expectation of `P_j` (UO-24 names one).
* **UO-10 — one trial, one outcome.** With several detectors. *Not available on `Σ_*` as built:* the
  arena has one pointer torus and one detector site. An arena with several pointers, or one pointer
  with a partitioned readout, is an extension of M1 that has not been made.
  ▸ **The arena is made, 2026-11-10 (BACKLOG ~~#359~~) → UD-70. Not met.** `diracTwoPointerFlow` has
  two pointers on two sites under one flow. It has no readout, so there is no outcome yet and the
  statement is not proved.
  ▸ **Two detectors in one run, 2026-11-11 (BACKLOG ~~#364~~) → UD-86 to UD-88. Met with
  conditions, up to the completion of the write, for a carrier with at least `7/8` of its
  occupancy on one detector's site. Not met for a carrier split between the sites, which is the
  content of the obligation.** Each torus of
  `diracTwoPointerFlow` has a readout and a ready arc (UA-4). On a record fibre, for detectors at
  two different sites and a carrier with at least `7/8` of its occupancy on one of them, that
  detector's readout history is an `IsRecordWrite` and the other detector's readout is not `true`
  up to the completion of that write (`diracTwoPointerFlow_record_fst_only`,
  `diracTwoPointerFlow_record_snd_only`). *What stays:* (o1) for a carrier split between the two
  sites nothing is proved, and this argument does not exclude both detectors displaying, or
  neither: that case is the content of "one outcome", and it is open; (o2) the other detector is
  covered only up to the completion of the write, and in particular not through the window in
  which the first record is held; (o3) the detectors are fast and their rates
  comparable; (o4) the outcome shown is a definite fact fixed by where the carrier is, with no
  probability in it.
  ▸ **The split carrier, 2026-11-11 (BACKLOG ~~#370~~) → UD-99, UD-100. Answered for this coupling,
  as an obstruction: both detectors display.** On the split fibres, a set of pairs `(θ₂, ψ₂)` of
  positive Haar measure for two `trainF` detectors of equal strength, a carrier with at least
  `3/8` of its occupancy on each of the two detectors' sites makes both readouts `true` at the
  flow time `1/κ₁` (`diracTwoPointerFlow_double_display`), neither having been `true` early
  (`diracTwoPointerFlow_split_not_shown_early`). The equal superposition over the two sites is
  such a carrier (`diracTwoPointerFlow_splitRay_double_display`). So (o1) is answered in the negative for
  the coupling of UA-5, in which a pointer is driven by the occupancy of its site: there are
  split carriers (at least `3/8` on each site) and fibres of positive Haar measure on which both
  readouts are `true` at one flow time. By §8 rule 4 an obstruction closes the obligation as
  firmly as the positive statement would, for this coupling. *What it does not say:* nothing
  about the weights of quantum mechanics, which are conditional on the cell law (UA-6) and are
  neither derived nor contradicted here; and nothing for a coupling other than UA-5's. *What
  stays:* (o2) and (o3) for both kinds of carrier, with nothing for slow detectors or for rates
  outside the split fibres; the carriers between the two classes; and
  what would select one outcome, which is not in this joint energy.
  ▸ **The selection by cells, 2026-11-11 (BACKLOG ~~#371~~) → UD-101, UD-102. Not a
  second answer to (o1), which stays answered for UA-5: the conditional alternative, one fibre
  angle with a partitioned readout on `Σ_*`, kinematic, with the weights assumed; and a no-go for
  locality. UO-10 is not met by it.** The site cells `siteCell c j`
  are disjoint for every arena point and every carrier (`siteCell_disjoint`). Conditional on
  UA-6 the split carrier selects each of its two sites with probability `1/2` and never both
  (`epistemicMeasure_siteCell_splitRay`). On three sites no readout that depends on the carrier
  only through the occupancy of its own site has one outcome almost surely with the weights
  (`not_siteLocal_of_one_outcome`), so the cells are not all local to their sites
  (`siteCell_not_siteLocal`). By UD-102: a family local in the stated sense either selects two
  sites with positive probability for some carrier or misses the weights. UD-100's pointers are
  coupled through their own site projectors and both display; their readouts are not shown to be
  local in that sense, and they are on the two-pointer arena, while the cells are on `Σ_*` with
  one fibre angle.
  *Conditions:* kinematic, no flow writes a cell; the weights are UA-6; "local" is the stated
  sense, and a position-valued hidden variable with the carrier's weights as its law is outside
  it; three sites. *What stays:* a dynamics that writes the cell (the discharge of UA-6).
* **UO-11 — repeated trials.** Convergence of frequencies. *Test:* a law of large numbers under the
  product of preparation measures restates UA-2, UA-3 and UA-4 (it is UD-22's
  `born_frequency_convergence_N` again). It is a result about the dynamics only if the sequence of
  trials is generated by one flow with a renewed apparatus.
  Since BACKLOG ~~#359~~ a second pointer can be on the arena (UD-70). A second pointer is a second
  apparatus and not a renewed one: both are prepared at the start.
* **UO-12 — back-reaction and calibration visible.** The carrier's generator contains the detector
  term (UD-3). The statistics statement must be about the coupled carrier, with the disturbance as a
  term of `ε_meas`.
  ▸ **Visible in one conditional statement, 2026-11-10 (BACKLOG ~~#354~~) → UD-48.** The basin
  probability at time `t` is the rate field at the coupled carrier, whose generator has the
  detector's term, averaged over `θ₂`. The disturbance is not bounded there as a term of `ε_meas`.
  ▸ **Bounded, 2026-11-10 (BACKLOG ~~#356~~) → UD-55, UD-57.** On the fibre `θ₂` the detector's
  term moves the weight of a basis state by at most `2 |t| |g f(θ₂)|`
  (`abs_diracWeight_sub_zero_coupling_le`), and the basin probability of a torus-generated field
  by at most `2 |t| |g| F`. At the coupling and the readout time of UD-54 that is at most `1/12`
  (`abs_diracBasinProb_trainF_sub_le`). The bound is stated in the apparatus input (UA-4): the
  profile's bound `F`, the coupling `g` and the time. Conditional on UA-6. An upper bound only.
* **UO-13 — no-signalling and sequential update.** On a shared arena carrying two detectors. *Test:*
  citing UD-22 is citing the sector level.
  ▸ **The arena is made, 2026-11-10 (BACKLOG ~~#359~~) → UD-69, UD-70. Not met.** On
  `diracTwoPointerFlow` the second detector does act on the first pointer: its term is in the
  carrier's generator, and the first pointer's displacement moves by at most
  `2 |f'(θ₂)| |g₁| |h(ψ₂)| |g₂| t²` (`abs_diracTwoShiftFst_sub_le`). That is a bound on one orbit.
  It is not a statement about statistics, and it does not depend on the distance between the sites.
  No-signalling here would be a statement about the law of the first record under a change of the
  second detector's setting, and there is no record on this arena yet.

**Two calculations bearing on gate A.** They were written before either was in Lean, to be proved or
corrected and not cited. Both are in Lean since BACKLOG ~~#355~~, and the note under each says what
survived.

1. *A base-independent fibre region cannot see the state.* At a fixed base trajectory the flow acts
   on the fibre by `(θ₁, θ₂) ↦ (θ₁ + s(θ₂), θ₂)` (UD-2), which preserves Haar measure on `T²`. So
   under the product preparation `δ_[ψ] ⊗ Haar` (UA-2), the probability that the fibre point lies in
   any fixed region of `T²` after any time is that region's Haar measure, whatever `ψ` is. Outcome
   statistics that depend on `ψ` therefore need one of two things: a region that depends on the base
   point (which is what `globalBasin` does, through the rate field of UA-6), or a non-uniform law for
   `θ₁` (a ready state, UA-4).
   ▸ **Proved 2026-11-10 (BACKLOG ~~#354~~, UD-45 to UD-47), sharpened in two ways and corrected in
   one.** Sharpened: (i) the conclusion holds for the whole law at one time and not only for regions
   of the fibre, since the push-forward of the preparation under the flow equals its push-forward
   with the pointer held (`map_diracPointerFlow_dirac_prod`); (ii) the law of `θ₂` is arbitrary, and
   only `θ₁` need be uniform. Corrected: the second alternative above, a ready state, is not a
   different fibre law. It is the same law conditioned on the pointer being ready at time `0`, and
   the event then refers to two times. The theorem is about one time, and for that event it fails
   (UD-49).
2. *A threshold on a ready pointer does not give a linear law.* With `θ₁` ready and `θ₂` uniform,
   the pointer's displacement is `f'(θ₂)` times a quantity that grows with the occupancy. In the
   idealised case where that quantity is `2 g w T` for a weight `w`, the click probability is the
   Haar measure of `{θ₂ : |f'(θ₂)| ≥ τ / (2 g w T)}`. Since `f'` is bounded, this is zero for all
   `w` below a cut-off, and it is linear in `w` only if `|f'|` has an unbounded tail, which no smooth
   periodic profile has. Choosing `f` to shape the law would place the target in the apparatus.
   ▸ **Proved 2026-11-10 (BACKLOG ~~#355~~, UD-50 to UD-54), corrected in two ways and sharpened in
   one.** Corrected: (i) the displacement is not `f'(θ₂)` times `2 g w T`. It is within `|κ| (‖H_D‖
   + |g f(θ₂)|) T²` of `κ T w`, with `κ = −2 g f'(θ₂)` and `w` the occupancy at time `0`, and the
   cut-off is proved with that term in it (`abs_diracPointerShift_le_weight`,
   `diracClickProb_eq_zero_of_weight_le`). (ii) The click probability is not the measure of a set of
   `θ₂` alone. It is the `θ₂`-average of a window that rises from `0` to `1/6`
   (`diracClickProb_eq_lintegral`), because the pointer is ready on an arc and not at a point.
   Sharpened: the conclusion is more than a law that is not linear in `w`. The click probability is
   not the expectation of any operator, and the distance is stated
   (`exists_mul_le_abs_diracClickProb_sub_arenaObs`). Not formalised: the remark on an unbounded
   tail. The theorem needs the cut-off only.

If these survive formalisation, the direct route from `Σ_*`'s threshold write to Born weights is
closed, and gate A's answer is an obstruction with a stated missing condition: the base-dependent
cell law (UA-6), or a different pointer dynamics.

Both have survived, with the corrections noted (BACKLOG ~~#354~~, ~~#355~~).

**Gate A, answered (BACKLOG ~~#355~~).** The programme's question: does the selected dynamics
determine measurement probabilities, or does it require the Born-shaped rate premise? On `Σ_*` both
halves have an answer. The dynamics does determine a probability for its own click event, with no
rate field (UD-50). That probability is not the quantum one: it has a cut-off in the occupancy, and
it is at a stated distance from the expectation of every operator (UD-51, UD-53, UD-54). The corpus
obtains the quantum weights only through the rate field of UA-6, and this dynamics does not supply
that field. So the reconstruction of the quantum statistics is kept as a conditional one, on UA-6
(UD-48 is its form on `Σ_*`, and UD-57 its error bound since BACKLOG ~~#356~~), and the
obstruction is the result of M3. Its scope is the coupling of
UA-5 with one pointer, the two arcs, the uniform fibre law and, in the instance, a short readout
time. It is not a statement about every dynamics on `Σ`.

### M4 — a clock inside the common Hamiltonian (`UnifiedDiracClock.lean`)

* **UO-14.** A two-level clock as part of the joint energy on an extension of `Σ_*`, its reading
  written as a record, and operational time recovered from clock records alone. *Not guaranteed:*
  UD-14's rate is a device fact on the free chain, not a record, and not under the detector coupling.
  *Test:* an observer's reconstruction that uses `t` — a readout scheduled at a flow time, a window
  stated in `t` and then read as the clock's own — has given the observer access to UA-7.
  ▸ **Part A, 2026-11-10 (BACKLOG ~~#357~~) → UD-58 to UD-60. The obligation is kept.** *Met:*
  the clock is part of the joint energy of an extension of `Σ_*` (UA-12), and the pointer's
  displacement follows the clock's hand at the dilated rate, within a back-reaction term and a
  localisation term (`abs_clockPointerShift_sub_hand_le`, `clock_pointer_device`). *Not met:* the
  reading as a record (the pointer swings and returns; no threshold, no `ActualOccurrence`, no
  count); operational time from records alone (every time in UD-58 to UD-60 is `t`, and by the
  test above a turning point read off at a flow time is access to UA-7); the detector-resolution
  error; a clock reading and a position record in one run.
  ▸ **Part B, 2026-11-10 (BACKLOG ~~#358~~) → UD-61 to UD-65. Met, with five conditions that stay
  here.** The reading is a record: each tick of the pointer is an `ActualOccurrence` whose `evolve`
  field is `clockPointerFlow` (`clockTickRecord`), and every tick record of a run reads the one
  history (`clockTickRecord_history`). Operational time is a function of clock records:
  `clockOpTime` is the number of tick records times the rest period, the count holds no value of `t`
  (`isTickCount_comp_orderIso`), and for the device of `dirac_device` it is within
  `2π/(μ₁ − μ₀) + ((μ₁ − μ₀)/E₀) T` of `√(1 − (v/c)²) T` (`clock_tick_device`). The
  detector-resolution error is the first term, one tick. By the test above: a tick is a threshold
  passage of the readout history and not a reading scheduled at a flow time, no window is read as
  the clock's own, and in the inequality `T` is the quantity the count is compared with. The
  horizon at which the count is taken is still a flow time, which is condition (c).
  *Still owed under this identifier:* (a) the swing beyond one mode on a fibre with
  `f(θ₂) = 0`: an instance of the four inequalities of UD-62 for a packet of positive width or a
  fibre with `f(θ₂) ≠ 0`, up to a stated horizon; (b) a holder of the count: it is a function of the
  record history, no real function of the state holds it (UD-65), and a register for the count
  modulo a fixed number is not built; (c) the event at which the count is taken is given by a flow
  time, and M6 needs it given by a record (an emission, a reception); (d) a tick record and a
  position record in one run, which needs both pointers on one arena. Until then M4's acceptance,
  clock readings and measurement records from the same dynamics, is met for the clock alone; (e) a
  readout at a site.
  ▸ **Both pointers on one arena, 2026-11-10 (BACKLOG ~~#359~~) → UD-71. Condition (d) is not met.**
  `clockSiteFlow` is one flow of one joint energy carrying the clock's pointer and a site pointer
  (`clockSiteFlow_apply`). On a fibre where the detector's profile vanishes it is `clockPointerFlow`
  exactly (`clockSiteFlow_fst_of_lift_eq_zero`), and on every fibre the clock's hand is within
  `2 |f'(θ₂)| |g₁| |h(ψ₂)| |g₂| t²` of the hand of UD-58 (`abs_clockSiteShiftFst_sub_le`). What (d)
  still asks: a readout on each torus, the tick records of UD-64 and a position record as
  occurrences of `clockSiteFlow` in one run, with the swing's four inequalities and the write's
  conditions re-derived with the cross-talk as one more error term. For (e): the second pointer's
  coupling is at a site (`deviceSiteProj`), and the clock's is not.
  ▸ **Records in one run, 2026-11-10 (BACKLOG ~~#360~~) → UD-72 to UD-76. Condition (d) is met, with
  three conditions that stay here.** A readout and a ready arc are on each torus of the arena of
  UD-71 (UA-4). Tick `n` of the clock's pointer and a write of the site pointer are two
  `ActualOccurrence`s with the one propagator `clockSiteEvolve` (`clockSiteTickRecord`,
  `clockSitePositionRecord`, `clockSiteTickRecord_evolve`, `clockSitePositionRecord_evolve`). The
  tick record's preparation is the position record's start state carried by that propagator
  (`clockSiteTickRecord_prep`), and both histories are readouts along the one orbit
  (`clockSiteTickRecord_history`, `clockSitePositionRecord_history`). With the detector coupled the
  clock's pointer is a swing under the four inequalities of UD-62 with the cross-talk added to the
  error (`isSwing_clockSiteFlow`). The site pointer's displacement is monotone on a fibre of
  nonnegative rate, with no cross-talk term (`clockSiteShiftSnd_mono`), and its write needs three
  displacement values (`isRecordWrite_clockSiteFlow_site`). One instance has both records with no
  hypothesis left but the parameters (`clockSite_records_train`). *The conditions:* (d1) the
  instance is on the fibres where both profiles vanish, a null set of `echoMuL`, and for one
  momentum mode; there neither pointer acts on the device, which moves freely and drives both. Off
  those fibres the swing's four inequalities and the write's three values are hypotheses, the
  swing's error grows as `T²`, and no instance on a set of fibres of positive measure is given; (d2)
  in the instance the occupancy of every site is `1/(M+1)`, so the position record does not single
  out its site, and a carrier localised near a site with a record that distinguishes sites is not
  given; (d3) the tick records are held for less than a turn, the position record for a stated
  window, and every time in them is a flow time (UA-7). M4's acceptance, clock readings and
  measurement records from the same dynamics, is met in that instance and conditionally elsewhere.
  Conditions (a), (b), (c) and (e) are unchanged. The count of ticks at the position write is not
  taken: that write as an event is condition (c).
  ▸ **A record that singles out its site, 2026-11-11 (BACKLOG ~~#363~~) → UD-84, UD-85. Condition
  (d2) is met, with three conditions that stay. The write half of (d1) is met for carriers with
  at least `7/8` on the detector's site.** On the record
  fibres, a set of pairs `(θ₂, ψ₂)` of positive measure for the detector profile `trainF` and a
  strong enough detector (`volume_siteRecordFibres_trainF_pos`), a carrier with at least `7/8` of
  its occupancy on the detector's site writes the position record
  (`isRecordWrite_clockSiteFlow_site_of_occupancy`) and one with at most `1/8` is not displayed
  through the window (`clockSiteFlow_site_not_shown_of_occupancy`). For a carrier with at least
  `7/8` on a site `x` the readout at `1/(2κ)` is `true` exactly when the detector is at `x`
  (`clockSiteFlow_site_shown_iff`). *The conditions:* (d2a) the two placements of the detector are
  two flows and not one run; one trial with two detectors is UO-10; (d2b) the detector is fast,
  the write is complete in a time short against `1/‖H‖`, and no moving packet is followed; (d2c)
  the carrier is a class of rays by occupancy, with nothing between `1/8` and `7/8`. *What stays
  of (d1):* for other carriers, the mode of UD-76 among them, the write's three values stay
  hypotheses; the clock's swing on these fibres is still conditional on the four inequalities of
  UD-62, so the tick record and the position record are outright together only in the instance
  of UD-76. (d3) is unchanged.
  ▸ **The count at a position write, 2026-11-11 (BACKLOG ~~#366~~) → UD-92. Condition (c) is met in
  part in the instance of UD-76: the flow time at which the count is taken is proved to be the
  instant the site readout's history turns; the count is still taken at that flow time (c1), in
  one mode on a null set (c2).** The site readout switches at an instant
  its own history fixes (`echoSiteShown_clockSiteFlow_train_iff`), the tick history has a count
  there, and the count times the tick period is within one period of it
  (`clockSite_count_at_display_train`); likewise at the start and the completion of the write
  (`clockSite_count_at_write_train`). *The conditions:* (c1) the two histories are aligned by the
  flow parameter: the count is of the tick history up to the flow time of the event, and by the
  test above that alignment is access to UA-7; an event that stamps a register would remove it,
  and that is condition (b); (c2) one momentum mode on a null set of fibres. Conditions (a), (b)
  and (e) are unchanged.
  ▸ **A pointer for (b), 2026-11-12 (BACKLOG ~~#374~~) → UD-106, UD-107.** On the arena of UA-16
  nothing but a pointer's own angle depends on where that pointer started. This bears on (b)
  and does not close it: a register driven by the same occupancy as the pointer is not
  excluded.

### M5 — causal order from interventions (`UnifiedCausalOrder.lean`)

* **UO-15.** Influence defined as the response of a record to a local intervention on `Σ_*`, with a
  propagation bound for the **coupled** generator `H_D + g f(θ₂) P_j`. *Not guaranteed by UD-15,*
  which is for the free generator. Expected, not checked: `P_j` is on-site, so the support-growth
  argument of `norm_commutator_chain_le` goes through with the coupled generator's norm. *To keep
  distinct:* a factorial tail is suppressed influence; exact exclusion is a different statement, and
  the lattice with continuous time gives the first. *Test:* `CausalPrecedes` is induced by an assumed
  graph (UD-16); a causal order read off that graph is the interaction topology of UA-5 restated.
  Since BACKLOG ~~#359~~ the generators with two pointers are fixed: `diracTwoH` (two
  site detectors) and `clockSiteH` (the clock's pointer and a site detector). An influence between
  two recorded events needs two pointers, so the bound is owed for these and not for the one-pointer
  generator named above. Expected, not checked: both added terms of `diracTwoH` are on-site, and in
  `clockSiteH` the level swap exchanges the two levels at each site and moves nothing between sites,
  so the support-growth argument should go through. The cross-talk bounds of UD-69 are not that
  bound: they do not depend on the distance between the sites.
  ▸ **The bound, 2026-11-11 (BACKLOG ~~#361~~) → UD-77 to UD-81. The propagation bound is proved for
  both coupled generators. The obligation stays, with five conditions.** Both generators hop at
  most one site at every pair of pointer angles (`hopsOne_diracTwoGen`, `hopsOne_clockSiteGen`),
  and the support-growth argument goes through for any such generator
  (`norm_commutator_hopsOne_le`). The response of a pointer to an intervention is bounded on the
  arena of two site pointers for both pointers and both interventions
  (`abs_diracTwoKickResponseSnd_le`, `abs_diracTwoAngleResponseSnd_le`, and the two for the first
  pointer), and on the clock-and-site arena for the site pointer under a kick
  (`abs_clockSiteKickResponseSnd_le`). There the position record of UD-74 survives a remote kick
  within margins (`isRecordWrite_clockSiteFlow_site_of_kick`). *The conditions:* (e1) the bounds are
  factorial tails, suppression and not exclusion, exact only at time `0`
  (`arenaObs_kick_of_idOffOn`); (e2) they are upper bounds: no response is shown to be attained or
  bounded below, so influence is limited and never exhibited; (e3) no relation between two record
  events is built, and `CausalPrecedes` stays induced by an assumed graph, so the title's causal
  order is not reached. By the test above, the predicates `HopsOne` and `SiteSuppOn` are the
  interaction topology restated, and what is added to it is the bound. No influence is defined in
  Lean: the response is the difference written in each statement; (e4) on the arena of two
  site pointers the response is a displacement's and not a record's, since no record is read there
  (UO-10); (e5) the clock's pointer has no bound in the distance, because the level swap is
  supported on no proper set of sites (`mem_of_siteSuppOn_levelSwap`): a tick record may respond to
  a kick anywhere on the ring with no delay from this argument. The interventions are assumed
  (UA-14) and are no part of the dynamics. Every time is a flow time (UA-7).
  ▸ **A response that arrives, 2026-11-11 (BACKLOG ~~#362~~) → UD-82, UD-83. Condition (e2) is met
  at distance one, for one carrier and one kick. It stays for larger distances.** The response to
  a kick is its first Taylor term that the kick does not commute with, up to the next tail
  (`abs_arenaObs_flow_kick_sub_sub_le`, `abs_displacement_kick_sub_sub_le`). On the arena of two
  site pointers, from the carrier `arrivalRay k` and for the sign kick at the site `k − 1`, the
  second pointer's response is `2 g₂ h'(ψ₂) J t²` up to `8 |g₂| |h'(ψ₂)| ‖H‖² t³`
  (`abs_diracTwoKickResponseSnd_sub_le`), and it is not zero for `0 < t` with `4‖H‖²t < |J|`
  when `g₂ h'(ψ₂) ≠ 0` (`diracTwoKickResponseSnd_ne_zero`). *What stays of (e2):* the carrier of
  the instance has half its weight on the detector's own site; the bound of UD-78 at distance one
  is not shown attained; the leading
  coefficient for a kick two or more sites away is not computed, so no response is shown to
  arrive from farther than the neighbouring site; the first pointer's response and the response
  to an angle have no leading term here; nothing is exhibited on the clock-and-site arena.
  Conditions (e1), (e3), (e4) and (e5) are unchanged: the response found is a displacement's.

### M6 — radar geometry from records (`UnifiedRadarGeometry.lean`; gate B)

* **UO-16.** Radar coordinates computed only from emission, reception and clock records written by
  the flow; two observers assign the same Minkowski interval within stated errors; the residual tends
  to zero along a scaling family that is instantiated on the arenas. *Not guaranteed:* UD-17's
  scaling law is kinematic (UA-11), and the family is not instantiated. *Test:* a theorem whose
  hypotheses include a messenger at speed exactly `1`, or a clock at exactly `√(1 − v²)`, is UD-17
  again. And by §1, a pass at gate B is an operational reconstruction from UA-8.
  ▸ **A reception record, 2026-11-11 (BACKLOG ~~#365~~) → UD-89 to UD-91. One ingredient. Not
  met.** A pointer of the joint flow, coupled to the far half of the ring, writes at some flow time
  in `[625π/3, 4375π/3]`, an interval about the predicted arrival `625π`, and its readout is not
  `true` before `625π/3` (`regionPointerFlow_arrival_write`). That holds on a set of conserved
  angles of positive Haar measure, for `trainF` at one strength; the carrier is one ray, so no
  measure of the set of start points is stated. The threshold reads accumulated weight and does
  not by itself certify a crossing. *What is missing for a radar coordinate:* an emission record in the same run;
  a clock in the same run, and its count at each record (UO-14 (c)); a second observer; the time
  of the write located, which here is only somewhere in `[625π/3, 4375π/3]`; a detector on less
  than half the ring; and a family along which the errors shrink.
  ▸ **Departure and arrival in one run, 2026-11-11 (BACKLOG ~~#367~~) → UD-93, UD-94. Two
  ingredients. Not met.** Two pointers of one joint flow, on the near and the far half of the
  ring, write a departure record and an arrival record along one orbit, the first complete by
  `625π/3` and the second not showing before it (`regionTwoFlow_departure_arrival`). *What is
  still missing:* a clock in the same run, which is a third pointer (the arena for it exists since
  BACKLOG ~~#368~~, UD-96; nothing is read on it yet); the count of that clock at each record (UD-92 has it for a position write on
  another arena); the two times located; an emission in place of a prepared departure; a second
  observer; detectors on less than half the ring; and a family along which the errors shrink.
  ▸ **A clock and two detectors in one run, 2026-11-11 (BACKLOG ~~#369~~) → UD-97, UD-98. Three
  readouts and two counts in one run. No signal travels. Not met.** On the three-pointer arena of UD-96,
  one orbit has the clock's ticks, a record on each of two site detectors, and the clock's count

  set by the detectors' strengths and is not a transit time. *What is still missing:* a
  travelling carrier in this arena (the crossing bounds of UD-12's module for the two-level

  not the carrier's own level; a register that holds the count at the record (UO-14 (b)); an
  emission in place of a prepared start; fibres of positive measure, with a cross-talk bound for
  three pointers; records that single out their sites; and record objects (`IsRecordWrite`,

  these arenas is the carrier's own internal level (UA-12). If the carrier is the messenger, its
  count between two detections is the messenger's own tick count and not an observer's clock
  reading at emission and reception. Whether M6's clock may be the carrier's own level is to be
  decided before a travelling carrier is built here; if it may not, the missing object is a
  second carrier and not another pointer torus. By the test
  above, nothing so far is evidence about the speed of a messenger or the rate of a moving clock.
  ▸ **The decision, and the first brick, 2026-11-11 (BACKLOG ~~#372~~) → UA-16, UD-103, UD-104.
  Not met.** The author decided: the clock is a second carrier, and it is the tick clock of
  UD-58 to UD-65. On the product arena of UA-16 one flow carries the messenger with its departure

  ▸ **The composite carrier, first brick, 2026-11-12 (BACKLOG ~~#376~~) → UA-17, UD-110 to

  ▸ **The author's decisions, 2026-11-14.** Five decisions, each taken with the charter as
  the guide. They fix what M6 must contain; none is built yet.
  (1) *The gated pointer returns, on a fifth torus.* A record is an ontic fact held by a
  degree of freedom, and in the variant UA-17 (f) no degree of freedom holds a count. The
  run is to be rebuilt with the three region pointers, the clock's own pointer and the gated
  pointer of UA-17 together. This needs a further clause of UA-17, not yet written.
  (2) *The emission record is a departure record.* `Σ` is closed and has no outside source. The
  emission is a record at the observer's home region that the messenger has left it, with the
  number of tick records the gated pointer has written by then. No trap and no clock-triggered
  release.
  (3) *The reflector is a wall on an open chain.* The ring is replaced by a line with a hard
  end, a term of the energy; the return is then the wall's and its distance is a parameter.
  The packet bounds of UD-107 to UD-116 are to be redone for a chain with an end.
  (4) *The second observer: at rest first, moving to pass.* An observer is a home detector
  with its own clock carrier. Agreement of two observers at rest (up to a shift) is a step;
  M6 is met only with an observer in relative motion, since only that tests Lorentz
  kinematics.
  (5) *Positive measure in the pointers' angles.* The M6 records must hold on an open set of
  the pointers' conserved angles (UA-4 item 3, the typicality reading UA-3). The
  messenger's packet and the clock's start remain prepared states, as apparatus input.
  Statements on pinned fibres are steps and do not count towards M6.
  ▸ **The target and the plan, 2026-11-14 (agreed with the author). Nothing here is built.**
  *The target.* `radar_lorentz_error` (`RecordLayer/DiracTwoCharts.lean`, ~~#280~~) already
  turns seven hypotheses into the Lorentz residual: two kinematic relations (the messenger
  covers the separation at speed `1`, out and back) and five reading bounds (the moving observer
  `B`'s readings of its emission, its return and the meeting, within `d_E`, `d_Ret`, `d_MB` of
  `√(1 − v²)` times their flow times; the rest observer `A`'s readings of the relay event and
  the meeting, within `d_R`, `d_MA` of theirs). There they are hypotheses, which is the
  kinematic law UD-17. M6 is met when, on one arena and for one flow, each of the seven is a
  theorem about records of that flow with an explicit error, each reading is the number of tick
  records a gated pointer wrote in its first open period (UA-18 (e); no coordinate holds that
  number), and all the errors tend to zero along a family instantiated on the arena. By the test
  above, no hypothesis may state the messenger's speed or the clock's rate; both must come from
  the energy within stated errors (the messenger from the band velocity of a packet, the moving
  clock from the dependence of its level splitting on its momentum). *The plan, in order.* (1)
  This statement. (2) A probe of the moving observer: say what a moving observer is in `Σ` and
  prove the one lemma showing its clock records exist with a rate within a stated error of
  `√(1 − v²)` times the rest rate; if that fails, M6 fails on these arenas and is reported as such.
  (3) Freeze the arena: the chain with walls, the messenger, one clock carrier per observer, the
  pointers; one assumption clause. (4) One observer's radar: departure record, return record
  from the wall, the number of gated tick records at each, radar time and distance formed from
  the counts. (5) Two observers at rest: agreement up to a shift, within stated errors. (6) The
  moving observer: the seven items above, and the residual along the family; M6 is met here and
  not before. (7) The same on an open set of the pointers' angles. *Rules.* No row outside this
  list; what is found on the way goes to a Remainder line. One review per step. If a step's
  lemma fails, work stops and the failure is reported. *Parked for step (4):* UD-135, UD-136
  (~~#387~~) and the mirror lemma for the wall.
  ▸ **Step (2), the probe of the moving observer, 2026-11-14 (BACKLOG ~~#388~~) → UD-137,
  UD-138. Not met; the probe found no obstruction and is not decided.** The author decided: the
  moving observer is a localised packet of the clock carrier with a coincidence detector (a
  pointer turned when the messenger and the clock carrier are within a fixed width of each
  other). Its clock: on a fibre where the pointer's profile vanishes, a clock packet's pointer
  is a swing up to a horizon with an error bound linear in the horizon
  (`isSwing_clockPointerFlow_packet`), and for the localised packet of UD-64's device the count

  observer (no messenger, no detector, no meeting); an exhibited packet of positive width;
  a recorded speed. *What the probe leaves open:* the coincidence detector, which is a new
  term in the energy (step (3)); the packet's position as a record; a horizon shown long
  enough for an exchange.
  ▸ **Step (2), the clock's records shown to exist, 2026-11-14 (BACKLOG ~~#391~~, ~~#392~~)

  family with `n ≥ 400` and `Δ ≤ 1/400` the count is short of the undilated count by more

  in Lean, the clock's predicted position crosses many resolved arcs within the horizon when

  small as well. No obstruction is visible; none of that is a theorem.

  ▸ **Two points settled by the charter, 2026-11-15 (the author's instruction: follow the
  charter's guidance).** (i) *The first pointer ticking on after the return.* UA-18 (e)
  already decides it: a reading is the number of tick records of the pointer's first open
  period, a fact about the run, fixed from the event that closes the gate until the gate
  reopens; ticks written after it belong to no reading, and no coordinate stores the number.
  The charter's record is an actual occurrence of the flow, not a value some pointer shows
  later. So UD-161 and UD-162 are in the required form (each states its window), and M6 is
  not asked for a pointer that shows two readings together. (ii) *The phase condition
  `1/6 < |cos R t_a|` and the quickly closing gate.* By UA-18 (b) and UA-4 the three carriers
  are prepared states and are not varied over. The clock's phase when the gate closes is
  fixed by the preparation, so the condition is one more inequality on the prepared
  parameters, of the same kind as the budgets. It is admitted on that footing, with two
  consequences that bind M6: the statement must exhibit values that meet it, and its scope
  must say that for preparations outside it (and UD-156 shows such preparations exist) this
  arrangement of pointers gives no reading. No preparation is excluded silently, and no new
  kind of term is added to the arena to avoid the condition (UA-18 (c)).

  work under UO-16 is the list R1 to R7 in the scope of UD-164, and a brick is built only if
  it discharges an item of that list on the arena of UA-18.** R3 and R4 are hypotheses that

  are kept as proved; those of them that are general in the carriers (UD-148, UD-157, UD-159,
  UD-161) are the intended tools for R1, the rest are not on the path.

  the chain with two walls, with the emission at `B`'s start.

  ▸ **R4, and of R2 the clock at rest and the wall, 2026-11-15 (BACKLOG ~~#406~~, ~~#407~~)

  ▸ **The tolerance test for one gate, 2026-11-16 (BACKLOG ~~#415~~) → UD-184, UD-185.

  ▸ **Feasibility and error budget, 2026-11-16 → `specs/unified-m6-feasibility.md`. Reading,
  no Lean.** The author's research delivery plan of 2026-11-16 makes a feasibility check of
  the whole construction its first task. Result: no obstruction found; a family in `k`
  with total relative error about `16/k`, from the ticks lost in reading five counts, on a
  comparison scale that does not shrink. Three findings change what has to be built: the
  swing theorems pin `B` and the messenger beside one wall at the start, so `A` sits at the
  other and the distance is the whole chain; `A` at that wall is the translate by half the
  ring of the clock of UD-175 and is not built; and UD-183's arithmetic is for half that
  distance. The unproved steps that could still fail are R7 and the walled chain's reading
  theorem with its own constants.
  ▸ **The wall as a property of the energy, 2026-11-16 (BACKLOG ~~#416~~; Task 3 of the
  delivery plan) → UD-186. Not met.** `odd_sector_chain_flow_equiv`: the mirror-odd sector
  of the ring, with its evolution, is the chain on `{0, …, M}` with hard ends and the

### M7 — `3 + 1`

* **UO-17.** BACKLOG #294–#298, on the unified arena, through `2 + 1` where it helps

  group velocity and the anisotropy), #296 (packets), #297 (records and the cone on this
  lattice), #298 (the `d + 1` statement). Nothing of UD-206 is on the unified arena yet.

  rotation residue of order `a²`. Open: #296, #297, #298.

  arena as frozen in UA-18. **Open decision for the author** (the note's §7): mark M7 on the

### M8 — which assumptions force the geometry (gate C)

* **UO-18.** At least three comparisons: alternative couplings (UA-5); alternative pointer rate laws,
  including `sqContext` (UD-24 already separates it from the moment map); alternative locality or
  dispersion (UA-8), including models that fail Lorentz. *Test:* a lattice correction
  (`lattice_frame_residue`, the anisotropy of #295) is a prediction only if a nonzero physical scale
  is independently justified. Otherwise it is an artefact of the regulator.

### M9 — capstone and review (`UnifiedRecordSpacetime.lean`)

* **UO-19.** One theorem on one arena, and an independent review for hidden target formulas,
  redundant hypotheses, circular structures and unused dynamics. The review checks each conjunct
  against §6.

  Hilbert space only. Open: (2) to (5).
  ▸ **Fifth brick, 2026-11-19 (BACKLOG #437, part) → UD-226.** (1) for the contact term of
  UA-19 (b) itself: the bound is `‖Q‖ |g| ∫₀ᵗ ‖P e^{−isH₀} X‖ ds` with `P` the coincidence
  projector. Open: the integrand as the coincidence weight of two packets; (2) to (5).

---

## 6. When a theorem only repackages a posit

**The general test.** For a proposed theorem `T` about `Σ_*`, list the hypotheses and unfold the
definitions in its statement. `T` repackages a Register 1 entry if

1. a hypothesis is that entry, or is equivalent to it by a theorem already in the corpus; or
2. a definition in the statement contains the conclusion (a region sized by the weight it is then
   shown to have; a clock defined to read the time it is then shown to read); or
3. the object `T` is about is not produced by `diracPointerFlow` or an extension of it, so the
   dynamics is unused; or
4. `T` holds for every flow preserving the measure, so the Hamiltonian is unused.

A theorem that fails the test may still be worth having. It is then recorded in Register 2 as
*conditional on* the entry, with the entry named in its docstring, and it does not pass a gate.

**`IsTorusGenerated`.** The definition (`RecordLayer/CellLawForced.lean`):

```lean
def IsTorusGenerated (c : ContextField N) : Prop := IsGeneratedRateField c.rate
```

Its standing, stated once so that no milestone has to re-derive it:

* It is a **premise**. It is Posit 1 in its live form. No theorem in the corpus derives it from a
  dynamics.
* It is **extensionally equivalent to its conclusion**: `torusGenerated_eq_momentMap` gives
  `c.rate p i = momentMap p i` from it, and `isTorusGenerated_momentContext` gives it for the moment
  map. `POSITS.md` calls the pair a characterisation, not a discharge, and that is the status here.
* It holds of `momentContext` because `momentContext` is **defined** as the moment map. That is a
  fact about a definition (the third rule of §1).
* The one theorem with `IsTorusGenerated` as its conclusion, `isTorusGenerated_of_calibrated`, has as
  its hypothesis calibration against every einselected coupling, and `isCalibrated_forall_iff` shows
  that hypothesis is **equivalent to** `rate = momentMap`. It is a premise conversion (BACKLOG
  ~~#152~~): it moves the posit from the rates to the readout's calibration. It does not remove it.
  Whether calibration becomes Posit 1's live wording is the author's decision and is not taken here.
* What the M1 dynamics does supply is one half of that picture and only that half: the pointer's
  drift is the coupling's occupancy (UD-4, UD-7; the general statement is `drift_eq_sum_momentMap`).
  A drift is a mean motion of one pointer. It is not a rate field, a cell, or a probability.

**Consequences for the milestones.** Until UO-20 is met, each of the following is a restatement of
Posit 1 and may not be reported as dynamical:

* an M3 theorem with `IsTorusGenerated c` or `∀ S, IsCalibrated c.rate S` among its hypotheses;
* an M3 theorem whose outcome regions are `globalBasin (momentContext _) i`, `circleCell` sized by
  `momentMap`, or the click arcs of UD-11 sized by site weights (`globalBasin_prob` returns whatever
  rate the field supplies; `globalBasin_born` is that with the moment map put in);
* a statement that the dynamics "generates the torus action" on the strength of
  `fundamentalForm_torusField` alone — that theorem is about the back-reaction of an einselected
  coupling, at the linear level, and on `Σ_*` the carrier also runs `H_D`, which does not commute
  with `P_j`.

**The other standing cases.**

| A theorem saying… | …repackages | unless |
|---|---|---|
| the carrier evolves unitarily on `Σ_*` | UA-8 (the energy is `−2 ⟨H⟩`) | never: it is the choice of energy |
| the measure is the right one because the flow preserves it | UA-2 | never: UD-23 |
| frequencies converge to the region's measure | UA-2, UA-3, UA-4 | the trials are generated by one flow |
| the pointer starts ready | UA-4 | a reset dynamics is on the arena |
| observers recover Lorentz kinematics | UA-8, UA-11 | messenger speed and clock rate are derived from records of the flow, with their errors (and then it is still §1's first distinction) |
| a record is written | UO-4's test | its `evolve` is `diracPointerFlow` |
| a clock reads the elapsed time | UA-7 (a time stated in `t` and read as the clock's own) | the time is a function of tick records of the flow, and the rate they follow is derived with its errors (UD-61 to UD-63) |
| the causal order is the coupling graph | UA-5 | influence is demonstrated by a response |
| the result holds on "the Dirac chain" | §1's second distinction | it is on `Σ_*`, or an identification theorem is cited |

---

## 7. What the audit found in the four sources

* **[`POSITS.md`](POSITS.md).** No posit's standing needs changing. The posit count is ten and every
  entry has its discharge line. Two things a reader of the programme should know that the register
  does not say in one place: Posit 3 is a theorem on `Σ_*` (UD-5), and Posit 1's entry does not
  mention the calibration conversion of ~~#152~~ (by the author's reservation on that row; §6 states
  its standing without changing the posit's wording). **One stale passage, corrected in this
  landing:** Posit 10 still said I3 had no rate field from the base "until BACKLOG #136 lands";
  that row closed on 2026-10-13 (`jointMomentContext`, `jointMomentContext_rowSum`). A pointer to
  this note is added to the register's cross-listing section.
  Since BACKLOG ~~#355~~ Posit 1's discharge line records the answer to gate A. Its standing and the
  count are unchanged.
* **[`records-to-spacetime-status.md`](records-to-spacetime-status.md).** Its "what is assumed" list
  is carried into UA-7, UA-8, UA-10 and UA-11 unchanged. Its sentence "every record layer here is
  kinematic: no `H_int` generates the cells" is not changed by M1A, which is a Hamiltonian system
  with no record and no cell on it (§2). The note predates M1A and does not mention it; a line
  pointing here is added.
* **[`beyond-1p1-scoping.md`](beyond-1p1-scoping.md).** Its rows #292–#298 are M7's content (UO-17),
  and its "not reached" list is carried over. Its expectation that the clock results transfer to
  `d` dimensions was checked on 2026-11-17 (UD-206). What of its rows the unified arena can
  carry is in [`unified-m7-feasibility.md`](unified-m7-feasibility.md).
* **[`residues.tsv`](residues.tsv).** The open rows R-012, R-016, R-019 and the boundary rows R-015
  and R-017 are each cited above where they bind. R-016 is about the shear and joint-lift
  propagators and lists the time-independent writes as proved; M1A is of that second kind and did
  not touch the row. No row is added: the `UO-n` entries are this programme's ledger of missing
  implications, and one becomes a registry residue when a Lean module carries it.

---

## 8. Keeping the contract

1. A milestone's landing updates this note in the same commit: a `UO-n` it meets moves to Register 2
   with its theorem names and its arena; a `UO-n` it meets only conditionally stays, with the
   condition named.
2. A new assumption gets a `UA-n` **before** the theorem that uses it lands, and a posit-level one
   goes to `POSITS.md` with its discharge line.
3. Every new theorem's docstring names the `UA-n` entries it depends on when they are not visible as
   hypotheses.
4. An obstruction is a result. A proved no-go, or a named missing condition, closes its `UO-n` as
   firmly as the positive statement would.
5. Identifiers are stable: add, never renumber.

Plans for the unfinished tasks of the plan of 2026-11-18:
[`unified-records-spacetime-task-plans.md`](unified-records-spacetime-task-plans.md).
